# Phase 2 progress: India auto-capture

Slices continue the numbering of Phase 1. Same rules: every claim says what was exercised, every number its conditions, and what was not done is stated.

## Slice 34: the capture pipeline (FR-CAP-05, first half; FR-CAP-12 storage)
**Built:** the part that turns a text into a pending item, with no screen yet. **Sender rules** (`SenderRules`): a header like `AX-HDFCBK-S` is reduced to its root `HDFCBK` (operator prefix and category suffix dropped, case ignored); a phone number has no header and is never captured. **Classifier** (`CaptureClassifier`): the sender is judged **before the body is read**, so a text from a person or an unknown header is never parsed; then OTP, notices (due dates, KYC) and declined payments are set aside with their own reason; only a transaction from an allowlisted header reaches the inbox, always with confidence `GENERIC` (the Phase 0 finding that generic parsing misreads about 1 in 65 directions stands: nothing may skip review). **Inbox store**: pending, confirmed and dismissed items; the text of a message is kept only while the item is pending and is erased when it is confirmed or dismissed; the same text delivered twice is one item (fingerprint of source, sender, the time the phone stamped it and the normalised words, so two identical-looking texts at different times are two transactions). **Capture events**: for every text looked at, one row of time, header root and outcome, **never the words or numbers**, pruned to 90 days; this is what capture health will read. The person can add a header the list misses (one per line or comma separated).
**Verified:** 8 classifier tests on both targets, 6 inbox-store tests, 5 device tests of the whole pipeline on the encrypted database (fields parsed, duplicates, every ignore reason leaves only a count, added headers, fingerprint). 11 mutations of the classifier and pipeline caught; 2 survived and are equivalent (a phone number is already rejected by the header pattern's need for a letter; spacing around header names is trimmed anyway), and one real gap (newline-separated headers) got its own test.
**Said plainly:** (1) **the allowlist of 36 header roots is provisional**, written from public knowledge and unchecked against live traffic; the corpus from consenting testers is what will confirm it, and a person can add a missing one. (2) the parser is still the generic one measured in Phase 0 (62% fully right on 144 sanitised public samples, tuned on those same samples): per-bank rules are the large remaining work for the 98% target and need real messages. (3) **No receiver, permission or screen exists yet**, so nothing is captured on a phone; the pipeline is exercised only by tests. (4) **Merchant** is not extracted yet, so the inbox will show the text, not a payee. (5) The Play SMS permission declaration has not been written or submitted: it should be, early (the roadmap says so), and it needs the company's Play account.

**Verification:** 656 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 218 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 35: SMS capture on the phone and the review inbox (FR-CAP-05, FR-CAP-12, FR-SEC-06 partly)
**Built:** More, then **Capture from texts**: a switch with plain words on what it does and does not do, shown *before* Android asks for the permission (the switch asks for `RECEIVE_SMS` only when turned on; denied means it stays off). A receiver reads incoming texts only while the switch is on and the permission is granted, joins the parts of a long text, and hands each to the pipeline from slice 34. The home screen shows **"N to review"**; **To review** lists each captured transaction (payee guess or bank, amount, money in or out, the bank's date, the bank and the last four digits, the text itself, and a reminder that it was read automatically) with **Add**, **Change** (pick the account and, for a debit, a category) and **Skip**. Add creates the expense or income dated by the **bank's date**, noted with the payee guess (`PayeeGuess`: a UPI address, or the name after "at/to"), marked as coming from an SMS with the bank's reference; it remembers which account a given bank and last four digits went to, so the next one starts there. A reference already in the ledger is refused with the reason, not added twice; an amount in a different currency than the ledger's is refused. Skipping or adding erases the stored text. An SMS that arrives while the screen is open updates the list.
**Verified on the emulator through its real telephony:** with the switch on and the app killed (not force-stopped), three texts were sent with `adb emu sms send`: a HDFC debit, a text from a personal number, and an OTP from the HDFC header. Exactly one item appeared ("1 to review"), the other two left nothing; the card on screen was read and looks right. Also: 5 device tests (banner, add, income, remembered account, skip, repeated reference, currency guard, permission display and the switch), 13 mutations of the confirm/skip logic caught after 2 first survived (the bank date, the currency guard: each got a test), and the parser pieces on both targets.
**Said plainly:**
- **Not tested:** a message arriving while the phone is asleep, in Doze, on a real phone, or from a real carrier; multi-part texts and dual-SIM were not exercised (the receiver joins parts by sender, written to the API's contract but never run with a real multi-part message). Android also does not deliver texts to an app the person has *force-stopped* until it is opened again (seen here: three texts sent after a force-stop captured nothing); that is Android's rule, and it should be said in the privacy text.
- **SMS to inbox time (the ≤ 2 s criterion) is not measured.** The reading itself takes milliseconds on the emulator; the delivery time on a real phone is unknown.
- **A debit added with no category when none has been used yet**; there is no automatic categorisation until the rules slices.
- The generic parser still misreads about 1 in 65 directions on public samples (Phase 0), and its reference and account formats are incomplete: this is why nothing is added without a tap.
- **No Play SMS declaration yet, and `RECEIVE_SMS` is a restricted permission:** the app cannot be published with it until the declaration is approved (roadmap: submit early). Nothing on a store build can capture until then.
- Notification capture, per-source auto-confirm and capture-health screens are not built.
- **A real old bug fixed on the way:** loading more rows (scrolling the list) added to the in-memory page list outside the write lock, so it could overlap a refresh and throw `ConcurrentModificationException` on a background thread, which ends the app's process. A full run's timing gate crashed on it (the new capture refresh at start made the overlap likelier). `loadMore` now runs under the lock like every other change; a new stress test (400 page loads against 150 saves) passes with the fix and crashes the app process on the old code.
- **The cause of the unexplained "attempt to re-open an already-closed object" failures (slices 29 onward) is now found and reproduced.** The container closes the database on purpose (a fresh ledger, test tear-down); background work in the view model that was already running then met a closed database and threw on a background thread, which ends the app process, so an unrelated test in the same run was reported failed or "process crashed". All the view model's background launches now go through one dispatcher whose handler ignores *only* a closed-database error (any other exception still reaches the uncaught handler as before). A stress test (300 refresh triggers against 6 closes) passes with the guard and kills the process without it. This is also what made the earlier ViewModelStartupTest failure (which I could not reproduce then) and the XlsxReaderTest and PrivacyUiTest failures in this slice's first full runs. It is a cause I can show, not proof that no other cause exists.
- `RECEIVE_SMS` is declared with `uses-feature telephony required=false`, so devices without telephony can still install the app (found by the build's lint, which failed the first full run).

**Verification:** 664 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 226 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (Three earlier full runs of this slice failed: lint on the missing telephony feature flag; a crash from the paging race; a crash from background work meeting a closed database. All three are fixed above.)

## Slice 36: categories for captured transactions (FR-CAT-01 layers 1 and 2, FR-CAT-02, FR-CAT-03)
**Built:** Capture from texts, then **Category rules**. A rule is "when the payee or text contains these words, use this category": every word must appear (any order, any case), rules are tried **from the top and the first match wins**, they can be moved up and down and removed, and **before saving one the screen can test it on your history** ("It would have matched 3 of your past entries"). Under the rules sits the **learned payee map**: when you add a captured expense, the payee (reduced to lower-case letters and digits, a UPI address without its bank handle, so `Swiggy`, `SWIGGY.` and `swiggy@icici` are one payee) is remembered with the category you chose, and the next one starts there. **Corrections stick**: choosing a different category for the same payee replaces the earlier one. A rule beats what was learned. The inbox says where a category came from ("your rule" or "what you chose last time") and suggests nothing when nothing applies. A category that was deleted or hidden is never suggested, even if a rule names it. Both are in backups (restored only where this phone has no rule with that id or no entry for that payee).
**Verified:** pure rules (key normalisation, ordering, rule over learned, dead categories, weak keys) on both targets; store tests (order and movement, duplicate and missing-category refusals, an edited rule keeps its place, learn/correct/forget, history counting with `%` and `_` as plain text, deleted entries not counted); 3 device tests through the screens (test-on-history count, add and see the suggestion, first rule wins and Up changes it, learn then correct). 14 mutations; 5 first survived (an edited rule losing its place, `%` acting as a wildcard, counting deleted entries, one-letter keys trusted, and one mutant that did not compile and had to be redone), each now caught.
**Said plainly:** (1) layer 3 of FR-CAT-01, the **shared merchant/VPA dictionary**, is not built: it needs a curated dataset shipped with the app, which I do not have. (2) Matching is plain words in the payee guess and the text: no regular expressions, no amount or account conditions, no "split" rules. (3) Only **debits** get suggestions; a credit never gets a category suggested. (4) Learning happens from the inbox only, not from categories you pick when adding by hand. (5) The payee guess is a heuristic (UPI address, or the name after "at/to"): a text whose payee it cannot read gets no learned suggestion, only a rule can match its words. (6) Rules testing counts payee and note of past entries, which is not exactly the text a future SMS will contain.

**Verification:** 678 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 229 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 37: duplicates (FR-LED-02, first part)
**Built:** every item in **To review** is checked against what is already in the ledger and against the other pending items, and says so. **Against the ledger:** an entry carrying the **same bank reference and amount** is called "Already in your ledger"; otherwise an entry of the **same amount and direction within a day** (an expense or a transfer out for a debit; income, a refund or a transfer in for a credit) is called "Looks like one you already added: Cafe on 5 Oct". The nearest day wins, ties broken the same way every time. A flagged item offers **Same one: skip** and **Add anyway**. Adding something whose reference is already on an SMS entry is refused outright (from slice 35). **Among pending items:** a text with the same direction and amount arriving within **10 minutes** of an earlier one, unless both carry different references, is marked "Probably the same payment as the text from HDFC Bank above" (a bank's alert and an app's about one payment). The same text delivered twice never reaches here (slice 34).
**Verified:** 5 pure tests (reference beats date, the one-day window and its edges, stable ties, blank references, the ten-minute window and its edge, different references are different payments, chains), 6 device tests through the screen (a hand-entered expense, next day counts and two days or another amount or an income do not, same reference on another date, a credit against income and a transfer in and not against an expense, two banks' texts for one payment, reference with another amount is not a match), 11 mutations caught (one survived: the amount check on reference matches, now tested).
**Said plainly:** (1) **"Dedup precision at least 99%" is not measured and cannot be from here**: it needs the labelled corpus of real, consented messages. What exists is correctness on constructed cases. (2) A **same-amount purchase repeated on the next day** (a daily coffee, the same rent twice) will be flagged as "looks like one you already added"; that is why it asks and does not decide. (3) The pending-versus-pending rule only compares *pending* items: once the first is added, the second is checked against the ledger by amount and day instead. (4) Matching ignores the account (a bank text knows only its last four digits), and the payee. (5) Failed-UPI-plus-refund and card-bill pairing (FR-LED-03) are **not built**: they need a message kind the generic parser does not give. (6) "Edits survive re-import" (FR-LED-04) is not built yet. (7) The device suite has now twice failed once at the launch wait of this class's first test and passed on repeat (1 in about 6 runs); the reason is still unknown.

**Verification:** 688 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 235 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 38: capture health and the privacy screen (FR-CAP-13 on-device part, FR-SEC-03 on-device part, FR-SEC-06 partly)
**Built:** **How capture is doing** (Capture from texts): a plain headline (off; Android has not allowed texts; no text seen in 30 days; quiet for a week or more; working) with the advice that fits it (battery saver, and that Android does not deliver texts to a force-stopped app), the counts for the last 30 days (found, same text again, one-time passwords, texts from people, bank notices, declined payments), the last time a transaction and a text of any kind were seen, the banks seen with how many were found and ignored, and **senders not on the list** with a "This is my bank" button that adds the name so its texts are read from the next one on. It is built from the capture-event counts of slice 34, so **it cannot show the words of a text**; a test checks the stored events hold none. **Privacy: what this app can see** (More): states whether the app can use the internet, read from the permissions actually declared in the installed app, and lists **every permission it declares with what it is for and whether Android has allowed it now**. The current built app declares **no internet permission**. A test fails if the app ever declares INTERNET, or declares any permission that has no description on this screen.
**Verified:** 5 pure tests of the status and counts (boundaries at exactly a week, off beats missing permission, ordering and ties, unknown senders), 2 device tests through the screens, 11 mutations caught after 3 survived and were closed (an unknown-sender filter, a tie order, and a redundant filter that I removed).
**Said plainly:** (1) **"SMS not detected" is a guess from silence**: the app cannot tell a phone that received nothing from a text that never reached it, so it lists the causes it knows (battery saver, force-stop, permission) instead of claiming one. (2) There is no **over-the-air parser rules** channel and no **public parser-health page** (FR-CAP-13, FR-SUP-02): both need a server, which this app does not have (and the no-internet promise above would have to change first, deliberately). (3) The privacy screen is read-only: permissions are changed in the phone's settings. (4) The advice text about force-stop is from Android's documented rule and was seen on the emulator (texts sent to a force-stopped app were not captured). (5) Opening this screen does not work around the unexplained **first-launch timeout** seen again in this slice: on an emulator right after a clean install, the very first launch of the app took more than 10 s once and about 100 ms every time after (and in 3 of the next 4 clean installs it was fast), so it is tied to something about that first run, not to the app's start (a fresh install measures about 150 ms); I could not isolate it.

**Verification:** 698 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 237 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 39: pairing (FR-LED-03): cash withdrawals, card payments and refunds are not spending
**Built:** the inbox now reads *what kind* of text it is, from the words, while the text is waiting (`MessageKind`): an **ATM withdrawal**, a **payment to a credit card** (seen from the bank's side or the card's: "payment of INR 15000 received towards your Axis Bank Credit Card", "credit card bill of Rs 2,500 has been paid", "Repayment of Rs 3,750 received for the slice credit card"), or a **refund or reversal** (credits only). Then:
- **Cash withdrawal** is added as a **transfer from your bank account to your Cash account**, not as spending.
- **Credit card payment** is added as a **transfer from your bank account to your credit card account** (the card's debt goes down, nothing counts as spending or income), whichever side's text arrived first; the other side's text is then flagged by the duplicate check (slice 37) as the same payment.
- **Refund** is added as a **refund linked to the purchase it reverses** (an expense of the same amount, or a larger one from the merchant named in the text, in the 120 days before, with that much still refundable), so spending goes down instead of income going up. With no matching purchase it stays plain income.
Each shows what it will do in words, lets you change the from and to accounts, and offers **Add as expense instead / Add as income instead**. With no credit card account (or no cash account) the text is just a payment, as before.
**Verified:** pure kind tests on both targets, run against all 20 public sample files too (every file classified by hand-checking the 13 that came out as card payment or refund: all right, none wrong); 6 device tests through the screens (ATM moves Bank to Cash and nets balances right; a card payment seen from both banks is one transfer, the card's balance is down once, the second text is flagged; a refund links to its purchase and cancels it on the card; no card account means no pairing and the plain-add choice works; a refund with no matching, a too-old, a too-small or an already fully refunded purchase is not matched). 14 mutations; 6 survived at first and each got its own test (reversal wording on a debit, "CC bill/payment" wording, an already-refunded purchase, and others).
**A bug of mine found on the way:** the "refund from an expense" screen from Phase 1 still checked the literal rupee, so in a ledger whose base currency is anything else a refund could not be started. It now uses the ledger's currency (slice 31 missed it).
**Said plainly:** (1) this is **FR-LED-03 partly**: **failed UPI plus refund** is handled only when the refund text says refund or reversed and a same-amount purchase exists; a debit that bounced back with a text that says neither (some banks say only "credited") stays plain income. **Own-account transfers** between two banks are not paired by text, only by the duplicate check when both sides are captured. (2) The wording lists come from a small public sample plus my own knowledge of bank messages; real traffic will have formats they miss, and a text that matches no wording is simply added as an expense or income (never silently as a transfer). (3) Card payment texts carry no payer account, so the "from" account is a guess (the remembered account for that bank, else the first bank account) shown for you to change. (4) The "from/to" you choose is remembered for that bank and last four digits for the next one. (5) Per-source auto-confirm is not built: only a rule written and tested for one bank's exact layout may ever auto-confirm (Phase 0 decision), and no such rule exists yet.

**Verification:** 712 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 243 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (The first full run was killed by the known Compose slot-table crash, `SlotWriter.moveSlotGapTo`, 28 tests in; it passed on the repeat; still unexplained.)

## Slice 40: weekly recap (FR-RPT-06, FR-NOT-01 partly)
**Built:** Notifications, then **Weekly recap**, **off until you turn it on**. Once for each finished Monday-to-Sunday week, from the reminder hour on any day of the week after it (so a phone that was off on Monday still gets it), outside quiet hours: "Last week: ₹12,340.00 spent", then how much more or less than the week before ("₹2,100.00 less than the week before (-15%)") and the biggest category ("Most on Food (42%)"). A quiet week after a busy one says so ("Last week you spent nothing"); two empty weeks send nothing. It has its **own notification channel** ("Weekly recap", low importance) so it can be muted on its own. The week is the calendar week in the phone's time zone: a Sunday-evening purchase belongs to that week, one a minute after midnight on Monday to the next.
**Verified:** 5 pure tests (which week is recapped on each day, across a month end and a year end, the text for less, more, same, no comparison and nothing, the planner: once, not before the hour, off by default, quiet hours, a new key each week), 3 storage tests through the real notification check on in-memory data (the week's boundaries at Sunday 23:00 and Monday 00:00, the previous week's total, the biggest category, the switch, once per week and a new recap the next week, two empty weeks), 1 device test (the switch in settings, the real notification on the emulator in its own channel, once). 10 mutations caught.
**Found and fixed in the test setup, not in the app:** the app's own periodic background check starts with the process and could run in the middle of a notifications test and show the notice first, so the test's own check found nothing: a card-reminder test failed 2 of 3 runs before this slice's test was added. These tests now cancel that background work before they start (4 of 4 runs then passed).
**Said plainly:** (1) the recap is **a notification only**: tapping it opens the app, there is no recap screen and no history of past recaps; the Reports screen already shows any week you choose. (2) **No income, no budget status and no "unusual spending" in it**: just spending, the week before and the top category. (3) The actionable parts of FR-NOT-01 (categorise or split from the notification) are **not built**. (4) It only goes out if the phone's own background scheduling runs the check; on phones that aggressively stop background work it may arrive late, which cannot be tested here. (5) The wording is in English only.

**Verification:** 725 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 245 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (Two earlier full runs failed: one on a notification-shade read race of mine from slice 32, now waiting for the system; one at the very first launch of the whole run, the pattern described in slice 38. That pattern now has its own first test, `AaaFirstLaunchTest`, which absorbs the slow first launch with a 60 s limit and logs its time; every other test keeps its 10 s wait. This hides *which* test hits the slow launch, not the slowness, and the cause is still unknown.)

## Slice 41: edits survive re-import (FR-LED-04), and a crash found proving it
**What was already true, now proved on every path:** a statement re-import never overwrites an edit and never brings back a deleted entry (Phase 1, 12 importer tests), a backup restore skips what is already there, and a **text delivered again** can no longer change an entry: it appears in **To review** as "Already in your ledger: *the name you gave it*", and Add is refused with a reason. New device tests cover the two text cases: after an edit (name and amount are untouched) and after a delete.
**A real crash found by the delete case:** a captured text whose bank reference belongs to an entry the person **deleted** made *Add* crash the app. The database's uniqueness rule on (source, reference) also covers deleted entries, my check looked only at live ones, the database threw `SQLiteConstraintException` (not the kind of error the screen handles), and that ends the process. It now says "You deleted an entry from this message earlier, so it is not added again. Skip this one." Without the check the new test kills the app process, so the test would catch a regression.
**Said plainly:** (1) entries typed by hand have nothing reliable to match a re-import on; a statement or text for something you typed yourself is only caught by the amount-and-day duplicate flag (slice 37), which asks, not decides. (2) A deleted entry cannot be brought back from a re-imported text; getting it back means restoring it from the delete's undo or typing it again. (3) Texts without a bank reference have no key except the text itself, so a re-send with different wording is judged by the amount-and-day flag only.

**Verification:** 725 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 247 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 42: notification capture, opt-in (FR-CAP-06)
**Built:** Capture from texts now also has **Read payment app notifications** (off by default). When it is on and the phone's **Notification access** has been given to this app (you turn that on in the phone's own settings: the switch opens that screen, and there is a button for it), the app reads the notification a **Google Pay, PhonePe, Paytm or BHIM** payment shows ("Paid ₹250" / "to Swiggy": the title and the text are joined) and puts a payment or a credit in **To review**, the same way a bank text does, with the same duplicate, category, transfer and refund help. **Notifications from every other app are dropped on the first line without their words being read**, as are group summaries (so a bundle of payments is not counted twice). One-time passwords, offers and failed payments are counted in capture health and never kept. The privacy screen now also lists **Notification access** and whether it is on.
**Verified on the emulator through the real system:** a device test grants the service through the same secure setting the phone's screen writes, posts notifications, and the system delivers them to the listener: with the switch off nothing is read; with it on "Paid ₹1,250 / to Swiggy" becomes one pending item (source NOTIFICATION, a debit of 1,250.00, issuer Google Pay), "Received ₹500 / from Ravi Kumar" a credit, an advert and an OTP are counted and not kept, the same notification again is one item, a group summary is not read. 7 mutations caught plus one survivor closed (the group summary); 1 equivalent survivor (using the time the service ran instead of the time the system stamped, which differs only if delivery is delayed).
**Said plainly:** (1) **the wording of real Google Pay, PhonePe, Paytm and BHIM notifications was not checked**: I tested wordings I expect ("Paid ₹250 to X", "Received ₹500 from Y") and the generic parser reads them, but a real notification that says something else is simply not a transaction. The package names are the apps' public identifiers, from memory. (2) For tests, **measurement and test builds also read this app's own notifications** (so a test can post one); a release build does not. (3) **Notification access is a powerful grant:** the system warns about it, and the app asks for it only on this switch. Play has its own review for apps that use it; this is separate from the SMS declaration and I have not written either. (4) A payment app and the bank both announcing one payment gives two items, which the duplicate check flags (same amount within the day, or two texts within ten minutes). (5) Only payment *apps*, not bank apps: banks' own app notifications are not read.

**Verification:** 733 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 251 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (The first full run failed in 4 AccountsUiTest tests with 'Activity never becomes DESTROYED (last state PAUSED)' while the emulator's own launcher was repeatedly crashing ('Crashing app skipping ANR: com.google.android.apps.nexuslauncher' in the log): an emulator fault, not this app; it passed on the repeat.)

## Slice 43: person or business behind a UPI address (FR-CAT-04, label only) and Phase 2 status
**Built:** the review row says "to a person" or "from a person" when the UPI address is a **mobile number** (money sent by phone number to a contact), "to a business" when it has a well-known merchant QR shape (Paytm or BharatPe QR, Razorpay, a PhonePe "q" plus digits), and **nothing** otherwise. A name-like address (`ravi.kumar@ybl`, `shop@okhdfc`) could be either a person who chose a name or a small shop, so it is not guessed. Tested on both targets (12 address shapes, bare numbers, none), 4 mutations caught plus one survivor closed, and on the screen.
**Said plainly:** this is **FR-CAT-04 partly**: the label exists, but a person-to-person payment is **not categorised differently** (no "Transfers/People" default, no person ledger yet, which is Phase 3) and there is **no shared merchant/VPA dictionary** (FR-CAT-01 layer 3 needs a curated dataset I do not have), which is what would turn `swiggy@icici` into "Swiggy, Food" for everyone.

### Phase 2 status, from the roadmap table
| Roadmap item | State |
|---|---|
| FR-CAP-05 SMS capture, sender allowlist on the header root, OTP and personal texts ignored | **Built** (slices 34, 35); allowlist provisional; **Play SMS declaration not written** |
| FR-CAP-06 notification capture, opt-in | **Built** (42); real notification wording unchecked |
| FR-CAP-12 review inbox with confidence | **Built**; **per-source auto-confirm NOT built**: see below |
| FR-CAP-13 capture health, "SMS not detected" | On-device report **built** (38); **over-the-air parser rules not built** (needs a server) |
| FR-LED-02 dedup, FR-LED-03 pairing, FR-LED-04 edits survive | **Built** (37, 39, 41), precision unmeasured |
| FR-CAT-01 layers 1 and 2, FR-CAT-02, FR-CAT-03, FR-CAT-04 | **Built** (36, 43); layer 3 (shared dictionary) **not built** |
| FR-NOT-01 actionable notifications | Weekly recap **built** (40); **categorise or split from the notification not built** |
| FR-RPT-06 weekly recap | **Built** (40) |
| FR-SEC-03 / FR-SEC-06 | Privacy screen and rationale text **built** (38) |
| FR-SUP-01 in-app support with human escalation, FR-SUP-02 public parser-health page | **Not built**: both need people and a server |
**Per-source auto-confirm is not built, on purpose.** The Phase 0 rule stands: only a parse produced by a rule written and tested for one bank's exact message layout may skip review, and **no such rule exists**: every parse today is the generic one (which reads about 1 in 65 directions wrong on public samples). An auto-confirm switch now would have nothing to act on. It needs per-bank rules (the roadmap's 200 to 300 message variants for the top 25 issuers), written from real messages from consenting testers.
**Phase 2 exit criteria: none is met, and none can be from here.** Parser coverage of 98% and dedup precision of 99% need a labelled corpus of real messages (what exists is 152 sanitised public samples and constructed cases); SMS to inbox within 2 s and battery need a real phone and real delivery; the Play declaration needs the company's account; 85% auto-captured and 5 corrections per 100 after 30 days need testers.

**Verification:** 739 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 252 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (The first full run was killed by the known Compose slot-table crash, SlotWriter.moveSlotGapTo, 237 tests in, in a different class than before; it passed on the repeat; still unexplained.)

## Slice 44: fixes from the review of Phase 2
**Fixed, each with a test that fails without the fix:**
1. **The database race at its root.** `AppContainer.close()` and `startFresh()` did not hold the write lock, and `write()` picked up the database before waiting for it, so a write could run against a driver closed in between (a closed database for the "start fresh" button in production as well as in tests). Now the write lock is taken first everywhere, then the open mutex, and the database is resolved only once the write lock is held. The guard from slice 35 stays as a second line of defence.
2. **A refund no longer looks like a duplicate of the payment it undoes.** Refund and failed-payment texts carry the original payment's bank reference; matching by reference alone called them "Already in your ledger" and one tap on *Same one: skip* would have thrown away the money coming back. A reference now counts as the same transaction only when it points the same way; the opposite way says "looks like its refund or reversal, not a repeat", keeps *Add* as the main action, and the entry gets its own key instead of being refused.
3. **Statement import against captured entries.** A payment confirmed from its text and then imported from the month's statement used to become two entries. A statement row that matches an entry from another source (text, notification, typed by hand) on the same account, direction and amount within a day is now not added again, **counted and shown on the import result** ("N matched entries you already have"). Each existing entry answers for at most one row, so two real same-amount purchases are not both swallowed.
4. **Entry time.** A captured entry keeps the real time of day of its text when it arrived on the bank's date; only a delayed text is placed at noon on the bank's date (before: always noon, which lost the order within a day and the time for the duplicate windows).
5. **Corrections later stick too.** Changing the category of a captured entry in the ordinary editor now teaches the payee, as choosing in the inbox does.
6. **Money into a credit card is not offered as income.** A plain credit on an account remembered as a card is proposed as a payment received on the card (a transfer from your bank), with *Add as income instead* still there.
7. **Test hygiene for notification capture.** The app's own notifications (bill and card reminders, the recap carry amounts) are never read by the capture service; the test hook is now a marker on the notification, not the app's package.
**Checked and rejected from the review:** a force-stop explaining the missing banner (already known and documented); the receiver permission and goAsync (already so); the fallback dedup key (it already includes the text's own timestamp); the refund currency (fixed in slice 39); the card test read-once race (fixed in slice 40). **Not accepted as the cause:** that the first-launch stall is a launcher dialog: I saw one launcher crash, but never captured what held focus during a stall, so the cause stays unknown.

**Verification:** 746 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 257 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (Earlier full runs of this slice failed: the known Compose slot-table crash twice, in different classes, and once a new-row timing gate at p95 19.3 ms that ran at 5.1 to 6.3 ms three times alone right after; both unexplained, each passed on repeat.)

### Slice 44 follow-up, and what is still open
**Also fixed after a second look at the review:** the home-screen widget read the quick entries outside the write lock (now inside it); the inbox now also looks up the suffixed reference of a reversal that was already added, so a re-delivered reversal says "Already in your ledger" and adding it again is refused with a reason, not a database error (new device test: debit, reversal, reversal again).
**Still open, plainly:**
1. **The Compose slot-table crash is getting more frequent, not rarer:** it killed 3 of the last 5 full runs, always while an activity is being destroyed (`performDestroyActivity`), in different test classes. It has not been investigated this session beyond recording it. It is a crash inside a library while a screen closes, so a real person leaving a screen could in principle meet it; I have no evidence either way. Next steps, not done: try the newest stable Compose runtime, look for state changed off the main thread (the capture and inbox flows are the newest background-to-screen paths), and use the recreate stress test to get a reliable repro.
2. The one timing-gate miss (p95 19.3 ms against 12.5) could not be reproduced; the lock change and the statement-import match query were not timed on the 100,000-entry dataset.
3. The notification-capture test hook is a marker on the app's own test notification; a cleaner test would build a notification as if from the real Google Pay package and call the service's handler directly.

**Verification:** 746 host/JVM tests, 258 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests passed in 2 of 3 consecutive full runs of this exact code; the third was killed by the Compose slot-table crash. **Compose crash investigation, partial:** in isolation it is rare (1 crash in 20 runs of two classes that had crashed in full runs), so it needs a long-lived process. I added `compose.waitForIdle()` before every test activity is closed (a plausible race between the test environment's recomposer and the activity teardown): before it, 4 of the last 6 full runs crashed; after it, 1 of 3. That is **not** enough to say it helps, and the crash is not explained.

## Slice 45: statements whose descriptions contain unquoted commas (found with the owner's own HDFC text exports)
**The problem:** three real HDFC "text" statement exports imported all but a few dozen rows. Every refused row had a comma inside its narration: the file does not quote it, so the row has one cell too many, every column after it moves one place right, and the debit read as a date ("The debit '...' is not an amount"). About an eighth of the rows were refused overall.
**Fix:** a row with more cells than the header has is put back together by folding the surplus cells into the description column (empty cells at the end, from a trailing comma, are dropped first, so they are never mistaken for text). The import preview says how many rows were put back together.
**Checked against the bank's own numbers:** with the fix every row of all three exports imports with no problems, and in each file opening balance plus credits minus debits equals the statement's closing balance to the paisa; each file's closing balance is also the next file's opening balance. (The real statements are not in the repository and no figures from them are recorded here; the tests use synthetic rows with the same layout, including a narration with commas.)
**Said plainly:** (1) the narration is rejoined with a plain comma, so its original spacing around the comma is kept as the bank wrote it; (2) the repair assumes the narration is the only free-text column, which holds for this layout; a file with commas in two text columns would still need its own handling; (3) this does not categorise the rows: they arrive uncategorised, as every import does.

**Verification:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 258 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 46: a livelier home screen (look and feel, step one)
**What changed (judged from screenshots of the old home, add form and drawer):** the month total now sits in a **hero card** in the accent colour (a soft gradient to a deeper tone of itself; in dark mode, where the accent is lightened, to a lighter one, so the text keeps its contrast at both ends), with Search, Reports and More inside it; the **balances are small cards** (name over amount) instead of outlined chips; the list is **grouped under day headings** ("Today", "Yesterday", "Wed, 30 Sep"), each row has a **tinted initial** of its category (the same colour for the same name every time) and a bolder amount, money in is green; rows **animate into place** when the list changes; the **Add button is extended ("+ Add") at the top of the list and shrinks to a round "+" while scrolling**. Every test tag is unchanged. The day headings are items of their own in the lazy list, so scrolling stays lazy, and everything is drawn opaque.
**Verified:** the full tests (the balance-card tests were updated for the new two-line card), screenshots in light and in dark with a different accent, and the scroll check at 100,000 entries on the emulator (below).
**Not done, said plainly:** no glass or blur yet, no bottom bar, no redesign of the add form, no screen transitions: those are step two. Motion follows the phone's "remove animations" setting because it uses the standard animation APIs; I have not turned that setting on to see it. Category colours are derived from the name; the colour chosen in Categories is not used here yet.

Emulator figures, best case; the same unverified phone caveats apply.

## Slice 47: look and feel, step two
**Changed:** the hero card has a soft light in its top corner and a fine edge, and its actions sit in a **frosted pill** (a translucent fill with a fine edge); the add form's **amount is large and bold** with rounded corners and each **category chip carries its coloured initial**; **every screen except the add and edit form rises and fades in** over 220 ms when it opens (the home list does not, so cold start is untouched; the form opens at once, so the measured 18 ms to a focused amount field is the whole of what the person waits for). Load-more was checked after the day headings were added: the trigger counted list positions, which headings inflate, so it would have loaded far too eagerly; it now counts entries, with a device test (400 entries on 400 days: scrolling to the middle loads nothing, scrolling to the end loads one page and does not run away).
**Not done, said plainly:** the glass here is **translucent and edged, not blurred**. Real blur needs Android 12 or later (the emulator is Android 8, where it could not be seen), and I kept it off the scrolling list on purpose; a later step could add it to the hero card on Android 12+ only. **No bottom bar**: it would move Search, Reports and More, and I would rather ask you before changing where those live. Screen transitions are an entrance only (no exit animation or shared-element motion): a cross-fade between two screens puts both on screen at once, which the tests, and the many screens that share "back" buttons, do not allow without more work. The grey "Back up" card is unchanged.

**Verification:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 259 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. **Emulator, 100,000 entries, machine load 6.6:** scroll 0.3% janky frames (p99 12 ms), cold start data composed 193 ms, add screen 18 ms, warm start 51 ms: no change from before the redesign. Best-case emulator figures; nothing measured on a phone.

**Follow-up to the review of step two:** the add and edit form no longer use the entrance animation (an earlier version added 220 ms of fade to the screen where speed matters most; the measured 18 ms stopped at the focused field and did not include it). No test covers that the form is exempt (the animation is not observable from the tests without timing it), so this is checked by reading the code and by eye only. The load-more test was mutation-checked (reverting to the position-based trigger made it fail), so it does discriminate. The chips are still the standard filter chips, so their selected state and touch size are unchanged.

**Verification of the follow-up:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 259 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 48: look and feel, step three
**Changed:** **bottom bar** (Home, Search, Reports, More) replaces the buttons that were inside the hero card; Search, Reports and More keep their tags and More opens the same menu, so the many tests that go "More, then a screen" are unchanged. The hero card is now just the title and the figure, and on **Android 12 and later** two softly **blurred lights** sit behind them (below Android 12 the corner light and fine edge from step two are all there is). The **back-up card** and the **"to review" banner** are tinted rounded cards in the accent and the tertiary colour instead of grey slabs. Screen entrance stays for every screen except the add and edit form.
**One test changed, on purpose:** `ListHeaderTest` asserted the title was wider than 120 dp, a guard against the old header overflowing and squeezing "Spent this month" to one letter. The actions are no longer beside the title, so it is just its own text (about 106 dp); the threshold is now 90 dp (a squeeze showed as about 10 dp) and the height check is unchanged. That is a loosening of a number to fit a layout change, not of what it protects.
**Not done, said plainly:** the bottom bar has **text labels only, no icons** (the Material icon set is not a dependency and unicode glyphs are not reliable on Android 8), so it looks plainer than a typical bar; the bar is on the home screen only; real blur is on the hero only and nowhere near a scrolling list; **no exit animation** or shared-element motion between screens.

**Verification:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 259 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

### Step three, checked on a real Android 17 image
The Android 12+ blur was never seen on the Android 8 emulator, so I booted the Pixel_7 image (Android 17) and looked. **Two real faults only that image showed, both fixed:** (1) the blurred lights were sized to fill the whole screen, stretching the hero card over everything and drawing hard-edged squares: they now take the card's size (`matchParentSize`) and use an unbounded blur edge; (2) on Android 15 and later the app draws under the system bars and the clock and battery were **white on a pale screen** (invisible): the bars' icon colour now follows light/dark. A first version of that fix touched the window from a worker thread under the test harness's clock and failed one test with `CalledFromWrongThreadException`; it is now posted to the view's own thread (this is the same family as the unexplained Compose crash, a recomposition running on a worker under the test clock).
Screenshots of Android 8 and Android 17, light and dark, are on the Desktop. The second emulator was shut down before the tests, which would otherwise have run on both.

**Verification:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 259 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 49: from the owner's phone (Samsung S22 Plus): tabs under the navigation bar, coloured debits and credits, a nicer More menu
**1. Tabs under the navigation bar (a real bug, reproduced).** With the phone's navigation bar showing, the Home, Search and Reports tabs sat underneath it and could not be tapped. Cause: on Android 15 and later an app draws under the system bars (the app targets API 36) and my bottom bar did not keep clear of them; the Save buttons of the add form, category form, import, repeating form and the bulk-edit bar had the same fault. **Reproduced on an Android 17 emulator with three-button navigation**: before the fix the tabs spanned y 2295 to 2348 px, under the bar that starts near 2270; after it they span 2169 to 2222 px, above it (screenshots on the Desktop). All six now pad for the navigation bar. **No automated test guards this**: the test tools used here cannot run on Android 17 (`InputManager.getInstance` is gone) and on the Android 8 emulator the bar is never drawn over the app, so a test there would pass with or without the fix; I wrote one, found it could not fail where it could run, and deleted it rather than keep a test that proves nothing. It was checked by measuring the screen.
**2. Debits and credits told apart.** Money out is red with a minus ("-₹125.00"), money in is green with a plus ("+₹5,000.00"), and a transfer between your own accounts has neither (it is not spending or income). The reds and greens are chosen per theme (darker in light mode, lighter in dark mode) so each reads against its background, but I did **not** measure their contrast ratios; they were chosen by eye. Applied to the home list and to search results; reports, the calendar's day list and the review inbox still show plain amounts. Tested: the sign and kind logic with 3 mutations caught, and the list shows the right sign for an expense, an income and a transfer.
**3. The More menu.** Grouped under four headings (Inbox, Money, Make it yours, Privacy and data) as **two tiles to a row**, each with a tinted round badge and a symbol, a count badge on "To review", rounded corners. A first version was one tall list; on the test phone it did not fit, the lower items were scrolled out of reach, and 23 tests that open them failed (they found the items but could not see them): tiles fit all fourteen places on one screen. If a phone's screen is shorter than about 560 dp the menu would still need scrolling, and I have not tried one. Every item keeps its tag, so the tests are unchanged. The symbols are emoji, chosen from the oldest emoji set after two (a puzzle piece and a shield) showed as empty boxes on Android 8; **emoji look different on every phone maker's font** (Samsung's will not match the screenshots).

**Verification:** 750 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 261 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. (Three earlier full runs of this slice failed: 23 tests because the first, one-column More menu did not fit on screen; one because `AddExpenseFlowTest` still expected the row without its new minus sign, now updated to check the minus on the row. Run alone, that class's other test failed 2 of 3 times at its launch wait; on the previous commit with none of this slice's changes it failed 2 of 5 times, so it is the known unexplained first-launch stall, not this change.)

## Slice 50: from the owner's phone, round two
**1. More is a page, not a pop-up.** Tapping More now shows the tiles as a full page in place of the list, with the bottom bar kept (More is highlighted; Home returns; Back closes it; the add button hides). Two tiles to a row, three on a screen 600 dp or wider. The bottom bar's four tabs are equal width, one line each, and the label shrinks to fit (checked at 720x1520 with the system font at 130%: "Search" and "Reports" fit, though at that size they come out in slightly different sizes, which is uneven but not cut off). All tags are unchanged.
**2. Review cards on a narrow screen.** The buttons now wrap onto a second line instead of running off the edge (checked at the same narrow, large-text setting).
**3. Credit shown as debit.** I could not see the owner's actual PhonePe text, so this is a fix for the wording I could reason about, not a confirmed fix for that message. A text like "... received ... Sent by RAVI via UPI" held both a credit word and "sent", and the parser took the first debit-like word. The passive forms "sent by / paid by / transferred by" are now read as the other person acting when the text also has a credit word ("paid by your card at X" with no credit word is still a debit). Mutation-checked, and the sample-corpus recognition count is unchanged (a first, wider version of the rule lost 29 recognised samples and was narrowed). **Fallback for any other wording:** every card now has "It was money in" / "It was money out", which turns it before adding (store test, device test, both mutation-checked). **Please send me the redacted PhonePe text** so I can fix the real cause.
**4. Two payments of 100 in a minute showing as one, adding 100.** The cause is **inferred, not observed**: I have no real message. Two things could produce it, and both are fixed. (a) One delivery that carries two separate alerts was joined into one text and only the first amount was read; each piece that is a whole transaction on its own is now kept as its own message, and a tail is joined to the one before it (`SmsParts`, 5 tests, mutation-checked; the receiver wiring itself has no automated test because it needs a real broadcast). (b) Two same-amount payments from one sender were labelled "Probably the same payment as the text above", and a second 100 was labelled "Looks like one you already added" after the first was added; now the same amount from the same sender is two payments unless the bank reference is the same, and two different references are two payments. This **reverses an earlier decision** (a test said "a different reference still counts as a likely duplicate"): the rationale was a hand-typed entry with another number, which is rare against the real case. Seen on the emulator: two identical 100 texts a second apart give two separate cards with no duplicate note.
**5. Set a budget.** Now a tinted rounded card with a "Set" button and a line saying what it does; once set, a larger "left of limit" line, a thicker rounded bar, and the per-day figure. Colours follow the state (on track, near, over). Tags and texts the tests use are unchanged.
**6. The launch stall in tests: cause found.** Captured with a screenshot, focus and a dump at the failure: the screen was the app's own "Your data can't be opened: the Keystore key is gone" page, not a hang. The tests wiped the database with `container.close()` then `EncryptedDriver.reset()` **outside the container's locks**; background work (the app's idle maintenance after a screen closes) can open the database in the middle of the reset, leaving a key file with no Keystore key. `AppContainer.startFresh()` does the same wipe under the locks. After switching one class to it, 6 of 6 runs passed where about half failed before; then 46 reset sites in 44 test files were switched. **The race itself I did not observe directly; it is the explanation that fits the evidence.** The same stall in the very first launch after install (`AaaFirstLaunchTest`) has no earlier test and may be a different cause. Production only wipes through `startFresh()`, so a user cannot hit this.

**Verification:** 767 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 262 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 51: PhonePe notification captured twice (real evidence from the owner's phone)
**What was seen:** one payment of 10 received through PhonePe gave **two identical "To review" cards** (this time correctly as money in: "Chandu: Chandu. sent ₹10 to you."). So the notification path, not the SMS path, duplicates, and the earlier fix for "sent by" credits worked on that wording.
**Cause, partly mine:** an app can post one notification twice (the update or a second post), each with its own post time, so each had its own fingerprint. And my previous change (two same-sender, same-amount texts with no reference are two payments) removed the "Probably the same payment" label that would have flagged the second one. I did not see why PhonePe posts twice and cannot tell from the screenshot how many seconds apart the two were.
**Fixed in two steps:**
1. **Repost guard** (`Capture.REPOST_WINDOW_MS`, 10 s): the same words from the same app within 10 s are one payment and the second is dropped (counted as a duplicate event). Applies to notifications only: two identical bank *texts* a second apart stay two payments. The tradeoff is explicit: two real payments of the same amount to the same person within 10 s would be merged (needs the same person, the same amount, the same words, inside 10 s). Once an item has been added its words are forgotten, so a repost after that would not be matched.
2. **Label for slower reposts:** two notifications from one app with the same amount within 10 minutes are labelled "Probably the same payment as ... above" (with "Same one: skip") even though they share a sender, unless their references differ. Nothing is dropped on this rule.
Tests: store query (edges of the window, source, sender, words), ingest at 3 s, 9 s, just past 10 s and with other words, the SMS case, the label rule; 8 mutations, all caught.
**Also:** the review card's amount is now signed and coloured like the home list (minus and red out, plus and green in), so a wrong direction is visible before Add; the flip test checks the minus before and the plus after.
**Withdrawn:** the `SmsParts` splitting from slice 50 is removed. Android delivers one broadcast per logical message, so two separate texts do not arrive in one delivery, I had no evidence for it, and it could cut a real two-part alert into two items. The duplicate-label changes in slice 50 stay.

**Verification:** 760 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 264 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (host count is lower than slice 50 because the five `SmsParts` tests were removed, net of the new ones).

Compose follows the phone's animation-scale setting, so with animations off these snap; I did not test that setting.

## Slice 54: names and starter categories
**Names.** `PayeeGuess` now reads the person's name from a payment app's notification: the title ("Ravi Kumar: ...") or the leading sentence ("Chandu. sent ₹10 to you", "Anita paid you ₹120"). A name that is a heading ("Payment successful", "Money received", "HDFC Bank", "Dear Customer") or has an amount is rejected. A name in the title beats a bare phone-number address; with no title the UPI address is still the name. Written against the owner's real wording; the other shapes are my guesses at how PhonePe and Google Pay word things. **"Name this payee"** on each review card: type a name once for an address (a phone-number address, say) and every later text for the same address shows it, and the entry's merchant uses it. Blank clears it.
**Categories with nothing learned.** Order is now: your rules, then what you chose for this payee, then a **starter list** of common Indian merchants (Swiggy, Zomato, Uber, Ola, Jio, Airtel, Amazon, Netflix and about 100 more, in `DefaultRules`), then, for a payment to a mobile-number address, **Other** with the note "a payment to a person". The starter list matches whole words of the **payee only** (never the bank's name or footer, so "Airtel Payments Bank" does not make everything Bills), uses a category only if one with that name exists and is visible, and the card says where the suggestion came from ("a common merchant"). Everything stays a suggestion you can change.
**Not done, said plainly:** no contacts lookup (it needs the contacts permission, a Play-review and privacy cost; left off); **no suggestion for credits** because income has no category in this app; the starter list is my own and unvalidated against real statements; a payment to a *named* person (as opposed to a phone-number address) is not recognised as a person, so it gets no "Other" default until you pick once and it is learned; **remembered names are not in backups** (neither are learned categories or account choices: the backup carries a fixed list of settings), so a restore starts without them.
Tests: 12 new unit cases and 2 device tests; 10 mutations, 8 caught, 2 equivalent (the name pattern already excludes digits and "bank" was already in the not-a-name set), and one test added to catch the prefix-match mutant.

**Verification:** 770 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 266 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 55: old-format Excel statements, lent and borrowed, suggested categories
**1. Excel import (`.xls`).** The importer only read `.xlsx` (the zip format); a bank statement saved as the older binary `.xls` was refused before its contents were looked at. `XlsReader` (core/domain, no library: the compound file plus the BIFF8 records) now reads it: shared strings including those split across CONTINUE records, numbers (plain, RK, MULRK), formulas' saved values, dates by number format, workbooks small enough to live in the compound file's mini stream. Refuses a password-protected file, an Excel 5/95 file and damage with a message. **Checked against the owner's real HDFC statement** (kept outside the repo and not committed): every cell identical to xlrd's, the header found below the bank/customer preamble, every transaction row read, and the withdrawal and deposit totals equal to the sheet's own. The tests use made-up fixtures (a 150-row bank-style sheet, a tiny mini-stream sheet) with 9 mutations checked. **Also changed:** a row with neither a usable date nor a usable amount (the asterisk separator under the header, the summary block, the footer) is now ignored instead of being reported as a problem; a row with a real date and a bad amount, or a good amount and a bad date, is still reported. On the device the real file was read through the same code on the JVM, not through the phone's file picker.
**2. Lent and borrowed.** New kind in the add form ("Lent or borrowed": I gave / I got, a person, an account) and a **Lent and borrowed** page under More, Money: totals owed to you and owed by you, every person with their balance, and a person's page with the history and shortcuts ("I gave", "I got", "<name> paid it back" / "I paid it back", which fills in the whole amount the other way). Part payments just work: a person's balance is what you gave less what you got. It is built on the ledger's existing settlement entry, so it moves the account's balance but is **not spending or income** (the month total stays untouched, tested). New `person` table (with an upgrade path and a covering index), people in the backup file (documented, absent in older backups), entries titled by the person's name so search finds them, rows signed and coloured like the rest. Edit from the person's history; remove a person who has no entries. Tested through the real form and pages (3 device tests), the store (5 tests incl. backup round trip) and the domain; 10 mutations checked. **Not done:** renaming a person; a person's balance counts only these entries, not shared-expense receivables (the app has no UI for those yet); people are not part of the future sync design yet; no reminder to ask for money back; **loan interest is not tracked**.
**3. Categories.** A **Suggested** button on the Categories page opens a catalogue of ready-made categories under headings (Daily life, Home, Lifestyle, Health and learning, Money, People), each group with its usual subcategories: tick a group or single ones, and "Add N categories" creates them in one step. A group whose name you already have (the default Food, say) gets its subcategories added under that one; nothing is added twice. The "All categories" list in the add form is now grouped under parents (parent first, children indented) until you type, then flat "Parent › Child". The starter-merchant lookup now uses top-level categories only, so a subcategory named "Other" cannot take the place of the top-level one. **The headings are only shown on the suggestion screen; a category does not remember its heading.**

**Verification:** 791 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 273 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. The first full run of this slice hit the known Compose crash (`SearchUiTest`, a state write from a background thread; process died, 257 device tests ran); it was repeated by hand without changes and passed: 791 desktop-side, 273 device (1 skipped), 15 timing-gate. That is one more occurrence for the open crash investigation.

Follow-up to slice 55 (after review): a file with an Excel name that is really a web page or XML (some banks do this) now gets a plain message telling the person to save it as .xlsx or CSV, instead of being read as garbage CSV; reading such pages is not built. `BackupPerson` is now covered by the backup-document test. Correction to an earlier statement: **learned payee categories (`merchantMap`) and capture rules are in backups**; what is not is remembered payee names (`payee_name:`) and remembered accounts (`capture_acct:`). Checked only by the host tests and `ImportUiTest` (not a full verify run), because the change is a message and a test list.

## Slice 56: HDFC card "Txn" alert was not detected
A user's text "Txn Rs.20.00 On HDFC Bank Card 0862 At paytm.s2x4b6x@pty by UPI 664912339598 On 10-10 Not You? ..." was dropped as "no direction": it has no "debited" or "spent". The layout had been left unrecognised on purpose (a sign must not be guessed), with a test saying so, because no real sample existed. Now one does. **A text that starts with "Txn" and mentions a card and "At" is read as money out, but only for HDFC** (`GenericAlertParser.parse(body, senderRoot)`; the rule runs only when the header root is `HDFCBK`, so another bank's text in the same wording is still refused; my first version applied it to every bank and my summary said otherwise, which a review caught) (a credit word such as "credited" still decides the other way); it gets the card digits, the UPI reference, and the merchant (`PayeeGuess` now accepts "At <address>"). **This loosens an earlier rule on the strength of one text from one user**, so it is one HDFC wording; like every generic result it goes to "To review" and is never added by itself. The old test now asserts that texts with no direction and no card use are still refused. Three mutations caught. Other banks' card alerts without a direction word are still not read (tested). The text has no year ("On 10-10"), so the entry is dated the day the text arrived.

**Verification:** 796 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 273 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

**The test crash, narrowed.** Slice 55's first run and this slice's first two runs all failed with `CalledFromWrongThreadException` ending in the slot-table crash (SearchUiTest twice, HomeLayoutUiTest once after the first fix). The full stack shows the recomposition (`applyChanges`, `SubcomposeLayout.forceRecomposeChildren`, `requestLayout`) running on the **thread that wrote the state**, driven by the test harness's frame clock: a state write from the database thread makes the harness run the frame on that thread. Real devices use the main-thread frame clock, so this looks like a **test artefact, not something a user can hit** (inferred from the stack; not proven). Mitigation, not a cure: the search result and the slow figures are now published on the main thread (`withContext(Dispatchers.Main)`); after that one full run passed. Other background state writes (about a dozen sites, mostly in `refresh`) still exist and could fail the same way; if it recurs, move those too.

## Slice 57: a better To review
**Before:** each item was a block of small text with six equal buttons, the bank's whole message shown, and a "Change" toggle: slow to scan, one at a time. **Now:** a header ("4 to review", the money out and in waiting, and **Add all N clear ones** with a confirm that states the totals); each item is a card with an avatar, who, a large signed coloured amount, one line of facts (date, bank, card digits, person or business), warnings (a likely repeat, a related entry) as coloured callouts first, then a tinted **"Goes to <account> · <category> (why)"** box with its own Change, one wide **Add** (it says what it will do: "Add anyway", "Add as credit card payment") and Skip; the less usual actions (money in/out flip, name the payee, add as expense instead, same one: skip) are small buttons, and the **original message is behind "Show message"**. Cards animate away as they are added.
**"Clear" for Add all** means: not flagged as a repeat, no related entry, an ordinary payment (not a transfer or refund), **and the account is one the person has used for that bank card before, or the only account**. A new user with several accounts therefore gets no Add all until they have added one item per card. Anything refused on adding is counted and shown at the top. A review point caught that my first filter would have accepted an account the app had merely guessed.
Seen on the emulator with four made-up texts, including the owner's HDFC card text (Rs.20, card 0862, merchant `paytm.s2x4b6x@pty`, "a business"). Mutation-checked (6). **Not done:** swipe to add or skip; grouping by day; editing the amount or date on the card; the card shows the account and category as one sentence, not as two chips.

**Verification:** 798 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 276 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 58: a richer home banner
The one-line "N to review" banner on the home screen is now a card: **N to review** in bold, what is waiting in total ("₹1,769.00 out · ₹250.00 in, found in your texts"), the first three items by name with their signed, coloured amounts, "and N more", and a Review button; the whole card opens To review. It also uses remembered payee names. The HDFC card rule now matches HDFC Bank by issuer, so it covers both of its headers (`HDFCBK` and `HDFCBN`; tested). A credit text that says "Sent by RAVI KUMAR" now shows that name instead of the bank. Screenshot taken from a throwaway instrumentation test that filled the inbox through `Capture.ingest` (no SMS permission needed), then deleted. **Reviewed and not done:** "Edit and add" (open the full entry form from a card): worthwhile, but it must go through the same confirm path as the card (keeps source and reference, learns the category, marks the item done) and I have not built it. The review points about Add all (account must be a remembered one; refusals counted) were already in the previous slice.

**Verification:** 800 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 277 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

**The test crash, a better fix.** The slice 58 run crashed again at a third background state write (`LedgerViewModel` init collector), confirming that fixing write sites one at a time is endless (84 state writes). The cause, from the stack: the Compose test harness resumes a screen collector on the thread that wrote the state, and recomposes there. The screen's flow collectors in `MainActivity` (21) now resume on `Dispatchers.Main.immediate`, so a write from any thread wakes the screen on the main thread. After that, **two full runs in a row passed** (277 device tests each); before it, about half the runs crashed. Two runs is encouraging, not proof. This changes nothing on a phone, where the collectors already ran on the main thread.

## Slice 59: reports, visual
**Banner cost (review point, valid):** the home banner's preview loaded up to 500 pending messages and ran the payee guess over each one on every refresh (after every save). It now uses one totals query (`inboxPendingTotals`, tested, 2 mutations caught) and the three newest rows; the guess runs for those three only, with remembered payee names. The cost with a large backlog was not timed; it is now bounded by the query, not the backlog.
**Reports.** The page now has: a **summary card** in the app's glossy header style (Spent large, the comparison as a coloured chip whose words, "more" or "less", carry the meaning, and tiles for Income and Income minus spending; all three still open their entries); a **ring** of the five biggest slices and "Others" with the total in the middle and a legend that opens each slice (it sweeps in; works for category, account, tag and merchant views); a **trend chart** with rounded bars that grow in, the selected period bold with its amount named underneath; and coloured, rounded share bars in the list, using the category's own colour or a palette colour. Tags used by tests are unchanged; the list is tagged `report_list`, and the existing tests now scroll to rows because the rows sit lower. A new test checks the ring's shares, that the smallest slices fold into Others, and that a legend entry opens its entries. **Shares are rounded down** (that is the existing rule), so the five named slices plus Others are made to add to 100 (Others is what the five leave). Seen on the emulator with made-up data. **Not done:** a daily or weekly breakdown, income by category, a line for the average, tapping the ring itself (only the legend is tappable), and the ring colours for categories without their own colour are assigned by position, so a category can change colour when the ranking changes.

**Verification:** 801 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 278 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (801 desktop-side tests; an earlier summary of mine said 798, which was an earlier run).

## Slice 60: the home-screen widget
**Why it looked big and empty and did not resize:** the provider declared 4x2 as its *minimum* size with resizing allowed, but the content was drawn at one fixed size (Glance's default), so any extra room was blank and it could not shrink below 4x2. I could not test a real launcher (the emulator's launcher is Android 8); what I did was render the widget's own layout at five sizes and test the rules.
**Now:** a **rounded card** (light and dark, from colour resources) with **this month's spending** in large type (tap it to open the app), a **+ Add** pill, and the quick entries as **one-line chips** (name and amount), in a layout that depends on the size: 2x1 shows the figure and a "+" (no ".00", so five digits fit); 4x1 puts the figure, one chip and "+ Add" in one row; the default 4x2 adds a row of two chips; 4x4 shows up to six chips and the "Added … Undo" line when you have just tapped one. The provider now allows shrinking to 2x1 and growing as far as the launcher permits; the figure follows **Hide amounts** (masked on the home screen too); it refreshes when you leave the app, after a tap on the widget, and hourly (a new month starts at zero). Tests: the layout rules and what is drawn at each size, the month figure (this calendar month only, masked when amounts are hidden), and the existing tap, undo and binding tests; 7 mutations checked. **Not done / not seen:** a real launcher (Samsung One UI may snap sizes differently), a settings screen to choose which quick entries show or their order (they appear in the order the app lists them), and showing a category breakdown. The up-to-six chips come from quick entries that have a fixed amount; one without an amount (a name only) is not shown.

**Verification:** 801 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 283 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files. The first full run failed only at lint (Glance's resource colour provider is restricted API); the text colours now use the public day/night provider with the same values as the colour resources.

## Slice 61: four widget choices, and the App lock
The owner liked all four sizes, so the widget picker now lists **four entries** (standard 4x2, small 2x1, wide 4x1, large 4x4), each the same widget starting at that size; each can still be resized and its layout follows the size it ends up at. The standard one keeps its old identity, so a widget already on the home screen keeps working. Review point (valid): with the **App lock on**, the month's total spending is now masked on the home screen, as with Hide amounts (`QuickAddWidget.monthFigure`, tested for all four combinations; the lock path itself is not exercised end to end because enabling the lock needs a fingerprint prompt). **Verified on Android 17 (it is skipped on the Android 8 test emulator, which does not allow binding):** a widget placed from each of the four entries is found when the widget is updated, so a tap or leaving the app refreshes all of them. **Not verified:** the starting cell sizes were asserted only on Android 12 and later, which the test emulator is not; a `previewLayout` for the picker (Android 12+) is not provided, so the picker may show the generic preview.

**Verification:** 801 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 285 functional device tests (1 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files.

## Slice 62: the widget's amount is hidden until you show it
**Decision from the owner:** not everyone wants spending on the home screen. The month's total is now **hidden by default** (masked like Hide amounts) and a small **eye** beside "Spent this month" shows or hides it with one tap, for every copy of the widget; the choice is remembered (`widget_show_total`). The eye is **not offered** when the app's own Hide amounts or the App lock is on, because those force the total hidden; the app's settings always win over the widget's switch. The quick-entry chips keep their amounts (they are amounts the person typed for their own buttons), still masked under Hide amounts. Tested: default hidden, shown after the switch, the app's setting overriding it, the eye present only when it can be used and worded for what it will do, and the toggle action flipping it twice. **Not seen:** the eye on a real launcher; its tap target is about 20 dp, which is small, so say if it is fiddly. The widget picker's four entries and the update reach were checked as noted in slice 61.

**Verification:** 801 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 290 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (on the Android 8 emulator; the second emulator was off during the run).


## Slice 63: bills found in messages
