**Request from the owner:** when a credit card bill, an electricity bill or any other bill arrives by text or in a payment app's notification, detect it, tell the person, and let them add it to the app.

**What it does.** A message that says something is **due or payable** and carries an amount is recognised as a bill (credit card, electricity, water, gas, mobile, broadband, DTH, insurance premium, loan EMI, or "other"), with its amount, minimum due, due date, card or consumer number where the text has them, and whether it is auto-debited. It is checked before the transaction rules, so a statement is no longer thrown away as "not a transaction". Detection is deliberately strict: it needs a due/payable cue, and it refuses one-time codes, completed payments, spam and spend alerts that merely quote "Due Rs X". The 3 public-sample bills in the capture corpus are found and **none** of the corpus's transaction alerts is (the corpus caught 8 false positives while this was written; all fixed).
- **Notified at once**, once per bill, outside quiet hours (also straight after an incoming text or notification, not at the next hourly check). The lock-screen version of the notice says only that a bill was found; **Hide amounts** keeps figures out of the notice and the home banner. Tapping it opens the new **Bills** page.
- **Bills page** (More, and a banner on Home): *Found in your messages* (Add to my bills, Skip, show the message), *Coming up* (soonest first, amber within 3 days, red when overdue, Mark paid, Remove, Set or change the due date) and *Paid* (with "Not paid after all"). Tracked bills are reminded 3 days ahead, on the day and when overdue, each once.
- **Mark paid** can record the payment (a **transfer from a bank account to the card** for a credit card bill, never spending; an expense in Bills otherwise; the amount can be the total or the minimum or typed), or only mark it paid. Nothing enters the ledger until the person says so.
- **A later payment text settles it:** adding a card payment (at least the minimum, at most the total) or a same-amount debit from To review marks the matching tracked bill paid; a different card, a different amount, a payment more than 30 days before the due date or in another currency does not.
- A reminder of the same bill, even a skipped one, is the same bill (dedupe on kind, sender, card or consumer, due date and amount), a revised amount is a new bill. Bills are in the backup file (found-but-unanswered ones are not restored); Capture health counts "Found as bills".

**Not verified / limits.** Real wordings of utility and card bills were not available: only the three public corpus samples are real, the rest of the test messages are made up. The utility sender headers (BESCOM, MSEDCL, Tata Power, Jio, Airtel, LIC) are provisional guesses and can be extended in capture settings. Notifications from billers' own apps are only captured if that app is in the allowed list. Nothing was tried on a real phone or launcher yet.

**Mutation check:** 5 conditions in the auto-settle rule were broken one at a time (minimum bound, total bound, same card, 30-day window, currency); the first pass left 2 alive (window, currency) and tests were added that kill them.

**Two test problems found and fixed (neither in the bills code).** A second one first: `CategoriesTest` typed into the new-category dialog without waiting for it to open, which failed once on a freshly booted emulator; it now waits. The first: The first full run failed one existing device test (`AccountsUiTest`, a card's billing dates) because it ran on the 15th and the test hard-coded statement day 15: a payment made on the statement day itself belongs to the statement before it (by design, `CardStore.standing` counts payments from the next day), so "paid" never showed. Confirmed by running it with the emulator clock on the 19th (passes) and on the 15th (failed before, passes after the fix); the test now picks a statement day that is not today. The bills code was not involved.

**Verification:** 844 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 304 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (on the Android 8 emulator, the second emulator off). **Honest history of this verification:** it took six full runs. Run 1 failed on the date-dependent test above. Runs 2 and 3 failed because adb returned "Unknown API Level" for the device (a connection problem after `adb root`; the tests never started), and a timing gate measured on a machine/emulator in a bad state. Runs 4 and 5 each failed one *different* existing device test (`CategoriesTest`, `AppearanceUiTest`) and the timing gate at times, none of which failed when run alone 3 times; the same timing gate also failed on the unmodified previous commit on the same aged emulator (7 of 14), and passed 15 of 15 on a freshly booted one. So the timing gate and a few device tests are sensitive to how long the emulator has been up. The run that counts is run 6, on a freshly cold-booted emulator, all green. Nothing was loosened.

## Slice 64: bill detection, four follow-ups from review
1. **Money that moved is never taken for a bill.** The classifier used to read bill wording first, so a text such as "Rs 10,000 credited to your card. Total Amt Due Rs 5,000, Due Date 25-10-26" became a bill and the credit never reached To review. It now reads the transaction first; only a debit that is merely promised ("will be debited", "scheduled debit") is a bill. A test with those texts fails without the change (mutation checked).
2. **One reminder per card, by card identity.** A card whose statement is a tracked bill (due dates within 2 days) is reminded by that bill, not also by its billing dates, but only when the bill is known to be for that card: the account remembered for the bill's issuer and last four digits (`CaptureAccounts.key`), or, with nothing remembered, the only card due in that window. With two or more possible cards and nothing to tell them apart, both reminders are sent (a repeat costs less than a missed one). An earlier version matched on due date alone and could silence the wrong card; review caught it. Tests: one card, two cards with no mapping, two cards with a mapping; mutation checked.
3. **Un-paying is reversible.** A bill paid by an entry that is deleted goes back to tracked, but keeps the link to that entry; when the entry is live again (Undo, a restore, or one arriving by sync) the bill is paid again. Only "Not paid after all" cuts the link. This runs whenever the Bills page or banner loads and in the hourly check, so it does not matter which refresh happens during the Undo window. (A first version released only when no undo was pending, which other refreshes bypassed; review caught that.) The check is one read (`billsOutOfStep`) and writes only when a bill is out of step: the first version wrote on every refresh and the timing gate caught it (the rest of the screen settled in p95 477 ms after a save, limit 50 ms), so the write was removed from the common path. Bills marked paid with no entry stay paid. A device test deletes, sees the bill tracked, undoes, sees it paid, then deletes for good; a host test covers delete, restore and "not paid after all"; mutation checked.
4. **Privacy wording.** Capture settings now say that bill texts from banks and a few billers are read, that the sender is checked first, and that a bill's text is kept only until you add or skip it.

**Verification:** 854 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 305 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (cold-booted Android 8 emulator). The run before the read-gate fix failed one timing test (above); this one passed all 15.

## Slice 65: an interactive tour of the app, after install and after updates, and a video
**Request from the owner:** a walkthrough after install or update that explains each module, skippable; then, as feedback: it should be **interactive, navigating each section**, not plain text; and a **video** of how to use the app.

**The tour.** 19 stops. At each one the app **opens the real screen** (Home, the add form, Search, Reports, Calendar, Capture from texts, To review, Bills, Accounts, Categories, Budget, Repeating expenses, Lent and borrowed, Notifications, Privacy, Your data, the More page, back to Home), dims it, **lights up a real control** where there is one (this month's total on Home, the amount field in the add form; the registry is `ui/TourSpots.kt`, a screen marks a control with `Modifier.tourSpot(name)`), and explains the part in a card that moves to the other half of the screen from the lit control: what it is for, how to use it, and where to find it later.
- **Skip the tour** is in the card on every stop and ends it at once, returning to Home; the system Back button does the same. **Back** and **Next** move between stops (each one opens its screen again). **Try it** (on stops about a screen) ends the tour and leaves you on that screen to use it. A checkbox, **Do not show this tour after updates**, is on the first and last stop.
- **First install:** every stop. **After an update:** only the stops added since the version last finished or skipped, headed "What is new", with "See the whole tour" when that is fewer than all. Each stop carries the version that added it (`Guide.VERSION`, `GuideStep.since`), so adding stops later only needs a version bump. **Any time:** More, Help, How to use this app (all stops, no update checkbox, records nothing).
- Not shown to someone who wipes their ledger and starts over, nor over the App lock screen. Someone updating from before the tour existed has never seen it, so they get all of it once.
- Content and rules: `core/domain/Guide.kt` (shared, so iOS can reuse it); the layer: `ui/GuideScreen.kt`; opening and closing screens: `MainActivity.kt`; what was seen: `guide_seen` and `guide_off` in settings.

**Tests and mutation checks.** Host: the rule for what to offer (first run, update, off switch, version bumps with and without new stops). Device (9): a walk through all 19 stops checking that each shows its own real screen and none of the others and that the lit controls are registered; Skip on the first stop and from inside a screen (returns to Home); Try it; Back; update with and without the off choice; not shown after a wipe or when seen; More starts it from Home and records nothing. Broken in turn, each failed a test: the plan rule, skip not recording, "never again" not stored, a manual close recording, a stop not closing the previous screen, ending not returning home, the More page not being driven. One survived at first (the manual-close test began with the version already recorded) and the test was changed.

**Found while building it.** The first design was a pager of text pages; the owner asked for the interactive one and it replaced it. The dialog window dims what is behind it by itself, which greyed the lit window until that was switched off. The More page's new Help group had pushed "Your data" below the fold and failed 19 existing tests; it is now the last group.

**The video shows the app in real use, not the tour.** `tools/video/make_video.py` records 19 short parts on the emulator, one per part of the app, on the same **made-up** ledger. In each, the app is really used while a narrator speaks: adding an expense, income and a transfer; editing and swiping one away, then Undo; searching and filtering; a report drilled into a category; the calendar; Capture from texts; accepting a bank text in To review; finding a bill, adding it and marking it paid as a transfer to the card; adding an account and setting a card's billing dates; a category; a monthly budget limit; a repeating expense posted; money lent and a repayment; reminders; Hide amounts, App lock and the privacy page; the Your data page and a passphrase; and appearance. Narration is macOS `say` with an Indian English voice (`VOICE` to change it); a short title is spoken while the app is taken to each part so there is no silent navigation, and each part's own lines begin once its screen is up.
- How: `DemoVideoTest.kt.txt` is copied into the instrumentation tests, run once, and removed again; each part starts `screenrecord` itself, uses the app with Compose test actions, waits as long as each line lasts and logs when each began (device clock); the script lays each clip on its recording and joins the parts behind a title card. If a part misses a wait on a busy machine, only that part is recorded again (twice at most) and it says so. `ASSEMBLE_ONLY=1` rebuilds from recordings already on the device. Needs an emulator, ffmpeg and about 20 minutes; run it again after the app changes.
- Result: about 6 minutes 25 seconds, 540x1170, with the transcript beside it. Checked: a frame from each part, and frames just after narration begins for eight parts, show the right screen; no narration clip overlaps another; the audio is not silent or clipping (mean -21.6 dB, peak -3.5 dB).
- **Not checked:** I cannot hear it, so the voice, pacing and pronunciation (E M I and C S V are spelled out so they are read as letters) are unjudged. The part about bank-text capture shows the settings screen and a seeded text, because the emulator cannot grant SMS permission and receive a real message. App lock shows the page, not turning it on (the emulator has no screen lock). Export, backup and import open system file pickers, so only the page and a typed passphrase are shown. The narration is placed by a fixed estimate (`AUDIO_LEAD`, half a second). Paying a bill shows the keyboard over the confirm button for a moment.

**Opening the app to do something never lands in the tour.** Launched by the widget, the Quick Settings tile, the launcher shortcut or a bill notification, the tour is held back until a plain launch (and one already showing goes away, unrecorded). Found by review; three device tests (launch by add, launch by bills, an intent while the tour is showing), each failing when the deferral is removed.

**Not verified:** the text was written from what the app does today and read by me, not by anyone else; nobody has gone through the tour on a real phone, and its wording has not been tried on a real first-time user. Hide amounts was not tried together with the tour.

**Verification:** 866 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 317 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (cold-booted Android 8 emulator). **History:** it took many runs, and none of the failures was in the tour. Early runs failed on the Help group pushing Your data below the fold (fixed), then on a machine short of memory (swap nearly full): adb probe timeouts, 'No compatible devices', and single device tests missing a 10-second wait (CategoriesTest, DataTest, AddExpenseFlowTest) that passed alone and on the next run; the committed previous version also failed the timing gate in that state. `tools/verify.sh` step 1 is now two Gradle runs (build, then device tests) so the device probe cannot time out alongside the compile; the same tasks run and no gate was loosened. After about 14 GB of disk was freed (an unused emulator, scratch files, build outputs) one run failed AddExpenseFlowTest once (3 of 3 alone), and this run passed everything.

## Slice 66: accounts as cards, with a network, a tier and the last four digits
**Request from the owner:** show each account as a card with its balance, on the home screen and the accounts screen; for a credit card, show a card look by its network and tier; add the last four digits so texts and bills can find their account. How other apps (CRED and the like) source card images was asked; I found no public account of it, so it is not claimed here. **Decision:** our own drawn designs, not bank or network artwork (those are the issuers' copyright and the networks' trademarks), with a real-card feel.
- **Tiles.** Every account is a rounded card with a gradient: a colour each for bank, cash, wallet and loan; for a credit card a colour family per network (Visa, Mastercard, RuPay, American Express, Other) and a finish per tier (Classic, Platinum, and Premium, a shared near-black with a gold edge). The network is named in type or drawn as plain shapes (two overlapping circles for Mastercard); no logo is used. The accounts page shows full tiles (name, kind, last four digits, balance, what is due on a card); Home's strip shows small ones. Both read as one line to a screen reader and the tests ("Bank -₹400.00"); balances still mask under Hide amounts. Accounts are listed money first (bank, cash, wallet), then cards, then loans, by name within each.
- **The form** has an optional **last 4 digits** field for any account (digits only, at most four) and, for a credit card, network and tier chips (tap again to clear). Network and tier belong to a credit card: switching an account away from a card drops them.
- **Digits find the account.** A bill's card digits pick the card account in the pay dialog; a bank text's digits (a card when the text says card) pick the suggested account in To review; a tracked card bill silences only the card that carries its digits (before, the issuer-and-digits memory or "the only card due"). Several accounts with the same digits (a bank account and a card sharing 4821) are never guessed: a card is preferred only for a bill or card text, and only when it is the one card; otherwise the person is asked. What was learned from past choices still applies when nothing carries the digits.
- **Storage:** `account_look` (account, network, tier, last4) with logged changes like card terms, in the schema upgrade, in the backup file (`accountLooks`; restored only for an existing account with no look; unknown names and bad digits dropped) and in docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md. Tour: version 2 adds an "Account cards" stop; people who saw the first tour get only that one as "What is new".
- **Tests:** host (the design table, digit parsing and picking, the store, a backup round trip, the reminder de-duplication by digits) and six device tests (creating a card through the form, clearing a network, digits typed one key at a time, bank accounts never keeping a network, the order, a bill choosing its card, a text choosing its account) plus the update-path tour test. Mutation checked: the archived filter, the logging, the digit use in reminders, bills, texts, the network rule and the order each broke a test.
- **Found by the first full run (all fixed).** (1) The small tile's screen-reader label held the raw balance, so with Hide amounts on an amount was still exposed (`PrivacyUiTest` caught it); the label is now masked like every other amount. (2) The taller tiles pushed the welcome card's Skip button off the screen on a first launch (a review pointed out my first fix, making the card scroll and the test scroll, only hid that). The two empty tiles of a brand-new ledger now wait behind the welcome card and appear with the first entry or on Skip; a test asserts Skip is displayed with no scrolling and another that the strip comes back (both fail without the change). The card still scrolls on a short screen, the small tile is 100dp, and the two balance tests start from a dismissed welcome. The digits field takes a paste such as "XX 7710" or "**** 4821" (its last four digits) and ignores a fifth key typed after four. Also confirmed: the release APK contains the feature (the table name and the new form and tour text are in its dex; its size equals earlier builds, which I cannot explain). (3) My new form fields pushed "Set billing dates" down behind the keyboard; billing dates now come first, then network and tier, then the digits.
- **Not verified / limits.** Nobody has looked at the tiles on a real phone. No tile for a debit card (a bank account shows only its digits). A card's tile does not yet take a photo of the real card (offered, not built). Accounts created before this have no network, tier or digits until edited. The video was recorded before this change.

**Verification:** 879 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 324 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (a cold-booted Android 8 emulator). **History:** the first full run failed 5 device tests and the timing gate; three were my changes (the masked chip label, the welcome Skip, the form order; fixed above) and the rest were slow waits that passed when rerun. The next run passed the device tests but the timing test `RefreshBudgetTest` missed (126 ms against 50): run alone it gave 108 to 121 ms on this tree and 119 to 132 ms on the committed previous version on the same emulator, so the tiles are not slower than what they replaced and the emulator, after a long suite, was. `tools/verify.sh` now restarts the emulator before the timing gate (the gate and its limits are unchanged), and the run after that passed everything.

**Follow-up to slice 66, after review.** The two empty account tiles of a brand-new ledger now wait behind the welcome card (Skip is on screen again, asserted, with a test that the strip returns); a pasted last-four value such as "XX 7710" keeps its digits; the balance tests start from a dismissed welcome. `CategoriesTest` (the new-category chip) had failed in about a third of full runs, before and after the cards, because the form moves as the keyboard opens and a single tap can miss the chip; it now taps until the dialog is there (it also passes alone). **Verification:** 879 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 326 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (cold-booted emulator, quiet machine). Between the first commit of this slice and this one, several full runs failed only the timing gate or one slow wait while the Mac was short of memory (swap 6 of 7 GB used); the committed previous version failed the gate identically then, and it passed once memory was free.

## Slice 67: one motion language, a glass tab bar, folding home, card lists, a wallet stack for accounts

**Request from the owner:** make the whole app fluid, with the smooth sliding of the Apple TV focus and, if possible, liquid glass; a way to turn animations on and off; the top of home smaller while the entries scroll; a more visual transaction list; accounts as an Apple-Wallet-style stack on the accounts screen; the backup reminder moved off home.

- **Motion (`Motion.kt`).** Three durations, two curves, a spring helper and haptics for save, undo, limit, paid and tick, all scaled by one setting: **Appearance > Motion: Off, Quick, Relaxed (default, 1.8x), Slow (2.8x)**. Off is the old Reduce motion; the phone's Remove animations setting also forces it. Stored as `motion_speed` and `reduce_motion`.
- **Screen host (`ScreenHost.kt`).** The 24 on/off flags became one keyed `AnimatedContent`: the add form glides up from the bottom, a tapped row grows into its editor (container transform), tabs crossfade, other screens come forward. A leaving screen is hidden from accessibility and its Back handler stands down (`BackHandler` wrapper).
- **Glass bar.** Home, Search, Reports and More share one bar; a lit, translucent pane glides under the current tab on a spring and stretches while it travels. It hides while the keyboard is up. It is not true liquid glass (no blur or refraction of what is behind).
- **Home.** A 14-day spending line (drag to read a day), a month total that rolls to its new value (semantics carry the final text at once), an animated budget bar, and a top that folds away while the entries scroll up and returns at the top of the list (`HomeCollapse.kt`). The backup reminder is now a red dot on More and a card at the top of the More page.
  - *Superseded in 0.7.0: Home now scrolls as one page; HeaderFold removed (`HomeCollapse.kt` is gone). See Slice 88.*
- **Add form.** An optional number pad, the amount read back in Indian words (`AmountWords`), a sheet look.
- **Lists.** Entries are rounded cards with a category tile on Home, Search, report drill-down, lent and borrowed, and repeating items (`EntryListStyle.kt`). Categories, History, the inbox and the bills page keep the older flat look.
- **Accounts.** The cards stack like a wallet and spread on a tap (`WalletStack`); Home keeps its strip.
- **Tests.** 17 new device tests (`MotionUiTest`, a wallet-stack test) plus `AmountWordsTest`; the onboarding tests now look for the dot on More and open More for the card.
- **verify.sh.** Step 2 now builds the test apps first and lets the machine settle before measuring; without that, recompiling right before the first measurement made search and save timings 2-3x slower in the script while the same tests passed alone. The gate and its limits are unchanged.
- **Not verified / limits.** Nothing was checked on a real phone; the videos are emulator captures one frame at a time. No in-app back-gesture preview, no real blur, no baseline profile (first-launch stutter on a release build is untested).

**Verification:** 917 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 339 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (a cold-booted Android 8 emulator). **History:** earlier runs of this work failed on lint (an indent and an unused lambda value), on a test written for the faster glide, and twice on the timing gate for the reason above.

## Slice 68: similar entries are offered the same category, and captured texts start from history

**Request from the owner:** when a category is chosen for a transaction, find similar earlier ones (an iCloud subscription every month, or a bank statement just imported) and suggest the same category; the person reviews and accepts all, or goes one by one. Texts and notifications that look similar should be suggested a category from history. The person always decides.

- **Matching (`SimilarEntries`, pure and tested).** Two names match when their keys are equal (the existing `merchantKey`: case, punctuation and a UPI handle ignored) or one holds the other and is at least four characters ("iCloud" finds "Apple iCloud+ 50GB"; "Ola" never finds "Coca Cola Stall"). Entries already in the category, the entry just filed, and entries with no single category (splits) are never offered.
- **Finding candidates.** A new query `similarCandidates` reads the full-text index for the name's words (at most four, three letters or more) and returns up to 300 recent expenses or incomes with their one category; no schema change.
- **When.** 1.2 seconds after a save of an expense or income with a name (a second save replaces the look), in the background, so the save path is unchanged. It runs for new entries and for edits, so tagging one entry of an imported statement offers the rest.
- **The offer (`SimilarDialog`).** "Same as iCloud? 11 earlier entries look like this one and are filed elsewhere (Other 9, Fun 2). File them under Subscriptions too?" with **Move all 11**, **Review one by one** (one entry at a time: Move or Skip, "Move all the rest", "Done"), **Not now** (quiet for this name and category until the app restarts) and **Stop asking about this** (stored as `similar_off:<key>`). Nothing changes until the person agrees; the move is one change that Undo takes back (it goes through the existing bulk edit).
- **Captured texts and notifications.** A new suggestion source `HISTORY` (the category most earlier entries of that payee are in; a tie goes to the newer) sits below the person's rules and what they chose last time, and above the starter list. To review shows it as "(your earlier entries)".
- **Tests.** Host: matching, ordering, splits, ties, search words, the history order. Device (`SimilarUiTest`, 5): move all moves only the matches and not another payee; review one by one moves only the accepted ones, newest first; Not now changes nothing and does not ask again; no offer when all are already there; a captured text starts from history. Mutation checks: dropping the "not already in the category" filter fails the host test; moving only one id fails two device tests.
- **Not verified / limits.** Nothing was tried on a real phone or on a real imported statement. Matching is by name only (not amount), so "Amazon" will offer every Amazon entry. The offer is not shown for transfers, loans or split entries, and not after a bulk edit or an inbox accept.

- **Follow-up: turning "Stop asking" back on.** The name is now kept as written (`similar_off:<key>` holds it), and Categories ends with a list **Suggestions turned off** with an **Ask again** button for each; it also clears this session's quiet. Tests: the stop, the list entry, ask again, and the offer returning. The owner chose to keep matching by name only (no amounts).

**Verification:** 929 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 345 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (a cold-booted Android 8 emulator).

## Slice 69: income and expense categories, and bulk category change from search

**Request from the owner:** (1) in search, select transactions and set a category for all of them; (2) categories for income, kept apart from expense categories.

- **Category kinds.** A category is for `EXPENSE`, `INCOME` or `BOTH` (`CategoryKind`; BOTH exists only for categories made before this that an income already used, found by the upgrade). New column `category.kind` (upgrade in `SchemaUpgrade`, database snapshot regenerated), in the sync log, in the backup (`kind`, default EXPENSE for an older file). A subcategory always takes its parent's kind. Five income categories are created once (Salary, Interest, Gifts received, Refunds and cashback, Other income; fixed ids `income:...`, not logged, a marker stops them returning after a delete). Changing a top-level category's kind is refused while something of the dropped kind uses it; an expense category cannot merge into an income one.
- **Categories screen.** Tabs **Expenses | Income**; a new category takes the tab's kind; the edit screen has Expenses / Income / Both chips for a top-level category and `Follows <parent>` for a subcategory, with the refusal shown in place; suggested categories only on the Expenses tab.
- **Pickers.** The add form, the split editor, repeating items, budgets, the inbox and capture rules offer only categories that serve what is being chosen; switching an entry between Expense and Income swaps the category to a valid one (the last used income category is remembered as `last_income_category`); a category created from the income form is an income category. A captured text's suggestions (rules, learned, history, starter list) are limited to expense categories.
- **Search.** Under the count, **Select all N** (and **Clear selection**); the selection now clears when the search changes and never holds an entry that is no longer listed. The bulk bar has two lines (count and Cancel, then the actions) with a prominent **Set category** that opens a picker grouped into Expense categories / Income categories (a third group only if legacy BOTH categories exist), with a search box over 12 entries. Kind is enforced in `EntryActions.bulkSetCategory`: an income is never given an expense category or the reverse; entries skipped are counted and named in the notice ("Changed the category of 9 entries. 2 skipped: ..."); Undo restores the changed ones. The home list shares the same bar.
- **Found on the way (all fixed).** (1) The tab bar's "hide while the keyboard is up" from slice 67 never worked: the window is resized for the keyboard, so the insets API reported nothing. A layout-based check (`rememberKeyboardOpen`) replaces it; Search with the keyboard up now shows the bar only when the keyboard is away. (2) A one-line bulk bar pushed Cancel off a narrow screen. (3) "Ask again" sat under the add-category button. (4) The verify script's migration check failed until the schema snapshot was regenerated.
- **Tests.** Host: kinds on create, subcategories, kind change refusals, merge across kinds, the seeded defaults. Device: `CategoryKindsUiTest` (5), `IncomeCategoriesUiTest` (8), `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest` (5); existing category, bulk-edit, motion and similar-entry tests updated for the tabs, the scrolling edit screen, the two-line bar and the keyboard.
- **Not verified / limits.** Nothing was tried on a real phone. An older backup restores its categories as expense categories, so income entries already in it keep their category but the income picker will not offer an expense-kind category. Reports still group income and expenses as before (no income-by-category view). Income categories have no suggested catalogue.

**Verification:** 932 host/JVM tests (both targets counted), 363 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests; verified in two steps by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files (a cold-booted Android 8 emulator). **History:** the first full run failed on the schema migration check and five device tests (listed above); the second passed.

## Slice 70: the first iPhone build

**Request from the owner:** a build to install on the iPhone and start testing.

- **Shared core on iOS.** `core:domain`, `core:parsers` and `core:storage` now compile for iOS (Kotlin/Native). Four JVM-only calls were replaced by common ones (`toSortedMap`, `String.format`, `HashMap.merge`, `@Volatile`, a deprecated `String(CharArray)`); behaviour is unchanged on Android.
- **Facade (`core/facade`).** `AppCore`: accounts and balances, categories by kind, add (with amount parsing and reasons), recent entries, month spending, the similar-entries offer and move, delete. Four host tests; it is built into `ExpensesCore.xcframework` with a native SQLite driver.
- **App (`iosApp/`).** SwiftUI: Home (month spend, balances, entries by day, swipe to delete), Add (expense or income, category chips, account), Categories (expense / income), the similar-entries sheet. Four XCUITests pass on the iPhone 17 simulator (add shows row and total; a bad amount is explained; income and expense categories are separate; similar entries are offered and reviewed one by one). I looked at the home and add screens in a simulator screenshot.
- **Signed device build.** Release build signed with the Apple Development identity and a team profile that includes the owner's registered iPhone; packaged as `~/Desktop/Expenses-iOS-0.1.0-test.ipa`. Steps in `docs/IOS.md`.
- **Not verified / limits.** Not installed on a real iPhone (none was connected); the profile expires after 7 days; the feature set is small (see `docs/IOS.md`); no database key beyond iOS Data Protection.

**Verification (slice 70):** 936 host/JVM tests, 363 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests on a cold-booted Android 8 emulator, plus 4 iPhone UI tests on the simulator. **History:** the script's own timing step failed twice (8 and 9 of 15) with Xcode and the iOS simulator running on the same Mac; the previous commit failed the same tests in the same state, and after quitting Xcode and cold-booting the emulator the gate passed 15 of 15 on this tree, so the script's PASS line was not obtained in one run: the three parts above were each observed passing on this tree.

## Slice 71 (stage 1 of 3): the UI and ViewModel move into a shared Compose Multiplatform module

**Decision:** the iPhone app gets every screen by sharing the Android app's Compose UI and ViewModel through Compose Multiplatform (same `androidx.compose.*` packages), not by rewriting the screens in SwiftUI. The small SwiftUI app from slice 70 stays until the shared one replaces it.

- New module `:shared` (android library + iosArm64/iosSimulatorArm64): all of `app.expenses.ui`, `LedgerViewModel` (now a plain `ViewModel(container, platform)`), `EntryActions`, `AppContainer(platform)`, `DataTransfer` (the old `DataActions` logic), `Formatting`, setting keys.
- `Platform` (shared interface) is everything the shared code needs from the OS: database, files at String URIs, attachments, backup cipher, auto-export, capture and notification hooks, app lock, statement decoding, and capability flags; `AndroidPlatform` implements it with the existing Android code; `IosPlatform` has a first skeleton. UI hooks (`rememberFeel`, `rememberFilePickers`, `PlatformBackHandler`, `rememberKeyboardOpen`, `systemAnimationsOff`, ...) are `expect`/`actual`.
- Android behaviour unchanged; the shared module compiles for Android and iOS. Date text is now English-only (it followed the phone's locale before).
- Tests: tests that passed a `Uri` now pass its string; `isDatabaseClosed` and the timing counters are public.

**Verification:** 936 host/JVM tests, 363 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests, by tools/verify.sh from deleted result files on a cold-booted emulator (the first attempt ran with no emulator and was discarded).

**Next:** stage 2 moves the app's Compose root (`MainActivity`'s screen switching) into shared code; stage 3 is the iOS host (Xcode project hosting the shared UI, iOS platform services: database, files, share, Face ID lock) and testing on the simulator.

## Slice 71 (stages 2 and 3): the shared root and the iPhone app with every screen

- **Stage 2.** `MainActivity`'s Compose root moved into shared code as `ExpensesRoot(viewModel, host: RootHost)` (`AppRoot.kt`, `AppLockPort.kt`); the activity keeps intent actions, window bars, widget refresh and the lock. Screen keys, order and testTags unchanged; Android tests needed no edits.
- **Stage 3.** `IosPlatform` (native SQLite under Data Protection, files, attachments, statements, `.xlsx`), `IosAppLock` (Face ID / Touch ID / passcode), `IosBackupCipher` (the Android backup layout, with CryptoKit/CommonCrypto supplying only PBKDF2 and AES-GCM from Swift), `MainViewController`, and a thin Swift host (`iosApp/`: project.yml, Info.plist, PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy, a generated 1024 icon). The earlier SwiftUI screens and `core/facade` were removed. Cross-platform backups: a file made by the Android steps opens on the iPhone code and the other way round (`tools/backup_interop`). 3 unit tests and 8 simulator UI tests pass; the signed Release build installs on the owner's iPhone (it then needs Developer Mode on and the profile trusted before it will open).
- **Not on iPhone:** SMS and notification capture, notifications, auto-export, widget/tile/shortcuts, a separate database key (see `docs/IOS.md`).
- **Not verified:** nothing has been seen on a real iPhone; Hide amounts, Budgets, a real statement import, backup restore, attachments and the real Face ID prompt were not exercised on the simulator.

**Verification (Android):** 932 host/JVM tests pass. The 363 functional device tests passed 362 and 361 across runs; the one that fails differs from run to run (`AccountsUiTest.anUnusedAccountCanBeDeleted...` failed 1 of 3 alone; `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...` once) and both passed when re-run, so they are flaky under load, not caused by this change (the first of them failed the same way before this slice). **The timing gate did not pass in the script on this host:** with 98% disk and 2.3 of 3 GB swap in use it failed 7 to 9 of 15 tests; the previous verified commit (e0cfaae) failed 9 of 15 in the same state, so this is the machine, not the code. The previous commit passed 15 of 15 earlier on a quieter host; this change touches no database code. Re-run the gate on a quiet machine before the Play/App Store release.

## Slice 72: message capture and reminders on the iPhone (Android untouched)

**Request from the owner:** the iPhone cannot read texts, so bank-SMS capture needs an alternative; reminders and the other missing iPhone pieces; nothing may change on Android.

- **Why not the same as Android.** iOS gives apps no access to messages or other apps' notifications. The two routes iOS allows are built: a **Shortcuts automation** ("When I get a message" containing debited/credited/Rs./INR, run immediately, action **Capture bank message**) feeding the app through an App Intent that works when the app is closed, and a **Paste** box on To review. Both run the same classifier and review inbox as Android (`MessageIngest`, a common copy of `Capture.ingest`; Android's own code is not touched). A bank message with no sender (paste) is accepted only when its words identify a bank (`PasteRules`). Words of anything that is not a transaction or bill are discarded; only a count is kept, as on Android. The Capture screen on iPhone shows the step-by-step setup and an Open Shortcuts button; **Add expense** is also an App Intent (Siri, Action button, Shortcuts).
- **Reminders.** Local notifications (no server): bills and cards due, scheduled ahead when the app is open (`NotificationService.plannedFor`, additive) plus a background refresh task and a check when the app opens; permission requested when reminders are turned on. Android's hourly work is unchanged.
- **Android safety.** Nothing under `androidApp/` changed. Shared changes are behind `Platform.supportsShortcutsCapture` / `notificationTimingNote` / paste hooks that default to false/null and that `AndroidPlatform` does not override.
- **Install guide.** `docs/ANDROID_INSTALL.md`: what testers see (unknown sources, Play Protect, restricted settings), what cannot be skipped and why (Android's hard-restricted permissions), the Play route without a D-U-N-S number, and Android developer verification (free limited-distribution account).
- **Not verified.** Nothing seen on the real iPhone (it was unreachable); background refresh timing and tapping a notification could not be tested on the simulator; a real Messages automation has not been run.

**Verification:** 937 host/JVM tests pass; iPhone simulator: 11 unit tests and 17 UI tests pass (capture, paste, reminders, every More screen). Android device tests: 363 run; each full run had 1 to 4 failures in tests that pass when re-run (CategoriesTest 3 of 3 alone, Loans, SuggestedCategories, SearchBulkCategory, Accounts), and the Loans test also failed 1 of 2 on the previous commit in the same state, so they are load flakes. **The timing gate did not pass:** with swap at 4.5 of 6 GB and 99% disk it failed 7 of 15, on a cold-booted emulator and in the script; the previous commit failed 9 of 15 in a similar state. Not re-measured on a quiet machine: do that before the next Android release.

## Slice 73: the Google Play build and the privacy policy

- **Play build type** (`play`, like `release` plus the same signing, `./gradlew :androidApp:bundlePlay` -> `.aab`): `src/play/AndroidManifest.xml` removes `RECEIVE_SMS`, the telephony feature and `SmsReceiver`, because Play allows SMS permissions only for default SMS apps and a short exception list. `BuildConfig.SMS_CAPTURE` (true everywhere else) feeds `AndroidPlatform.supportsSmsCapture`; where it is false the Capture screen offers only payment-app notifications and is called "Capture from payment apps" (screen and More tile). Checked in the built files: the Play APK/AAB manifests have no SMS permission or receiver; the normal release APK still has both.
- **Docs:** `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` and a hostable `docs/site/privacy.html` (contact email left as `[YOUR CONTACT EMAIL]`), `docs/PLAY_LISTING.md` (listing text, Data safety and declarations answers, release steps, limits).
- **Not verified:** the Play build was not run on a device (instrumented tests run the debug build); whether Google accepts the notification listener for an expense tracker is unconfirmed; help text elsewhere still says "texts" in the Play build.

**Verification:** 937 host/JVM tests, 363 functional device tests (2 skipped) and 15 timing-gate tests, by tools/verify.sh in one run from deleted result files on a cold-booted emulator: PASS.

## Slice 74: the keyboard no longer covers the screen, and Done puts it away

**Reported by the owner (on a real phone):** the keyboard stays on top of the screen, and after a search the keyboard must be dismissed by tapping something.

- **Cause.** The app targets Android 16 (API 36): from Android 15 the window is not resized for the keyboard (`adjustResize` is ignored under enforced edge-to-edge), and Material's `Scaffold` does not pad for the keyboard, so it was drawn over the bottom of the screen. The API 26 emulator still resizes, which is why this had looked fine there.
- **Fix.** The shared root takes the keyboard's height as padding (`Modifier.imePadding()` on the root surface, also on iPhone through the existing wrapper), so every screen with a text field keeps its bottom bar and buttons above the keyboard. On Search, **Done** (the keyboard's action key, previously Search, which did nothing) clears focus and hides the keyboard; so do choosing entries and scrolling the results.
- **Test.** `doneOnTheKeyboardPutsItAwayAndLeavesTheResultsShown` reads the system's input-method state (`dumpsys input_method`) and fails if the keyboard stays. Order matters: hiding the keyboard before clearing focus left it showing on Android 8; clear focus first, then hide.
- **Not verified:** on a real Android 15 or 16 phone (the emulator is Android 8, where the window already resized, so the new padding is untested where it matters); on iPhone beyond compiling.
- Also made `CategoriesTest.aDuplicateNameIsRefused...` wait for the dialog before typing; it still fails about 1 run in 3 on this emulator (it failed the same way before this slice), cause not found.

**Verification:** 937 host/JVM tests pass; the 364 device tests had one failure (the Categories test above, which passes on re-run); the timing gate failed 8 of 15 with the host at load 7 and swap at 2.6 of 4 GB, not re-run on a quiet machine. The change touches no database code.

## Slice 75: a package name of its own for Google Play

`app.expenses` is already taken on Play. The `play` build type now gets the application id `io.github.chandu007.expenses` (`androidComponents.onVariants`), and the code namespace stays `app.expenses`. The directly installed build keeps `app.expenses` so testers keep their data. Two places hardcoded the old name and were fixed: the home-screen widget's intents (now `BuildConfig.APPLICATION_ID`) and the launcher shortcut (`src/play/res/xml/shortcuts.xml` overrides the target package for the Play build). Checked in the built files: the Play APK/AAB has the new package, file authority `io.github.chandu007.expenses.files`, the shortcut targets it, and no SMS permission; the release APK is unchanged.
**Not verified:** the Play build on a device (the Android emulator could not start: the Mac's disk was full), and no device tests were run after this change (the debug and release builds are unchanged apart from the widget intents using the same package value); no full verification was run.

## Slice 76: app lock crash, Back from More, a new More page, backup file messages (version 0.1.1)

**Reported by the owner (Play build on a real phone):** (1) turning on the app lock crashed the app; (2) Back from a screen opened in More went to Home, not to More; (3) restoring a backup made by the direct-install build said "This is not a backup file from this app."

- **Lock crash, cause.** `MainActivity` gave the app's view-model factory as its *default* factory, and that factory only knows `LedgerViewModel`. The system fingerprint/PIN prompt (`androidx.biometric`) asks the activity for its own view model, so the call failed with `No initializer set for given class androidx.biometric.BiometricViewModel`. It came in with slice 71 and was hidden because every test used a fake prompt. **Fix:** the factory builds `LedgerViewModel` and hands every other class to the activity's usual factory. **Test:** `MoreMenuUiTest.theFingerprintPromptCanBeBuiltOnTheMainActivity` builds the real prompt and another view model on the real activity; against the old factory it fails with exactly the owner's error, against the fix it passes. Also run by hand on the minified Play build with a PIN set: switch on, system PIN prompt, lock on, PIN asked again on reopening, no crash.
- **Back from More.** Whether More is open was a local flag of the home screen and every tile closed it before opening its screen. It is now kept in `AppRoot` and a tile no longer closes it: Back from a screen opened in More returns to More, Back from More returns to Home. Checked by `MoreMenuUiTest` (Accounts, Calendar, Appearance, Your data) and by hand on the Play build. Opening More also lets go of the Undo bar of a just-saved entry, which otherwise sat over the foot of the page.
- **New More page** (`ui/MoreMenu.kt`, replacing the tile grid in `HomeStyle.kt`): one list of rounded cards per group (Inbox and bills, Money, Capture, Make it yours, Privacy and data, Help), each row a coloured icon, a title and a live line (account count and net, category counts, ...), Hide amounts as a switch, a search box that lists only matching rows, big "To review" and "Bills" cards with a count while something waits (then not repeated as rows), and the backup reminder as one slim bar. Every test tag is unchanged. Approved by the owner from the mock-up `Expenses-More-menu-proposal.png`.
- **Backup file messages.** `BackupFile.decode` now ignores a byte-order mark, blank space in front and a newline behind (what email, chat and drive copies can add), and says what is wrong: empty file, a zip/Excel file, a web page (a blocked or sign-in download), or a file from another format, always with the size and first bytes in hex and never the file's words. The format itself is unchanged and old files still read. **The owner's failure could not be reproduced:** unencrypted and passphrase-encrypted backups from the direct build restored on the Play build on the emulator, and a file written by the commit before slice 71 restored too. The new message will say what the owner's file actually is; the file or a description of how it was copied to the phone is still needed.
- **Each area looks like itself.** The five groups of the More page (Money blue, Capture teal, Make it yours purple, Privacy and data green, Help amber) are now an `Area` (`ui/AreaBar.kt`), and every screen opened from More uses `AreaTopBar`: the same coloured icon tile as its More row beside the title, and a wash of that colour behind the bar. Same Back button and tags as before. A long title ("Privacy: what this app can see") takes a smaller size instead of being cut off. Search, Reports, the entry form and the History/Budget-style detail screens keep the plain bar (Search awaits the owner's mock-up approval).
- **Screens looked at** (emulator, Android 8, empty ledger, light and dark): the More page and To review, Bills, Accounts, Categories, Repeating expenses, Calendar, Lent and borrowed, Capture, Notifications, Appearance, Customise home, Currency, App lock, Privacy, Your data, and Reports and Search in dark. **Not looked at:** the guide tour screens beyond the first, Budget, Category rules, How capture is doing, Import, History, the entry form and the Search results (no data was loaded, so lists with entries were not seen with the new bars).
- **Tests changed.** The More page is taller than the old grid, so tests now scroll to a row before tapping it, and a shared `openMore()` / `closeMore()` (`MoreTestSupport.kt`) replaces tapping the More button, which now toggles the page. Tests that leave a screen and expect Home call `closeMore()`.
- **Not verified:** the iPhone app (Xcode was removed from the Mac, so nothing for iOS was built or run; the shared More page compiles for Android only here); Android 15/16 (the emulator is Android 8); the Play build on a real phone beyond the lock and Back checks above. The Search screen was not changed: a mock-up awaits the owner's approval.

**Verification (after the area bars):** the functional suite ran 368 tests with 2 failures (`EditDeleteTest.swipingARowDeletesItAndUndoBringsItBack`, "failed to inject touch input", and `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest.anAllIncomeSelection...`); both pass when run on their own, and the Search test was made to wait for its selection before the second tap (it had tapped too early; it passed 3 of 3 alone before and after). `LoansUiTest.borrowing...` failed in a later batch and passes 3 of 3 alone. **Earlier, before the area bars:** 939 host/JVM tests pass. Device: the functional suite ran 368 tests with 3 failures (`LoansUiTest.borrowing...`, `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest.anAllIncomeSelection...`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...`), all 11 tests of those three classes pass when re-run alone, the same flaky classes as before. **The timing gate FAILS on this Mac: 9 of 15 on a cold-booted emulator, twice.** The commit before this slice (stashed and run the same way) also fails 9 of 15 with the same kind of numbers (e.g. category totals 317 ms vs 250 ms, search 630 ms vs 200 ms), and those tests read the database only, which this slice does not touch; so it is this machine today (after deleting caches and Xcode, rebuilt indexes, Docker running), not this change. It passed 15/15 in slice 73 on a quiet machine. `tools/verify.sh` therefore reports FAIL; it has not been loosened.
- **iPhone UI tests edited, not run (no Xcode):** `ExpensesUITests.swift` now expects `more_page` (not `add`) after `categories_back` and `data_back`, as Back now returns to More on both platforms.

## Slice 77: the Search screen redesign (version 0.1.2)

Approved by the owner from the mock-up `Expenses-Search-proposal.png`. `ui/SearchScreen.kt`, with small additions in the view model.

- **Search bar:** one rounded bar with a magnifier and a clear button (`search_clear`); Done on the keyboard puts the keyboard away and keeps the words as a recent search.
- **Filters:** category and tag chips show a drop-down arrow, or a cross while they filter; the date chips show a cross while on; "Save this search" is a chip with a star at the front of the dates row; saved searches are starred chips. The amount boxes stay on the screen (rounder), they were not folded into a chip.
- **Before typing:** the hint (`search_hint`), **Recent searches** (the last six words, newest first, no repeats, one-letter words not kept; a cross each and Clear; kept in the `recent_searches` setting; `rememberSearch`/`forgetSearch`/`clearRecentSearches` in the view model) and **Try** cards that set filters: Expenses this period, Income this year, Entries in "Other" (if that category exists). The mock-up said "Biggest expenses"; there is no sorting, so the card says what it does.
- **Results:** the matched words are highlighted in the title and second line (`MatchText`; never while Hide amounts is on, so it cannot get round the mask), a heading per day like Home, and the summary reads "3 results · ₹1,947.00 spent" (expenses only; left out when the list is cut at the most recent 100, because the total would be partial). Choosing entries swaps the top bar for "Choosing entries" with a cross (`search_selection_clear`); the bulk bar and every other tag are unchanged.
- **Not done from the mock-up:** the microphone (no voice search was built, and a button that does nothing is worse than none), and the amount as a drop-down chip.
- **Tests:** `SearchStartUiTest` (recent: remembered, forgotten, cleared, newest first, last six, repeat replaced; Try card and the spent total; the total with income in the same results; heading and the top bar). Mutation-checked: keeping nine recents, and totalling every kind, each fail a test (the second needed a new test, added). `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest` now ignores the summary line when it checks every row names the new category. `MotionUiTest.theBarShowsEveryTab...` waits for the keyboard before pressing Back: the keyboard now comes up a moment later than the test assumed, and the test failed 2 of 3 times until it did (it passed 4 of 4 before this slice, 4 of 4 after the wait).
- **Verification:** 373 device functional tests, 1 failure (`SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...`, known flaky, 2 of 2 alone). The timing gate was not re-run (it fails on this Mac both before and after; see slice 76). Seen on the emulator (Android 8, light and dark): the start page, results with highlight and day headings, choosing entries. Not seen: the iPhone (Xcode is gone), Search with many results, the saved-search chips, the tag chip. On the emulator the keyboard stays up after choosing an entry by long press (the code asks for it to go; not compared with the previous build).

## Slice 78: better alerts, a bill photo while adding, sending a backup to Drive, this month against last (version 0.1.3)

Asked for by the owner: improve budget, bill and credit-card alerts; an optional bill photo while adding an entry; Google Drive for backups by routes A and B (no internet permission); a "this month versus last month" line.

- **Already there, so not rebuilt:** per-category budgets exist (Budget screen); the card notice already said what is left and the minimum; Home already had a bills banner. An earlier note of mine saying budgets were overall-only was wrong.
- **Budget alerts** (`NoticePlanner`, `NotificationService.check`): the "near" level is chosen (50, 70, 80 or 90%, default 80; `notif_near_*`); a **pace** note when spending runs 20 points ahead of the month (30% used or more, 10 to 90% of the month gone) while still below the level, once per budget and period, never after near/over was said; a **month-end note** in the first five days of a month saying last month's overall budget was kept (`budgetend:`), nothing when it was exceeded. Each can be switched off.
- **Bill alerts:** tracked bills get a heads-up 2 to 3 days ahead and one the day before (each switchable), one on the day, then **one a day for 7 days while overdue** ("overdue by 3 days"), each once (`billtrack:<id>:overdue<N>`). Buttons on the notification (Android, `NoticeActionReceiver`): **Mark paid** (marks the tracked bill paid, records nothing in the ledger) and **In 3 hours** (WorkManager, skipped if the bill was paid meanwhile). Home's banner now reads "N bills due this week" (overdue counted).
- **Card alerts:** a **statement-made** notice (what is owed, minimum, due date) on the statement day and the two after, once; a **credit limit** (optional field in the card's billing dialog, kept as a setting `card_limit:<id>`, removed with the billing dates) with notices at 80% and 100% once per month and level; a **paid** notice once when a statement that was told about is paid in full; **Pay now** on card notices opens the add form as a transfer to the card with what is left (`MainActivity.ACTION_PAY_CARD`, `LedgerViewModel.payCardDraft`).
- **Settings** (Notifications screen): switches for the two ahead reminders, card statement, card limit, pace and month-end; the warning level chips; the ahead and card ones follow the bill switch, the pace and level ones the budget switch.
- **This month against last** (`PeriodCompare`): under the amount on Home: "▼ ₹5,895.00 less than last month so far (-78%)", the same number of days of the earlier period; nothing when the earlier period had no spending; not for yearly budgets. Worked out with the slow figures, not on the save path.
- **Bill photo while adding:** the Add form now has "Receipts and files": "Take a photo" (the phone's camera app through `TakePicture` and our file provider; no camera permission; shown only when a camera app exists) and "Attach a photo or file". Files are copied in at once and attached when the entry is saved; closing the form deletes them; a refused save keeps them. Up to 10, as for saved entries.
- **Backup to Drive:** route A is the system file picker, which lists Google Drive (the button is now "Save backup to a file" with a hint to choose Drive); route B is **"Send backup to..."** (Android share sheet: Drive, email, WhatsApp). A shared backup is checked first, may carry the passphrase, and is **not** recorded as a backup made, because the app cannot know it was sent. The file sits in the app's cache until the next share. Automatic Drive backup would need the internet permission and Google sign-in; not done.
- **Tests:** domain (planner rules, `PeriodCompare`), storage (service: statement window, limit levels, paid, month-end, threshold), device `AlertsUiTest` (settings, card limit dialog, Pay now, notification buttons, Mark paid, snooze queued, Home line, bills banner), `AttachmentsTest` (stage, save, discard, limit), `DataTest` (share). Mutation-checked: statement window, limit level, month-end days, paid-while-owed; one survived (a statement test that ran after the notice was marked sent) and was fixed.
- **Existing tests changed:** `NotificationsTest` scrolls to its switches (the screen is longer) and expects the new "bill is paid" notice; `LoansUiTest.borrowing...` waits for the form (it failed 1 run in 3 alone before, 0 of 5 after); the add-form test no longer expects "save first".
- **Not verified:** iPhone (Xcode is gone; the shared code was checked by eye for Android-only APIs, not compiled for iOS; notification buttons, Pay now, camera and the share sheet are Android only); the camera capture itself (a camera app exists on the emulator but the capture was not exercised); real notification delivery timing on a phone; Android 15/16. Pace, month-end, statement and limit notices were exercised through the service, not waited for in real time.

**Verification:** 383 device functional tests, 4 failed in the full run (`LoansUiTest.borrowing...`, and three `NotificationsTest` tests that this slice changed: fixed as above and then 12 of 12), the rest of the classes touched pass in re-runs (24 of 24 for attachments, alerts, notifications). Host tests pass. The timing gate was not re-run (it fails on this Mac before and after, see slice 76).

### Slice 78 follow-up (version 0.1.4), after review

- **Backups now carry the alert choices:** `BackupStore.SETTINGS` gained `notif_recap` and the new `notif_*` keys, and each card's credit limit (`card_limit:<account id>`) is exported and restored (restore only fills what is not already set). Round trip tested in `BackupStoreTest`.
- **App lock wording** now says the Mark paid button on a bill notice marks a tracked bill paid without unlocking (it records nothing).
- **Pace alert is gentler:** no verdict before 30% of the period has passed (rent and EMIs land early); the storage test now says 70% a week in is fine and a pace note comes later in the month. Entries from repeating items are still counted.
- **Pay now** no longer starts from a credit card as the paying account.
- **Send backup to...** warns on screen when no passphrase is set.
- **Not changed, on purpose:** the "In 3 hours" button is the snooze (WorkManager keeps it across a reboot); "bills due this week" counts tracked and found bills only, not repeating items or card due dates; a camera result after the activity is recreated is lost (the picked-file path is unaffected). Verification: host tests pass; 45 of 45 device tests in Notifications, Alerts, Data, App lock and Accounts.

## Slice 79: the four gaps and six new features (version 0.2.0)

**Gaps closed**
- **Bills due this week** now counts tracked bills, repeating items that wait for the person, and credit cards with something left (a card whose own bill is tracked counts once), with a total of what has a known amount. The banner shows when any of them has something.
- **Camera photo survives Android recreating the screen:** the target file is saved with the screen and a photo whose callback was lost is handed over once (`FilePickers.recoveredPhoto`). Not exercised on a device (the camera was not driven); an empty file from a camera app is refused.
- **Pace alert ignores planned spending:** what repeating items posted (`spend_line` joined to `recurring_done`) is taken off before judging; a rent-only month no longer reads as ahead.
- **Snooze:** the second button is "This evening" (before 15:00) or "In 3 hours", plus "Tomorrow" (09:00; this morning if before nine). Delay is worked out when the button is pressed (`Snooze`).

**New**
- **Spending insight on Home:** the category furthest above its usual (average of the earlier periods at the same point; needs two earlier periods; at least 25% and 500 more) in one line under the month figure.
- **Subscriptions** (More, Money): charges found from your entries (`SubscriptionFinder`: same merchant, steady gap monthly / quarterly / yearly, near-constant amount, still going), with the next date and what each costs a year (monthly x 12, quarterly x 4, yearly x 1), a total, and "Not one" to hide a wrong guess. A review caught a first version that used 365 / 30; fixed and tested.
- **Today widget** (Android): what was spent today and what the budget leaves per day; hidden unless shown from the eye, hidden under Hide amounts and the App lock, like the month total. The data and masking are tested; how it draws on a launcher was not seen.
- **Split a bill with friends:** on a new expense, "Split with friends" (below the account chips) shares it equally. It is **one expense with shares** (the model already had `Expense.shares`): the account pays the whole bill, reports and budgets count your share (the odd paise are yours), and the Lent and borrowed balances now include what friends owe from shared bills (their history shows "Their share of ..."). Undo is one entry. A first draft recorded loans next to the expense; a review pointed to the model and it was replaced. A shared expense still opens read-only in the editor.
- **Financial-year summary** (Your data): April to March, this year or last, as an Excel sheet of income, spending, spending by category and by month. A summary, not tax advice.
- **Not built:** reading a receipt photo (needs an on-device text-recognition library, which the app does not have; awaiting the owner).

**Tests:** domain (PeriodCompare, SpendingInsight, Snooze, SubscriptionFinder, BillSplit and sharing in drafts), storage (pace with rent, people balances with shares, financial year), device (AlertsUiTest, SubscriptionsUiTest, SplitBillUiTest, QuickAddWidgetTest, DataTest). Mutation-checked: pace with planned spend. The full functional suite (390 tests) had one failure: `QuickEntryTest.theNextEntryStartsWithTheCategoryAndAccountUsedLast` failed 3 of 3 alone because the split chip first sat above the category chips and pushed them off screen with the keyboard up; the split section now sits below the account chips and the test passes (12 of 12 with the attachment and split tests). Not re-run in full after that move. Not verified: iPhone (Xcode is gone), the camera, a launcher, Android 15/16, real notification timing.
- **Widget refresh (review fix):** the Today widget was refreshed only from the eye toggle. All widget redraws now go through `refreshWidgets` (quick add and Today): when the app goes to the background, after a widget add or undo, on the eye, and in the hourly check, so today's total follows entries and the new day within about an hour. Not verifiable here: no widget was placed on a launcher; the call sites are not tested.

## Slice 80: subscriptions to reminders, rollover, goals, travel, smarter splits, wide screens, a new tour (version 0.3.0)

- **Remind me (Subscriptions):** one tap makes a repeating expense like the latest charge (same name, amount, category, account), on its usual gap from the next expected date, not in the past; reminds, does not post by itself; once only.
- **Budget rollover** (Budget screen switch, off by default): what was left under each limit last period is added to this period's limit (one period only, never cumulative; overspending carries nothing and takes nothing off). Notifications and safe-to-spend use the larger limit. `budget_rollover` is in backups.
- **Savings goals** (More, Money): a name, a target, optionally months to reach it (the page says what to put aside each month); put in or take out by hand (a note, no entry), or follow an account's balance. Kept as the `goals` setting (in backups), so no schema change.
- **Travel mode:** on a new expense, "Paid in another currency": the currency, what was paid, and your own rate (the app looks nothing up). What left the account is that product, rounded half up; the foreign amount is stored in `Expense.original` (which the model already had) and shown on the row. The last rate for each currency is offered next time. Such an entry still opens read-only, now saying what it was paid in.
- **Smarter split:** "My amounts" (type what each friend owes, yours is the rest, which must be above zero) and "A friend paid" (`FriendPaid`, which the model already had: the amount is your share, no account moves, you owe them it). Lent and borrowed balances now include friends' shares of bills and what you owe for bills they paid, with matching history rows; removing a person who appears in either is refused.
- **Wide screens:** the app is drawn at most 720 dp wide, centred. Checked on a 1600 x 2400 px display at 280 dpi (about 914 dp wide) in portrait. Not checked: landscape, and a real foldable.
- **Large text:** looked at Home, More, Goals, Notifications, Add and Search at 1.3 x text. The switch rows on Notifications ran their text into the switch (found in that look, fixed afterwards with space before the switch, not looked at again); scrolling chip rows run off the edge by design. Not done: a TalkBack pass.
- **Tour:** version 3 adds five stops (Subscriptions, Savings goals, Smarter alerts, Share a bill or pay abroad, Financial year summary) and mentions rollover in the Budget stop. People who saw version 2 are offered just those five, in order; new tour targets Subscriptions and Goals open the real screens.
- **Not done, on the owner's decision:** PDF statement import (needs a PDF text library) and a language option.
- **Tests:** domain (Goals, ForeignAmount, drafts for amounts, friend paid and foreign), storage (rollover with a mutant that is caught, shares and friend-paid balances and backup round trip), device (Subscriptions Remind me, Goals, Foreign, Split modes, Guide what's new). Full device run: 398 tests, 1 failure (`SearchBulkCategoryUiTest.theIncomeSectionIsDisabled...`), passes 3 of 3 alone; the long-press helper now waits for the screen to settle first. The timing gate was not run (it fails on this Mac before and after). Not verified: iPhone (Xcode is gone), the camera, a launcher, Android 15/16, a real tablet or foldable.
- **0.3.1 (review fixes):** the currency conversion is whole-number arithmetic (rate in millionths, halves up, refused when too large to work out exactly) instead of floating point; the remembered rates (`rate_<CODE>`) travel in a backup like the goals and card limits; a goal linked to an account that does not exist on the phone falls back to the amount put aside by hand. Not changed: the app is one centred column up to 720 dp on wide screens (a two-pane Home was suggested, not built).

## Slice 81 (not yet in a build): money in from payment apps; planned next
- **Fix:** a payment-app or bank text where the other person pays you ("Chandu. sent ₹10 to you", "Anita paid you ₹120", "Rs.500 sent to you by Ravi") is read as money in; it was read as an expense because "sent"/"paid" are debit words. "your" never matches ("Paid ₹250 to your Airtel bill" stays money out), nor do "You paid ..." / "You sent ...". Tests in `AppSourcesTest`, undoing the rule makes them fail; the whole parsers, domain and storage host suites pass. Not tried on a real notification; items already in the inbox keep their direction (Flip fixes them).
- **Planned, to be scoped later (owner approved, not started):** bank alert emails via Gmail/Outlook notifications (needs InboxStyle lines, group-summary skip, cross-source duplicate check, sample emails); bill/receipt photo reading into items with ML Kit text recognition; credit card statement import (PDF text and CSV).

## Slice 82: where an entry came from, bank alert emails, PDF and photo statements (version 0.4.0)
- **Origin:** every entry made from a text, a payment-app notification, an email notification or a statement line keeps the original words (table `entry_origin`, in backups; old backups still restore). Opening the entry shows "Where this came from": the kind and sender, the words, and when it arrived. Hand-typed entries show nothing. The inbox keeps up to 2000 characters of a notification or email (600 before).
- **Bank alert emails:** opt-in switch (Capture screen, off by default) reads Gmail, Outlook and Yahoo Mail NOTIFICATIONS, never the mailbox, with no sign-in and no network. An email is kept only when its sender name or an address in it matches a known bank (or a name the user added) and the words then read as a transaction or bill, through the same parser as texts (so the money-in fix applies). Group summaries are skipped; a notification that lists several emails is read line by line; "never share your OTP" footers are removed before reading. Works only when the email app shows the text in its notification. Real Gmail/Outlook layouts and bank email wordings were not seen: every test wording is plausible, not copied.
- **Duplicates across sources:** a payment-app alert and a bank text or email of one spend are marked as a likely repeat; the same reference from two senders matches within 24 hours; an email naming different last-four digits than the other item is a separate payment (applied to emails only, because two banks' texts for one payment may name two accounts).
- **Statements from PDF and photos:** the Import flow takes PDFs (password asked for; never kept) and photos or screenshots, read on the phone (PDFBox-Android for text, bundled ML Kit text recognition for pictures and scanned PDFs). Text becomes a table run through the same preview and import as CSV. Dr/Cr marks decide direction, then a running balance on bank statements, otherwise the card convention (unmarked = spending on a card). Card payments ("PAYMENT RECEIVED", "BBPS PAYMENT") are skipped and counted, never income. The raw line is kept as the entry's origin. The preview says it was read from a PDF or photo and to check the amounts.
- **No internet:** ML Kit's usage-statistics library added INTERNET; it is removed in the manifest with `tools:node="remove"` and its upload service is removed. The release APK and the Play bundle list no INTERNET permission. The app still does not need a network.
- **Size:** release APK 11.6 MB before, 56 MB now (OCR for four ABIs); Play bundle 33.8 MB (people download about 11 MB more than before).
- **Tests:** parsers (EmailAlertTest 15), domain (StatementTextParserTest 33, DuplicatesTest), storage (OriginStoreTest, backup), device (OriginUiTest, EmailCaptureUiTest, PdfImportUiTest, DuplicatesUiTest). Full device run: 412 tests, 3 failures; two are known flaky classes and passed alone, and the third (DuplicatesUiTest) was a real regression from the new last-four rule, fixed by limiting it to emails; the changed classes passed after. The timing gate was not run. Not verified: any real bank statement PDF or email, a real photo of a statement (the PDF and picture tests are generated here; PDFBox put a space inside a word in one, "S WIGGY"), iPhone (no Xcode), a real phone, Android 15/16.
- **0.4.1 (found with the owner's real HDFC savings statement PDF, 23 KB, password-protected):** the first run read every line as "unknown direction", because a narration "UPI-... CREDIT CARD" made the whole file look like a card statement. Fixed: a statement with Withdrawal and Deposit columns is a bank statement; the summary's opening balance ("Opening Balance ... Closing Balance" and the line under it) seeds the running balance so the first line is settled too; the bank's reference number and value date no longer sit in the description. Checked on the phone path (PDFBox with the password, then the parser) against the statement's own summary: 43 debits of 5,65,536.57 and 12 credits of 8,55,479.90, 0 unknown, exactly as the bank printed. PDFBox's spacing tolerance is raised to 1.0 so a drawn PDF no longer splits a word ("S WIGGY"). A bill paid with "record it" now keeps the bill text as the entry's origin. Not done: the import was exercised through code on the emulator, not clicked through the real UI on this file; no minified-build (R8) run of the PDF path (the project has no test build for it); real card statements and bank emails still unseen.
- **0.4.2 (the owner's real HDFC credit card statement):** it printed the rupee sign as the letter "C", marked money back as "+ C 38,010.00" (and "+ 375" before the amount is reward points), ended every line with a bullet read as "l", and wrapped a reference ("... (Ref#") onto the line BEFORE the transaction it belongs to. Result: no amounts were found. Now the "C" before a decimal amount is the rupee sign, "+ C" is money back, the bullet is dropped, a line that opens a reference goes with the next transaction, reward points and reference tails are cleaned from the name. On the phone path with the real file: 11 purchases totalling 39,883.60, one 2.00 refund, the 38,010.00 payment to the card skipped and counted; the statement's own summary says purchases 39,883.60 and payments/credits 38,012.00 (38,010.00 + 2.00), so it matches to the paisa. Through the import screen's own path (`startImport` with the password) the savings statement shows 55 transactions, spending 5,65,536.57, income 8,55,479.90, and the card statement 12 transactions. A bill that was tracked keeps its message until it is paid or dismissed (it used to be cleared when tracked), so paying it with "record it" gives the entry its origin; a test covers track-then-pay. The two real files, their text and the passwords were used only in /tmp and on the emulator and are in no commit or test; the regression tests use invented names. Only two real layouts (HDFC savings, HDFC card) have been checked; other banks are untested. Not built: a warning when the parsed totals differ from the statement's own summary.
- **0.4.3 (card bills with no message):** the Home banner counts a credit card with money left on its latest statement (derived from the card's due day and its entries, so an imported statement makes one), but the Bills page listed only bills that a message brought, so tapping it showed an empty page. The page now lists such a card under Coming up ("<card> statement", what is left, due date, minimum when known) with Mark paid: the dialog takes the amount and the account it was paid from, and records a TRANSFER to the card (not spending: the purchases were counted when imported, so counting the payment again would double them). No Remove or Set-due buttons on these rows; a card whose own bill from a message is tracked for the same day shows once. Device test covers it (row shown, amount, no Remove, Mark paid gives one transfer and the one expense is unchanged, row gone). Full device run: 413 tests, 1 failure (the known flaky categories test). Not done: repeating items due this week also count in the banner but are not on the Bills page; the wrapped-narration spacing and long bank codes in imported descriptions are unchanged.

## Slice 83: the iPhone app catches up with Android 0.4.3 (Xcode 27.1 installed)
- **Builds on this Mac:** the iPhone framework links (the Kotlin/Native link runs inside the Gradle daemon and ran out of memory at its 3 GB: `tools/ios_build.sh` now passes `-Dorg.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx12g`). The test simulator was recreated (iPhone 17, iOS 26.5; the test script pins it).
