- **What the iPhone app now has that it lacked:** PDF statements with passwords (PDFKit), picture statements (Vision), bank emails (Shortcuts automation on the Email trigger, same classifier as Android), Share to Expenses (share extension, App Group queue), Today widget (WidgetKit, App Group), notification buttons (Mark paid, Later today, Tomorrow, Pay now), automatic copies to a Files folder (shared `AutoExportRunner`, which Android now uses too), Send backup through the share sheet, Take a photo of a bill. The new alert kinds (budget pace, month wrap-up, card statement, limit, cleared) are in iPhone's Hide amounts masking.
- **Bank texts and notifications:** iOS lets no app read them. Texts: the existing Shortcuts "When I get a message" automation (needs the person's one-time setup). Emails: the same with the Email trigger. Payment-app notifications (Google Pay, PhonePe, Paytm): impossible on iPhone; the bank's text or email about the same payment is the way in.
- **Tests (simulator):** 22 unit tests (backup interop, capture incl. email and shared text, statements: text PDF, password PDF, a picture read by Vision, widget data, notification buttons, auto export with trimming) and 18 UI tests, all passing. The real HDFC savings and card statements (owner's files, not in the repository) were run through the PDFKit path on the simulator: the same 55 transactions with the bank's own totals, and the same 12 card lines, as Android. The UI tests were fixed (they did not scroll to rows below the fold or behind the bottom bar, and tapped while a list was still moving; More stays open under its screens).
- **Android after the shared changes:** AutoExport, Data, Capture and email capture device tests pass (33 of 33); host suites pass.
- **Not verified:** any of this on a real iPhone (widget, share extension, Shortcuts automations, camera, share sheet, notification buttons, background refresh, a Files-provider folder: only a temporary folder was used); Vision on a real photo of a statement (a generated picture was used); a signed build: this Xcode has no Apple account yet ("No Accounts"), and the old provisioning profile has no App Groups, so `tools/ios_build.sh ipa` fails until the owner signs in under Xcode > Settings > Accounts and the profiles are regenerated.
- **Quick add widget added afterwards** (taps queued in the App Group, recorded by the app with the tap's time when it next runs; Undo while queued): unit test for the hand-over, and the Android quick-add tests still pass (26 of 26). Not looked at on a real home screen.
- **Not done:** the Quick Settings tile and launcher shortcuts (Android-only ideas); a snooze on iPhone cannot check that the bill is still unpaid at the moment it fires (a waiting one is removed when the app runs after the bill was paid).
- **Review fixes after this slice:** a damaged PDF that still starts with `%PDF-` took the iPhone app down (PDFKit answers nil, which Kotlin/Native does not allow for a constructor): the file is now checked first and refused in words (test added, it crashed before); the Share queue now removes only the texts it read, not everything; waiting snoozes are dropped for paid bills. 23 unit tests pass.

## Slice 84: a bank's own figures on Home; SBI wording; iPhone setup helpers (not yet in a Play build)
- **Home by account:** tapping an account tile on Home's strip narrows the list to that account (what left it or came into it; a transfer shows on both sides), with an outline on the tile and "Showing only X · Edit account · Show all"; tapping the tile again also clears it. An account's card in Accounts has "See its transactions on Home". Paging is by new SQL: two indexed lookups merged in Kotlin (`recentEntriesByAccount`/`ByCounter`, `entriesBeforeByAccount`/`ByCounter`, with new indexes `entry_account_time_seq` and `entry_counter_time`; a single `OR` query scanned the whole ledger for a rarely used account, checked with EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN on 200,000 rows; a storage test asserts both plans are index searches). In the filtered view a transfer is shown as money in for the account it went into and money out for the one it left (test checks the signs); unfiltered it has no sign as before. With no filter the old queries run unchanged. Tests: `AccountFilterUiTest` (2), full Android functional run 415 of 415, iPhone UI test.
- **SBI wording:** "A/C X8968 debited by 349.00 ... Refno ..." has no currency mark, so no amount was found. A bare figure is now the amount only right after debited/credited/spent/paid/received/sent/withdrawn/deducted plus by/for/of/with; a balance is never taken (tests). Parsers 79 tests, domain 358, storage 278, 0 failures.
- **iPhone setup helpers:** copy buttons for the trigger words and "Send a test now" on the Capture page. The Shortcuts automation itself cannot be created by an app; the owner confirmed the Capture bank message shortcut works when run by hand and asks for the message. A ready-made `.shortcut` file was imported by the owner and works but is not yet bundled in the app.
- **Not released:** Play still has 0.4.3 (code 13); this needs 0.4.4 (code 14). iPhone test build with the SBI fix is installed on the owner's phone; the account filter is not in it yet.

## Slice 85: text reading on Google Play, categories everywhere (versions 0.5.0 and 0.5.1)
- **Text permission on Play:** Google lists "SMS-based money management" as an allowed use, so a separate build type `playSms` (`./gradlew :androidApp:bundlePlaySms`, applicationId `io.github.chandu007.expenses`) keeps the text permission while the ordinary `play` build stays without it as a fallback (each Play version code can be uploaded once, so the two use different codes). Before Android's own permission prompt a disclosure screen says what is read and that nothing leaves the phone (`sms_disclosure`, test `SmsDisclosureUiTest`). The privacy policy (`docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`, `docs/site/privacy.html`) says so, and `docs/PLAY_SMS_DECLARATION.md` holds the answers given in Play Console. The declaration was submitted by the owner; it now sits under "Actioned" with RECEIVE_SMS declared. That shows it was filed, not that Google approved it.
- **0.5.1:** a captured payment in To review and the Category rules form show the first ten categories plus "More (n)", which opens the searchable category list, so every category can be chosen (`inbox_more_categories_<id>`, `rule_more_categories`).

## Slice 86: the owner's overnight list (version 0.6.0)
Twelve ideas and one request, built by five parallel slices and then integrated on one emulator.
- **Statements:** descriptions from PDF and photo statements are tidied (`StatementDescriptions`: a UPI/NEFT/IMPS/ACH narration reduces to the counterparty; wrapped words are glued only when sure; the full line stays in "Where this came from"). `StatementSummary` reads the totals a statement prints and the import shows `import_totals_warning` ("The statement says 43 debits ... but 41 were found") only when they differ. Layouts were taken from the owner's two HDFC files as described to the builder; no real file was run through it.
- **Bills:** the Bills page lists the repeating items the Home banner counts (Post it, Skip), so the banner and the page agree.
- **Credit cards:** spend this cycle, limit used (bar), left to pay, minimum due and days to the due date, on the tile and in the account dialog (`CardSummary`).
- **Getting started card:** five steps that tick themselves, shown for 14 days after the first entry, folded to one line until opened (so it never pushes the entry just added off a small screen), hidden for ledgers that already had entries (`FirstWeek`, settings `first_week_started`, `first_week_dismissed`).
- **Search:** From and To dates (either may be left open; both days included, phone's zone), an account filter, both saved with a saved search and in backups (new optional columns `from_day`, `to_day`, `account_id`; old backups restore). The filter rows were folded into the kind row and the range row so the results keep their room. Home narrowed to one account has This month / Last month / All and Search for that account (four new `...InRange` queries; the old ones are untouched).
- **To review:** swipe a clear card right to add, left to skip; "Add all from <payee>".
- **iPhone setup page:** Step 1, 2, 3 and a live line "Last text received 2 minutes ago".
- **Read items from a photo:** a bill photo is read on the phone into a list of items with ticks and a total (`BillItems`); applying fills the amount if empty and puts the items in the note.
- **Send feedback:** More, Send feedback opens a prefilled mail with version, system and phone model only; `openExternalUrl` now works on Android.
- **Wide screens:** from 840 dp wide, Home sits on the left and the entry editor opens on the right (`WidePane`); phones are unchanged by construction.
- **Tests:** host suites pass. Android device suite (the three timing classes excluded as before): the first run was cut by a process crash at 430 tests with none failing before it; the crashed test and the 24 that had not run passed on their own, after the fixes below. iPhone: 25 unit tests and all 20 UI tests pass on the simulator (run in three parts because of the time limit).
- **Fixed while integrating:** the getting-started card pushed rows below the fold and failed twelve older tests (now folded by default; those tests mark it dismissed); Search had grown so tall the results lost most of their room (account and date controls now share rows with the kind and range chips); the emulator's own "Google App has stopped" dialog dismissed Compose dialogs at random (that app is disabled on the test emulator); a Material3 date cell's text is the whole date, so the date test matches that.
- **Not verified:** all of it on a real phone, a tablet or an iPad; real statements, real bill photos; TalkBack and VoiceOver. `docs/REAL_DEVICE_CHECKLIST.md` lists what to try.

## Slice 87: bill photos on the iPhone and Android, each item as its own entry (versions 0.6.1 and 0.6.2)
- **0.6.1, found with the owner's own bill photos:** (1) Android's release build failed every reading ("Attempt to read from field ... on a null object reference"): R8 broke ML Kit; `androidApp/proguard-rules.pro` now keeps ML Kit as shipped (the debug build never showed it). (2) The iPhone app closed: `BillItems.parse` kept the position of a Qty column in a nullable Boolean, which Kotlin/Native crashed on (`ThrowInvalidReceiverTypeException`) when a bill had the line "Total Qty. 3 ..."; it is now an enum, and a line with figures is no longer taken for a column heading. The crash was reproduced and its end seen through the app's own framework in the simulator (the native unit-test runner passes the same input even on the old code, so `BillItemsQtyColumnTest` guards the reading, not the crash). (3) HEIC photos (what an iPhone saves) were refused as "not a picture": `ImageSniff` accepts them. (4) A rupee sign read as a star hid the grand total ("*605.00"); a price whose first digit was read as a letter ("i99.00") stayed in the name. Both fixed with a test from Android's reading of the same bill.
- **0.6.2:** (1) On an iPhone the list of items appeared and went away at once, and the button could not start another photo. The camera and the Files picker were opened over the "Take a photo / Choose a photo" dialog, which on iOS is a screen of its own, and the list of items was shown while those were still leaving. The two choices are now buttons in place (no dialog), and the camera and the picker answer only after they have gone (the camera's dismissal completion; half a second after the Files picker). Review follow-up: the top controller skips one that is being dismissed (`topViewController`), and the Files picker's answer waits until the picker is gone (`whenGone`, polled, not a fixed delay). This could be checked in the simulator only with a picture standing in for the camera (`-pick-file <path>` launch argument; `BillPhotoUITests`: the list stays, a second photo can be read, each item saved); the real camera and the real Files picker were not run, so it is not proven on a phone. (2) **Each item as its own entry:** after reading a bill, "Each item as its own entry" (new plain expense with an account and a category on the form) saves one expense per ticked line with the form's account, date, category and tags, the item as its note, the photo on the first, and, when the bill's total is above its lines, a last entry "Tax and other charges" so the entries add up to the bill (optional). All or none; one Undo (`UndoUi.bulkCreated`). Not offered for a shared or foreign-currency expense, or an edit. (3) A line can be corrected (name, price) before it is used. (4) "Copy the text that was read", so a bill that reads badly can be sent to improve the reader. Tests: `BillEntriesTest` (host), `BillItemsUiTest` (14), `BillEntriesSaveTest`, `BillPhotoUITests` (iPhone simulator).
- **Accuracy:** the reader works on whatever text the phone's recogniser finds, and shops print in many layouts; only three photos were available (one restaurant bill, two bank slips). It is a best effort that the person checks and corrects, not a promise.


## Slice 88: robustness batch (version 0.7.0)
Six pieces of work done side by side (Home, backup with files, the release-mode iPhone guard, the iPhone signing-expiry notice, release checks with accessibility, undo and loading states), then integrated and run together on one emulator and one simulator. Android 0.7.0 (code 27; Play has 0.5.1, code 18), iPhone 0.7.0 (build 25). From this version only the SMS Play bundle (`bundlePlaySms`) is built: Play accepted the SMS declaration and the testers use that build, so the no-SMS `play` bundle is no longer made.
- **Home scrolls as one page.** The cards, banners and entries are one scrolling page (`HeaderFold` and `HomeCollapse.kt` are gone). On a 1080x1920 display with 8 entries and the cards, 5 rows were visible and scrolled before; now 7 and a partial one. With an account filter on, no row could be reached while the cards were showing (0 rows before, all rows now). The account filter bar stays at the top while the page scrolls, Search's result count and "Select all" are now the first row of the results list (not a fixed bar), and the wide-screen More page was fixed.
- **Refresh is cheaper.** The per-account usage query uses the indexes now, the week-ahead line checks early, and the account rows are reused. `accountUsage`: 338 ms to 3.5 ms at 20,000 entries and 1,785 ms to 119 ms at 100,000 (before and after in the same run, on an emulator that was under load).
- **Timing is not settled.** The timing gates ran under load and cannot separate load from the change: `RefreshBudgetTest` p50 is 26.9 ms now against 3.4 to 4.0 ms recorded earlier. That is unexplained. It has to be measured again on a quiet machine or a real phone. The three timing classes (`PerformanceBudgetTest`, `RefreshBudgetTest`, `RecreateStressTest`) were not part of the integration run.
- **Backups can include photos and files** (`docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md`, version 2). The Data screen has a switch (on by default); a backup without files is still written as version 1. Files are written and read in pieces and a sealed backup with files uses the chunked `EXPENC2` layout (the passphrase layout without files, `EXPENC1`, is unchanged); a damaged file is skipped and counted and the rest restores. Automatic copies skip files over 100 MB, the share sheet takes files up to 25 MB, and on Android the payload is capped at min(128 MB, an eighth of the heap). An independent review found three major and several minor problems, all fixed by the same piece of work: two restores started together raced, automatic exports could overlap (a copy is now written under a temporary name and renamed, one at a time), and one unreadable file failed the whole backup (it is now left out with a note). The iPhone writes and reads the same files; `tools/backup_interop/Interop.java` reads what the iPhone wrote (`tools/ios_build.sh interop`, checked: files verified, sealed and plain).
- **Release-mode native test guard.** `tools/ios_build.sh native` runs the `core/domain` and `core/parsers` tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary on the simulator, the build the iPhone app ships. It fails on the old crashing `BillItems.kt` (0.6.1) and passes on the fixed one.
- **Release APK smoke test.** `tools/release_smoke.sh` builds the R8-minified release APK, installs it on the emulator and drives it, including reading `tools/fixtures/bill_fixture.png` (an invented receipt) with Add, Read items from a photo. It fails when the ML Kit keep rules in `proguard-rules.pro` are removed and passes with them. `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` says where it belongs in a release.
- **The SMS test crash.** `pm revoke` of a granted text permission kills the app's process, which under instrumentation is the test process; it ended a full device run once. `SmsDisclosureUiTest` (needs the permission not granted) now checks and skips when it is granted instead of revoking, and the one test that grants it, `ZzzSmsGrantedUiTest`, is named to sort last.
- **Accessibility** (`docs/ACCESSIBILITY_FINDINGS.md`, Google's Accessibility Test Framework over nine walks of the screens, on the API 26 emulator, light theme, default font): four touch-target ERRORs fixed (the Search "Select all" button, the bill items mode rows, the visible edge of a stacked account card, a getting-started row), the "+" on Accounts has a name, the bill item "Edit" buttons name their item, and one borderline contrast was fixed; six remaining WARNINGs were judged false positives (a disabled Save, text cut off by the keyboard or by the bottom bar). **Not fixed:** in Edit account and in the billing-dates dialog the on-screen keyboard covers Save, Cancel and the other buttons while a field has focus (the keyboard's Back or tick key closes it); only New account shrinks above the keyboard. Compose's own `enableAccessibilityChecks()` did not see any Compose element on this device, so the test also runs the framework over the accessibility node tree.
- **Undo and loading states** (`docs/UNDO_AUDIT.md`): skipping an item in To review, and skipping or removing a bill, now have an Undo bar. Reports shows a progress bar while it is slow to compute (`reports_working`) and the bill items dialog one while a photo is read (`bill_items_working`); the busy state of reading a bill photo was also fixed. A statement import has no Undo, on purpose (the reason is in the audit: an id that exists is skipped, so importing the file again after an undo would skip every row).
- **iPhone signing expiry** (`docs/IOS.md`): the iPhone app shows a notice on More and on Your data from 2 days before the free signature runs out (read from the app's own `embedded.mobileprovision`; not shown on the simulator, in Android or in a store build). `tools/ios_refresh.sh` rebuilds and installs, and `tools/com.expenses.ios-refresh.plist.example` is a launchd agent for a daily check. The schedule has never run, the script was tested only against a stand-in `xcrun`, and the notice has not been seen against a real provisioning profile.
- **Bill reading size** stays at 4096 px: smaller sizes changed the text the recogniser found.
- **Found while integrating, and what was done:**
  - `AlertsUiTest.aCreditLimitIsEnteredOnTheCardKeptShownAgainAndRemovedWithItsBillingDates` failed in the full run, and in 5 of 12 runs alone on the working tree; on the 0.6.2 commit a00b138 (built in a separate checkout) it passed 12 of 12. The cause is the keyboard-resize call that had been added to the billing-dates dialog (`CardTermsDialog`): with it removed the test passed 12 of 12 alone and the class 3 times in a row. How the dialog moving makes the tap miss was not observed, only that the test's tap on "Remove billing dates" does not take effect. The call is gone from that dialog (as it already was for Edit account; `docs/ACCESSIBILITY_FINDINGS.md` says so).
  - `AccountPeriodUiTest.searchThisAccountOpensSearchInsideItAndTheAccountCanBeChangedThere` is flaky on the 0.6.2 commit too: alone it passed 5 of 12 there and 7 of 12 on the working tree. A screenshot of a failing run shows the keyboard up and the third result row below the visible part of the list, so the test does not find it. Not changed.
  - `BackupFilesTest` (new, so there is no 0.6.2 baseline): `aSecondTapOnRestore...` failed 2 times in 17 runs (in the failure seen, the restore had already finished and the dialog was gone when the test looked for the disabled button), and `forTheShareSheetFilesGo...` ran out of memory once in the full run and passed 10 times after. As the code reads, the test keeps 28 MB of file bytes in a 48 MB test heap, and attaching a file hashes it from one `readBytes()` (`AttachmentFiles.copyStream`, not changed by this batch).
- **Tests (this integration):** host (JVM) 842, 0 failures (domain 442, parsers 79, storage 321, run with `--rerun-tasks`); the common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary 511 (435 + 76), 0 failures; Android device suite 497 tests in 85 classes in one run of 46 minutes (the three timing classes left out), run before the one-line fix in `CardTermsDialog`: 3 failures and 2 skipped (both in `QuickAddWidgetTest`), the failures being the three above. Reruns alone: `AccountPeriodUiTest`, `AlertsUiTest` and `BackupFilesTest` together (29 tests) passed all 29 in 3 of 4 runs, and the 4th failed one `BackupFilesTest` test (the restore-button race above); after the fix `AlertsUiTest` passed 3 of 3 class runs (9 tests each), and `AccessibilityUiTest`, `AccountsUiTest` and `AccountFilterUiTest` (19 tests) passed. The full suite was not run again after that fix. Release smoke test 7 of 7. iPhone simulator: 31 unit tests and 22 UI tests, all passing (run class by class); the signed IPA builds.
- **Not verified:** any of this on a real phone (Android or iPhone); a real Storage Access Framework folder or Google Drive for the automatic copies; a real iPhone provisioning profile for the expiry notice; timing on a phone; TalkBack and VoiceOver; dark mode and large text for the accessibility checks; the two-pane layout and landscape.


## Slice 89: bill reminder texts are bills, not debits (version 0.7.1)
- **What was wrong.** A Jio bill reminder ("Your bill dated ... is due for payment on 30-10-2026. Total amount payable : Rs.706.82. ... Kindly ignore, if already paid.") reached the iPhone through the Shortcuts automation and was filed as a DEBIT in To review. Reproduced on the exact text (phone number invented) before changing anything: `GenericAlertParser` returned `Parsed(DEBIT, 70682)` and `BillParser` returned the right bill (Jio, 70682, due 2026-10-30), but `CaptureClassifier` skipped the bill rules whenever the transaction parser had produced an alert. Two rules combined: (1) the word "paid" in "Kindly ignore, if already paid" is in the transaction parser's list of debit words, and it defeated the parser's own "due notice" guard (which only fires when there is no debit or credit word); (2) any parsed alert counted as money that moved. With no sender (the iPhone path) the text reached the classifier because the URL "www.jio.com" names Jio (`PasteRules`, root JIOPAY).
- **The rule** (comment in `CaptureClassifier`). A text that asks for money (`BillParser`: due, payable, pay by, bill generated, statement ready, an EMI or premium falling due, with an amount, and not worded as a receipt) is a bill, unless it also states that money moved (`MoneyMoved`): a past-tense movement word (debited, credited, spent, withdrawn, deducted, refunded, reversed, transferred, paid, purchase) outside a promise ("will be debited", "to be debited") and outside an "ignore if already paid" line, or a UPI reference number, or HDFC's "Txn ... Card ... At" layout. Then the debit or credit wins ("Rs.500 debited ... Your bill of Rs.1200 is due on 25-10" stays a transaction). Receipts ("payment of Rs X received", "thank you for your payment") are never bills and go to the transaction rules as before. "Kindly ignore, if already paid" and its variants (`PaidDisclaimer`) are taken out of the words before either parser reads them.
- **Also changed.** "Payment of Rs X successful for your ... bill" is a debit (it was dropped as "no direction"); the transaction parser's due-notice guard also knows "amount payable" and "due date/on/by"; a text that quotes "payments received after ..." on a card statement is a bill and no longer a credit; `JioHome`/`Jio fixed` bills are broadband bills; `BillParser.promisesDebit` is gone (replaced by the rule above).
- **Duplicates.** The bill's key is kind, sender root, card or consumer number, due date and amount, so the same text twice, or the same bill by text and again pasted, is one bill (`DUPLICATE`). A bill the person skipped does not come back. "Kindly ignore, if already paid" has no effect on the bill (nothing in a text says it is paid; the person marks it paid).
- **Items already misfiled.** `MessageIngest.reclassifyWaiting` runs when the app opens and when To review opens: each still-pending text item (source SMS, with its stored sender and words) is judged again by the same rules; one that is now a bill becomes a bill (same key, so no double) and the item is skipped. It is idempotent, and items the person already accepted or skipped, emails, payment-app notifications and items that are still transactions are not touched.
- **Tests.** Host: `BillReminderClassifierTest` (the exact text with an invented number, its wording variants, 11 bill reminders of every kind, real debits and receipts that stay transactions, mixed texts, a statement with "payments received"); the existing suites pass with no expectation edited. Device: `BillsUiTest` (the text through `Capture.ingest` shows on Bills and not in To review; a misfiled pending item moves, an accepted one and a real debit stay) and every class that ingests a text. iPhone: `CaptureTests.testABillReminderTextIsABillNotADebit`, the unit suite and `CaptureUITests`.
- **Not verified:** a real SMS on a phone; a real Jio message through the Shortcuts automation; the move of an already-filed item on the iPhone (the Android device test covers `reclassifyWaiting`; the iPhone has no test that seeds an old item). The Jio sender header (for example JIOHOM) is not on the allowlist: with a sender passed by the automation that is not on the list, the text is ignored as before; without a sender it is recognised by the word "Jio" (a text that never says "Jio" as a whole word, for example only "JioHome", is not recognised).


## Slice 90: capture quality, card payments in imports, safe to spend, reimbursements, protected copies (version 0.8.0)
Four pieces of work done side by side (capture, statement imports, money views, protected automatic copies), reviewed by an independent reviewer, fixed, then integrated and run together. Android 0.8.0 (code 29; Play has 0.6.2, code 25), iPhone 0.8.0 (build 27). Only the SMS Play bundle is built.
- **Capture quality.** (1) A bank text the app missed can be reported from "How capture is doing" (a sender and the pasted text), and an item in To review that was read wrongly from its own card; the report has the personal parts removed (names, phone numbers, account and card digits, UPI addresses, references, links, one-time codes, dates stay), shown before anything is sent and mailed through the existing feedback mail; a "Keep the amounts" switch (off by default) keeps only the amount of the payment itself: balances, limits, dues and closing amounts are always hidden. The reviewer found that the first version left balances and dues in the clear with the switch on and told the user they were hidden; the rule was rewritten (an amount stays only when its own clause says money moved and no word of the clause speaks of a balance, limit, due and so on; masking wins) and 17 cases pin it. Limits written down: a lower-case name after "to" is masked only when a phone number or address follows; with amounts kept, one corpus sample (an amount about 90 characters after "debited") is masked, which is the safe direction. (2) The sender rules are one declarative table (`BankRules`, 42 entries; the reviewer checked it holds the same 42 roots and issuers as the old list, and the corpus classifications were unchanged). (3) A merchant table of well-known Indian merchants (a test asserts at least 250 named ones; written from the public brand names, not from anyone's transactions) gives a clean display name and a starter category. The reviewer found that a person's name starting with a merchant name ("Ola Sharma", "Swiggy Kumar", "Jio Rao") was filed under that merchant; a name that starts with a merchant and goes on with other words now gets no category (kept: "Swiggy Instamart", "Amazon Prime", "Apollo Pharmacy Ltd").
- **Card payments and app exports in imports.** A statement line that pays a credit card ("CREDIT CARD PAYMENT", BillDesk or BBPS to a card company, CRED, "CC PAYMENT", a card company's own wording) is no longer spending: it becomes a transfer to the card, or, when the app cannot tell which card, a choice with Import disabled until it is made; on a card's own statement a "payment received" credit is skipped (the bank statement carries the transfer), and an existing transfer within 3 days answers for the line. The reviewer's first finding was that with exactly one card every detected line defaulted to that card even when it named other digits or another bank; now the default is a transfer only when the line is identified for that card or names no digits and no bank. The reviewer's seven false positives ("STARBUCKS CARD PAYMENT", "SODEXO CARD PAYMENT" and so on) are negatives in a table-driven test. The group lists three lines and a "Show all N" button lists them all. Presets for Money Manager, YNAB, Splitwise, Cashew and Spendee read those apps' exports (columns written from the apps' documented export layout; no real export was available): an unrecognised income/expense value, another currency, transfers and Splitwise settle-ups ("Payment") are left out and counted, not read as spending. Creating categories from an import is opt-in. Dialog keyboard room: the account dialogs get a fixed room above the keyboard (a dialog window is not resized, so the keyboard is never reported inside it); the Accounts header drops its help sentence when the safe-to-spend line is shown. The AccountTilesUiTest failure the reviewer reported (0 of 10) was bisected by STATEMENTS to the safe-to-spend line pushing the fourth tile off the list; fixed by the compact header, test unchanged.
- **Safe to spend** (Accounts under Net worth; Home under the budget card): cash, bank and wallet balances, minus what credit cards owe, minus tracked bills, EMIs and repeating items due before the horizon (the budget period's end, else 30 days); never shown below zero ("Nothing spare until ..."; "Short by ..." in its dialog); other-currency accounts are left out and the note says how many. Computed in the slow-figures pass, not on the save path. Setting `safe_to_spend_on_home` ('1' show, '0' hide, absent = shows only when an account holds money and something is due). **Sinking funds** (`sinking_funds_on`, default off, a switch on the Repeating page): the yearly bills' monthly set-aside is also subtracted. **EMI payments left**: a repeating item with an end shows payments left, the last date and "still to pay" (payments times the amount, no interest maths).
- **"I expect this back"** (reimbursements): on a plain expense, an expected amount and who from; it stays spending until money comes back; "Got it back" records an ordinary REFUND entry (partial allowed), "Not expecting it" closes it, both with Undo; a Home chip and a "Waiting to get back" section on Lent and borrowed list what is waiting. Schema: a new small table `entry_expect` (entry_id, from_text, person_id, expected_minor, closed), created with CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS at upgrade and in the schema (an old database opens; tested). Backup: an optional `expects` list, left out when empty, so a backup without any is byte-identical to before; an older reader ignores the key, an old backup restores fine (both tested); settings `safe_to_spend_on_home` and `sinking_funds_on` travel in backups. A new statement query `transfersLikeStatementRow` uses the existing counter-account index. Not built: a Search chip for "waiting to get back" and a swipe or long-press action on an expense.
- **Protected automatic copies** (opt-in, Your data, "Protect copies with a passphrase"; setting `auto_export_protect`): a background run cannot ask for the passphrase, so a key derived from it is kept in the phone's secure storage (Android: AES-GCM wrapped by a Keystore key; iPhone: a Keychain item readable after first unlock, this device only) and the automatic copies are sealed with it in a new file layout, **EXPENC3** (written in chunks like EXPENC2; the passphrase alone opens it through Restore, no key from the phone needed; `docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md`). The **key record is version 2**: 57 bytes with a 4-byte check, version 1 never shipped and is not read; a flipped bit is reported as damaged (all 456 single-bit flips tested). The key read has three outcomes (not found, unavailable, damaged) with three different status texts; in every case nothing is written, pruned or recreated and no plain copy is made. A hostile header (iterations, chunk size) is refused before any key work; the reviewer wrote an independent JDK-only reader that opened a Kotlin-written copy with the passphrase alone, and tried truncation, reordering, duplication and header changes (all refused). Excel files from before protection are not removed silently: the Data screen counts them and has a "Delete them" button (only names like `expenses-YYYY-MM-DD.xlsx`). The iPhone test entry points refuse to run on a device build. The protect setting and the key are not in backups. Changing the passphrase makes a new key; older copies keep the old passphrase. `docs/IOS_DB_ENCRYPTION.md` (investigation, no code) recommends NOT encrypting the iPhone database now (the OS already encrypts it at rest; a device-only key would turn restoring a backup onto a new phone into data loss) and spending the effort on protected copies and a tested restore.
- **Research documents** (no code): `docs/research/OUR_FEATURES.md` (what the app does, checked against the code at 0.7.1) and `docs/research/COMPETITOR_ANALYSIS.md`. OUR_FEATURES.md was written before this release: it still says there is no "expecting this back" marker and does not list the features above. The in-app Guide has the same gap.
- **Independent review** (REVIEW2): four major findings, all fixed before this integration, each with tests: STATEMENTS M-1 (one-card default), M-2 (Money Manager unknown type and currency), M-3 (Splitwise settle-ups), CAPTURE M-4 (redactor said balances stay hidden when they did not); the AccountTilesUiTest gate was fixed as above. Minor findings fixed: the key record check, three-way key read, test entry points compiled out of device builds, old Excel files; the merchant false matches ("Ola Sharma", "Apollo Tyres Ltd", "A2B Travels" and so on); the safe-to-spend note for other-currency accounts and an archived card's bill counted twice. Minor findings left as they are (stated by the reviewer): a repeating expense that pays a card bill is counted next to the card's owed balance, savings accounts of type BANK count as spendable (both explained in the dialog's estimate text); re-importing a line once recorded as a transfer does not turn it into spending, so a wrong transfer is deleted by hand; the passphrase-protected setting travels in the database but the key does not, so a restore onto a new phone stops protected copies until the passphrase is set again (the status says so).
- **Tests (this integration, all run by the integrator on the final tree):** host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`): domain 516, parsers 111, storage 369, 996 in all, 0 failures; the common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary (forced to rerun, fresh result files): domain 509, parsers 106, 0 failures; the shared module compiled for iosSimulatorArm64 and iosArm64. Android device suite (the three timing classes left out): 553 tests in 91 classes, one run of 46 minutes: 4 failures, 2 skipped (both in QuickAddWidgetTest); after rerunning the four classes together 3 times (35 of 35 each time) and the one new case alone 10 times (10 of 10), all four are classed flaky (see below). Release smoke test 7 of 7. iPhone simulator: 37 unit tests (including 7 AutoExportTests with Keychain) and 22 UI tests (ExpensesUITests 12, CaptureUITests 7, NotificationsUITests 1, BillPhotoUITests 2), all passing, every declared test name seen; `tools/ios_build.sh interop` ok for the files the iPhone wrote, including the protected copies (EXPENC3, plain payload version 1 and version 2 with a file) read by the Java reference.
- **Flaky in the full run (each passed on rerun):** `BackupFilesTest.forTheShareSheetFilesGo...` (out of memory in the 48 MB test heap, seen before in 0.7.0), `CategoriesTest.aDuplicateNameIsRefusedWhileTypingAndSaveStaysDisabled` and `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...` (both on the earlier known-flaky list) and `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest.clearSelectionLeavesNothingSelected...` ("2 selected" read "1 selected": the test long-presses a row and then taps another; first time seen; neither the Search screen nor the test changed in this release).
- **Save-path timing, old (825b814, the 0.7.1 commit) against new (this tree), the same method as the 0.7.0 check:** the debug and test APKs of each variant installed in alternation, 3 rounds, on a rebooted emulator, RefreshBudgetTest and PerformanceBudgetTest each time. The emulator was in its fast state in 2 rounds per variant and in its slow state in 1 (as in 0.7.0: in the slow state the save-path figures were about 5 times higher for both variants, so rounds in different states cannot be compared). Fast state, medians of 2 runs per variant, new over old: save to new row and total p50 3.55 against 4.15 ms (0.86), p95 7.05 against 7.55; rest of the screen p50 13.8 against 14.0, p95 24.9 against 30.6; whole save path p50 3.71 against 3.78, p95 7.94 against 8.42; one insert p50 0.53 against 0.54; account balances at 100,000 entries 25.3 against 25.6; the whole-ledger search 145.7 against 145.8; category totals for 10 years 50.6 against 48.3 (+5%). In the one slow round the pairs were within 9 to 20% on the save path p50s (new 21.2 against 19.4 ms, whole save 21.8 against 18.2) with p95s in the other direction (35 against 50 ms, 44 against 66 ms); one pair cannot separate that from the spread seen in 0.7.0 (9 rounds). Verdict: no measurable save-path regression. Budget pass/fail is the emulator's state, not the variant: both variants passed in the fast rounds. MONEY measured its own additions to the refresh at about 0.15 ms (p50) against a rebuild of about 42 ms, on a 20,000-entry ledger.
- **Not verified:** the Keychain and the Android Keystore on a real phone (including a Keychain item surviving a reinstall or a free-signing renewal, and the state before the first unlock after a restart); a real Storage Access Framework folder for protected copies; real bank statements and real exports from Money Manager, YNAB, Splitwise, Cashew and Spendee (every fixture is invented from the documented layouts); real bank texts (the redactor and bank rules were run on the repository's corpus and on invented messages); that the redacted report mailed from a real phone arrives and reads well; the new Compose screens on the iPhone simulator beyond the existing UI tests (the safe-to-spend, reimbursement and report screens were exercised on the Android emulator); TalkBack and VoiceOver on the new screens; timing on a phone.

## Slice 91: own-account transfers, narration titles, more categories, subscription finder (version 0.9.0)
Four pieces of work on the owner's real HDFC messages (two texts for one move of money between his two accounts were recorded as an expense and an income). Released as Android 0.9.0 (code 31) and iPhone 0.9.0 (build 29). This was first written as "Slice 91, not yet in a build" and is the only section for this work; the integration notes are at its end. All texts, names, amounts and digits in tests are invented (1234 is the source account, 5678 the destination).
- **Own-account transfers.** The two texts become ONE card, "Transfer from <A> to <B> ₹X", with Add (one transfer entry, both texts kept as its origin) and "These are separate" (two items, remembered in a setting; never paired again). Signals: the credit naming the source account's last four digits (`XXXXXXXXXX1234-TPT-...`, or the debit naming the destination's: `To A/c xxxxxxxxxx5678`, `to a/c **5678`) within 24 hours, or the same exact amount in opposite directions on two different known accounts within 30 minutes with transfer wording (Money Transfer, Fund Transfer, TPT, FT-, own account, self, IMPS, NEFT, RTGS). Both accounts must be known by the digits on them (or the account remembered for that bank and digits); with none set the cards ask for the last four digits. A lone text says "Might be a transfer to your other account" only when its digits name another own account or its wording says transfer and exactly one other account exists. When the first half was already added as an expense or income, the second card offers "Make this a transfer: it matches the earlier ₹X expense from <A>": the earlier entry is soft-deleted and one transfer added in one transaction, "Made it a transfer · Undo" restores the entry, deletes the transfer and puts the text back. Nothing is added without a tap. Pure matching in `TransferPairs` (domain), clues in `TransferClues` (parsers; `counterLast4` is also on `ParsedAlert`), the scan in `TransferScan` (shared, also used by an iPhone test entry), the writes in `CaptureTransfers` (storage), the card in `CaptureScreens.kt`. Card payments and ATM withdrawals keep their own handling. New testTags: `inbox_transfer_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_add_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_apart_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_why_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_show_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_text_out_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_text_in_<id>`, `inbox_transfer_hint_<id>`, `inbox_setup_digits_<id>`, `inbox_convert_note_<id>`, `inbox_convert_<id>`; `subscription_when_<name>`, `subscription_last_<name>`, `subscription_price_<name>`. Existing tags unchanged.
- **Found and fixed: a double count with statements.** A statement import did not know a captured transfer: the source account's debit line and the destination's credit line were imported as an expense and an income on top of it (proved by failing tests first). An ordinary row is now matched to a transfer already in the app (same amount, within a day, any source but another statement), once per side.
- **Narrations as titles.** `PayeeGuess` reads a bank's narration (`XXXXXXXXXX1234-TPT-note-NAME`, `Info: UPI-NAME-handle-ref`, `IMPS-ref-NAME`, `NEFT Cr-IFSC-remitter-holder-ref`, `ACH D- BILLER`, `SI-BILLER`, IDFC `NEFT/ref/NAME`, `ATM-CASH`) and gives the person or note, through `StatementDescriptions.tidy` (reused; it now also knows TPT, FT, SI and a leading masked account) and `NarrationTitle`. The raw text is untouched. SMS 1 has no payee ("HDFC Bank"); SMS 2's title becomes the typed note. On the 152-message corpus exactly 8 payee readings changed (null to a name), no direction, amount, date, reference, account or classification changed (details in OUR_FEATURES.md section 19). `ATM-CASH` is read as a cash withdrawal.
- **More starter categories.** A new install gets 15 more expense categories (Rent, Utilities, Internet and mobile, Insurance, Education, Fuel, Travel, Subscriptions, Investments, Loan EMI, Personal care, Gifts and donations, Taxes, Fees and charges, Cash withdrawal; after the original eight, which stay where they were) and 4 more income ones (Dividends, Rent received, Reimbursement, Freelance and business). An install that exists keeps exactly what it has; Suggested categories offers all of them (and the income ones) in one step. Restoring an older backup onto a new install takes the untouched newer ones away again. 150+ merchants have a better home in them (Netflix to Subscriptions, Indian Oil to Fuel, BESCOM to Utilities ...) used only when the person has that category, the old category is the fallback, so no existing ledger changes unless it asks; about 100 new merchants. Investments (SIPs) is a category, not a transfer (documented in `StarterCategories`). A bank's own charge words suggest Fees and charges; cash at an ATM with no cash account suggests Cash withdrawal.
- **Subscriptions.** The finder now finds every 28 days (a Jio plan was read as monthly, 12 a year instead of 13), every 12 weeks, half-yearly, a price step of any size (75 to 99 after 8 of 14 charges was missed), one service written several ways (APPLE.COM/BILL, AMZN PRIME), and one merchant billing several prices (Apple), tolerates a skipped cycle and a double charge, and says "Price went up from ₹75" and "Last charged ₹99 on 12 Oct" with the next date. Remind me makes a repeating item for every cadence. Autopay and mandate texts: "to be debited" (filed as a debit) and "Auto-pay of Rs.199 for NETFLIX is scheduled on ..." (ignored) now go to Bills; "Auto-pay ... was successful" is a debit.
- **Tests.** Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`): domain 541, parsers 124, storage 385 (1,050), 0 failures; the common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary (forced rerun): domain 534, parsers 119, 0 failures; the iPhone framework compiles for the simulator; iPhone unit tests (`tools/ios_build.sh unit`) 39, 0 failures, including the two new `OwnTransferTests`. Device (API 26 emulator): the whole suite once, 578 tests, 17 failures: eight `PerformanceBudgetTest` and one `RefreshBudgetTest` (timing classes, run at the end of a one-hour run; all passed when the seven classes were rerun alone), `BackupFilesTest` (out of memory in the 48 MB test heap, known), `AccountPeriodUiTest.searchThisAccount...` (known flaky), `QuickEntryTest` (passed on rerun), `AlertsUiTest.theWeekLineAlsoCountsRepeating...` (fails again on rerun, by its own date arithmetic: it builds a tracked bill's due date in the current month while the card's due day, three days ahead, has rolled into the next month; today was 29 Oct; not related to this work), and four real fallouts of the longer default lists (next paragraph). After those fixes the changed and text-ingesting classes passed: `OwnTransferUiTest` 6, `SubscriptionsUiTest` 5, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest` 3, with `PairingUiTest`, `InboxUiTest` and `DuplicatesUiTest` 43 tests in all, 0 failures; the other classes that ingest texts passed in the whole run. New: `TransferPairsTest`, `OwnTransferTextsTest`, `CapturePipelineTest`, `MandateTextsTest`, `CaptureTransfersTest`, `StatementAfterCapturedTransferTest`, `StarterCategoriesTest`, `MerchantHomesTest`, `CategoryHintsTest`, `SubscriptionDetectionTest` (host), `OwnTransferUiTest` (device), `OwnTransferTests` (iPhone, `IosTransferDebug`).
- **Fallout of 23 default categories on the device tests (fixed in the tests):** lists are longer, so tests that tapped a row that is no longer on screen or counted "8 chips" now scroll or count from `StarterCategories` (`CategoryKindsUiTest`, `CategoryPickerUiTest`, `IncomeCategoriesUiTest`, `CategoriesTest`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`).
- **Not verified:** real HDFC traffic; any other bank's own-account transfer wording (only HDFC's public sample shapes were used); a real phone. (The note that `AlertsUiTest.theWeekLineAlsoCountsRepeating...` fails near a month's end described the test as first written; it now dates the card's due day the way the product does, and passed in the integration run.)

### Slice 91 follow-up: the third independent review (REVIEW3)
- **Subscriptions (major).** The first version of the finder invented rows for ordinary frequent merchants (the review measured 331 of 400 profiles of "8 delivery or shopping merchants with 30-180 orders a year", 60% of "3 merchants, 180 orders in 400 days", 16% of "12 orders a year at one app", 10% of "6 merchants at round prices", 105 rows on a 20,000-entry ledger; the finder before the work, none). Now: three charges for a whole merchant (four per price of a merchant that bills several, unless it is a known recurring service), two charges only for a yearly pattern with a known service or the same price of 200 rupees or more with nothing between, a merchant charged more than six times in 30 days is never one unless it is a known service, gaps must keep the pattern with a skipped cycle allowed once, ATM cash excluded, "Not one" stored by merchant and cadence, the price note only after the new price repeats. Monthly is 27 to 34 days (February is fine) and told from 28-day plans by the day of the month. `SubscriptionPrecisionTest` measures 400 fixed-seed profiles of each kind: 0 false rows in every one (the numbers are printed as `PRECISION ...` lines); the 14-month true-positive table (`SubscriptionDetectionTest`) is still found.
- **Make this a transfer.** Undo now frees the deleted transfer's bank reference (the restored text could not be added or converted again); two texts carrying the same UPI or IMPS reference convert under "<reference>/transfer"; the earlier entry's note and tags carry over and an entry with an attachment or several categories is not offered; the card says the Undo lasts until the screen is left (it lives in memory).
- **Pairing.** Real transfer wording only (the rails IMPS, NEFT, RTGS no longer count, and a lone salary credit gets no hint); a best one-to-one assignment instead of closest-first; the parser takes the account money left (debit) or reached (credit) as the text's own account, so "sent to A/c 9876 via IMPS from A/c XX1234" is read right (the corpus is unchanged: no field of any of the 152 messages differs from before this follow-up).
- **Statement import.** A same-amount line is the transfer's side only when its words read like a transfer, it names the other account's digits, or the file holds the other side's row; a real same-amount payment imports.
- **Restore.** A backup category with the same name and kind as an untouched, unused newer starter replaces it on any device; a used starter or one with a rule on it stays.

### Slice 91 integration (version 0.9.0, run by the integrator on the final tree)
- **Schema and backup:** no new table, column or backup key. New queries only (`transferCandidates`, `transfersOutOfLikeStatementRow`, `transfersIntoLikeStatementRow`, `reopenConfirmedInbox`, `clearSourceRef`); the backup file format is unchanged. Restoring an older backup onto a phone with untouched, unused newer starter categories removes those starters in favour of the backup's same-named category (behaviour change in `BackupStore`, tested). A new install seeds 15 more expense categories (23 in all) and 4 more income ones; an existing install keeps what it has and is offered the rest in Suggested categories.
- **Independent review (REVIEW3), all findings fixed before this integration:** the new subscription finder invented rows (measured by the reviewer: false rows in 331 of 400 profiles of 8 busy merchants, 60% of 3-merchant, 16% of one-app, 10% of round-price profiles, 105 rows on a 20,000-entry ledger; the pre-change finder: none); after the fix the worker's `SubscriptionPrecisionTest` prints 0 false rows in 400 profiles of every kind and the 14-month true-positive table is still found (the "before" figures are the reviewer's, not re-measured). Also fixed: Undo of "Make this a transfer" left the bank reference taken so the text could never be added again; two texts with the same UPI/IMPS reference were refused on conversion; a statement row of a real same-amount payment could be swallowed as a transfer's twin; greedy pairing; parser own/counter digits when the destination is named first; monthly charges across February missed; a price note after one odd charge; a lone IMPS/NEFT salary credit hinted as a transfer. Left as the reviewer described them (minor, not fixed): restore onto a phone with even one entry can leave duplicate starter names; new merchants fall back to "Fun"/"Transport" for existing users who lack the newer categories; a captured/hand-typed transfer can match a real same-amount debit when the statement file lacks the transfer's own row (reported to the person as already there); "ATM-CASH" is a spending expense when there is no Cash account.
- **Tests (all run by the integrator on the final tree):** host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`): domain 558, parsers 126, storage 395, 1,079 in all, 0 failures, no stale result files (UiConventionsTest ran); common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary, forced rerun: domain 551, parsers 121, 0 failures, fresh XML; the shared module compiled for iosSimulatorArm64 and iosArm64. Android device suite (timing classes left out): 565 tests in 92 classes, 22 minutes, 4 failures, 2 skipped (both `QuickAddWidgetTest`): `AccountPeriodUiTest.searchThisAccount...`, `BackupFilesTest` (two tests), `SearchDatesUiTest.pickingFromAndTo...` (first time seen: "the 10th alone" assertion; the test and `SearchScreen.kt` are unchanged in this release; 12 of 12 alone and 3 of 3 class reruns passed); the other three are on the known-flaky list; the three classes together passed 2 of 3 reruns (the third failed only the known `AccountPeriodUiTest` case). No category-list fallout beyond the five classes the worker already adapted. Release smoke test 7 of 7, on the APK built before and again on the final 0.9.0 APK. iPhone simulator: 39 unit tests, all declared names passed; UI classes one at a time: CaptureUITests 7, NotificationsUITests 1, BillPhotoUITests 2 passed; ExpensesUITests failed its first test once (`testAddingAnExpenseShowsTheRowAndTheMonthTotal`, "note did not take focus"), then passed 3 of 3 alone and the whole class passed 12 of 12 on a rerun; `tools/ios_build.sh interop` ok for all six files.
- **Save-path timing, old (dcdf2b6, 0.8.0) against new (this tree):** debug and test APKs installed in alternation, rebooted emulator, 2 batches of 3 rounds (6 runs per variant) of RefreshBudgetTest and PerformanceBudgetTest. The emulator stayed in its slow state in all 12 runs (about 5 times slower than its fast state, as seen in 0.7.0 and 0.8.0; machine load average 5 to 8, up to 24 in one run), so only slow-state figures exist and the gates failed on their limits for both variants. Medians over the 6 runs, ms, old then new (ratio): save to new row and total p50 21.7 / 21.1 (0.97), p95 61 / 46 (0.76); rest of the screen p50 83.5 / 80.8 (0.97), p95 178 / 155 (0.87); one insert p50 2.08 / 2.10 (1.01); whole save path p50 18.7 / 19.9 (1.07), p95 41.9 / 48.0 (1.15, new range 39.7 to 144); balances at 100,000 entries 103.9 / 106.9 (1.03); whole-ledger search 644 / 640 (1.00); category totals for 10 years 228 / 213 (0.93). The per-round pairs for the rest of the screen p50 were 1.11, 1.72, 1.03, 0.86, 0.98, 0.82. Verdict: no measurable save-path regression, within the spread of this state; the fast state was not available for a finer comparison.
- **Throwaway timing on a 20,000-entry ledger (slow-state emulator, the same test run on both commits; deleted afterwards):** building To review with 50 waiting texts (`inboxRows`, includes the transfer scan): old 1,886 ms with the scan skipped (one movable account) and 1,912 ms with two accounts; new 1,883 and 1,971 ms (20 pairs found, 30 rows). The transfer scan itself therefore costs about 60 to 90 ms at 50 waiting texts (about 3%); the rest, about 1.9 s in this state, is the same on the old commit (not investigated; it is not the transfer scan). The subscription finder screen (3 monthly services over 20,000 other entries): old 135 ms, new 180 ms (p50 of 7 loads).
- **iPhone signing:** `tools/ios_refresh.sh --force-new-profile` was tried with the phone connected: it moved the cached profiles aside, the build failed with "No Accounts" (Xcode has no Apple ID signed in, so it cannot make new profiles), and the script put the three cached profiles back (the SHA-256 of all three files is identical before and after). The plain signed build and install were used; the profiles inside the 0.9.0 IPA are the cached ones (created 2026-10-28, expire 2026-11-04). The owner has to sign in under Xcode > Settings > Accounts before a forced renewal can work.
- **Not verified:** real HDFC traffic and any other bank's own-account wording; a real phone running the transfer cards, conversion with Undo, the new subscription finder on real history, the starter-category offer on an existing install; a real Keychain or SAF folder; the timing gates in the fast state of the emulator and on a phone; why To review takes about 1.9 s for 50 waiting texts on a 20,000-entry ledger in the slow emulator state.

## Slice 92: change the type of an entry (after version 0.9.0)

The owner had old expenses that were really cash withdrawals or money moved between his own accounts, and the edit form showed only the entry's own type.

- **What it does.** The edit form of an expense, income or transfer now has the same Expense / Income / Transfer chips as the Add form (tags `kind_EXPENSE`, `kind_INCOME`, `kind_TRANSFER`). Under them, while the chosen type is not the saved one, a note says what saving does (`kind_change_note`): "Saving changes this expense into a transfer. You can undo it right after." When an expense is switched to Transfer and the person has exactly one Cash account (and the expense was not paid from it) that account is preselected as the "To" account and the note says so; a split expense also says "The category split will be removed." Saving with the type unchanged is the old save, untouched. Not offered for a refund or lent/borrowed entry (no chips there) or for an entry that is read-only (shared, foreign currency, has refunds).
- **How it is done (`core/storage/KindChanges.kt`).** A ledger entry's kind still never changes in place (`LedgerStore.save` still refuses it). One all-or-nothing database step soft-deletes the old entry and saves a new one of the new kind (new id), the same swap as "Make this a transfer": date, account, title, note, tags, source and bank reference carry over, the original text (`entry_origin`) is copied to the new id, and the attachments (bill photos) are moved to it with one logged `entry_id` change each. The bank reference passes to the new entry (the unique index on source and reference covers deleted rows), so a text delivered again is still seen as "already in your ledger"; Undo hands it back. Nothing is learned from a type change (no capture rule, no last-used category or account).
- **Undo.** "Changed to a transfer / an expense / income · Undo" on Home's bar: the new entry is deleted (it lets go of the reference and the origin, its attachments go back) and the original is restored with the same id and every field; its history reads Created ... Deleted, Brought back. The bar lives in memory like every other Undo bar.
- **Refused, nothing changes (the message appears under the chips: `kind_change_problem`).** An expense with "I expect this back" (open or closed): "Turn off \"I expect this back\" first: save the expense with it off, then change its type." An entry marked as the payment of a bill: "This entry is marked as the payment of a bill, so its type can't be changed here." An entry a repeating item added: "A repeating item added this entry, so its type can't be changed here." Also (not reachable from the form, which shows these read-only): refunds, shares, foreign currency. A split expense becoming income or a transfer is allowed and says so in the note (Undo brings the parts back).
- **Flipping through the chips loses nothing.** Going back to the saved type restores its category, split and accounts. An income that becomes a transfer has its account as the "To" (the money arrived there), a transfer that becomes income is received in its "To" account; the "I expect this back" switch is not shown for an income or transfer made an expense.
- **Bulk (Home and Search selection): "Make transfer" > account.** Every selected plain expense becomes a transfer from the account it was paid from to the chosen one, all in one step with one Undo; skipped with the reason named: not an expense, already on that account, or one of the refusals above. `EntryActions.bulkMakeTransfer`, `LedgerViewModel.bulkMakeTransfers`.
- **Tests.** `KindChangesTest` (host, 15): every pair of kinds against balances, spending and income, exact Undo (rows, splits, tags, origins, attachments), references through change, Undo, ordinary edit and change again, history, refusals, a rejected entry changing nothing, splits. `ChangeTypeUiTest` (device, 17).

## Slice 93: a reference on a changed entry is a duplicate, and Transfer candidates (after Slice 92)

Two pieces. All texts, names and amounts in tests are invented.

### A. A text for a transaction whose entry was changed is a duplicate, not a reversal
- **The bug (found by review, then proved).** `LedgerViewModel.confirmCapture` and the To review hint decided "refund or reversal" from the *kind of the entry now holding the reference*. After Slice 92 an expense from a debit text can be made income; the same debit arriving again (another channel, same bank reference) then looked like the opposite direction, was saved under `<reference>/reversal` as a second expense, and the pending row said "looks like its refund or reversal, not a repeat". Undo of the change then left the original and the `/reversal` one both live: a double count.
- **The rule (`core/domain/ReferenceReuse.kt`, pure).** The entry keeps its original words (`entry_origin`, carried through a change). `core/parsers/OriginDirection.outgoing(sender, text, email)` reads which way money went in them with the capture rules (null when unreadable). A transfer is the same transaction whichever way a text points. An entry whose kind no longer matches the direction of its original words was changed (or its text was turned by the person): any text with the reference is the SAME transaction (refused as "already in your ledger"). Only an entry left as it was can be undone by an opposite text (expense + credit text, income + debit text). With no readable words the kind stands for the direction (the old rule). **Safest choice and why:** no schema change, nothing stored, works after a restore (origins are in backups; the inbox is not); the cost is that a *genuine* refund text for a converted entry is refused as a duplicate and must be added by hand, which can be seen and fixed, whereas the other error is a silent double count. The hint text on the pending row is computed by the same function, so it always matches the decision.
- **Tests.** `ReferenceReuseTest` (19 combinations of kind, original direction and text direction: same direction, genuine reversal both ways, expense to income, income to expense, transfer, unreadable words), `OriginDirectionTest` (3: bank texts, payment apps, emails with a safety notice, OTP/declined/blank give null), device `DuplicatesUiTest` +4: expense changed to income then the debit again is "Already in your ledger" and cannot be added, and after Undo of the change exactly one expense remains; income changed to expense then the credit again; genuine reversal of untouched income still offered and added; a text with no reference is never called a reversal.

### B. Transfer candidates (More, Transfer candidates)
The owner has many old entries that are really transfers (ATM withdrawals, moves between his own accounts, self UPI). The screen finds them and converts them in one step with one Undo. It never converts anything by itself.
- **Finder (`core/domain/TransferCandidates.kt`, pure; `core/storage/TransferCandidateStore.kt` reads).** Groups: *Cash withdrawals* (expense from a bank account, wallet or credit card, whose title, note or original text says an ATM withdrawal: `TransferWording.readsAsAtmWithdrawal`, which shares the capture parser's wording regex (moved out of `KindRules`, which now calls it) and adds the statement codes `NWD-`/`AWL-` and titles that are only "ATM", "Cash", "ATM cash", "Cash withdrawal"); *Moves between your own accounts* (an expense out of one bank account or wallet and an income into another, same amount, `TransferPairs.basis` decides exactly as the live pairing does: transfer wording and 30 minutes, or a text naming the other account's digits and a day; best one-to-one assignment; neither half is also offered alone); *Transfers to yourself* (one entry whose words say self transfer, to or from self, own account, my account, own name, own VPA: `TransferWording.saysSelfTransfer`, stricter than `says`). Excluded: anything `KindChanges.refusal` refuses (shared, foreign currency, refunds, bill payments, repeating items, "I expect this back"), entries on the Cash account, refund or reversal wording, answered "Not a transfer", and a refused half never takes another half's place in a pair.
- **Decision: a cash advance on a credit card IS offered** in the Cash withdrawals group (reason "Reads as a cash advance on a card"): the money moves from the card to the person's cash, and spending the cash later is recorded as spending, so leaving the advance an expense counts it twice.
- **Cost.** Two queries (`transferWordingRows`: a coarse lower-case LIKE prefilter over title, note and original text, one scan; `transferPairRows`: income joined to expenses of the same amount in a 36-hour index range on `occurred_at`), then the pure judgement. Only the survivors are asked `KindChanges.refusal`. Measured on the JVM (in-memory SQLite, invented 20,070 entries, 200 withdrawals, 30 moves, 10 self, 800 false friends): first read 90 ms, second 87 ms; worst case (5 distinct amounts, so every income has many same-amount expenses near it): 54 ms. It runs under the write lock off the main thread, only when the screen is opened; **nothing runs on Home or on a save**, and there is no count on Home or More (the Subscriptions row has none either). Each query is bounded to 20,000 rows, newest first.
- **No schema change, no new table.** "Not a transfer" is one setting per entry (`transfer_no:<entry id>` = 1), device-local like the subscription finder's "not a subscription" (not in backups). For a pair both entries are marked. "Show the N you marked as not transfers" clears them all.
- **Converting (`EntryActions.makeCandidateTransfers`, generalising `bulkMakeTransfer`, which now calls the same code with the same messages).** Each entry is replaced by a transfer through `KindChanges` (date, title, note, tags, source, bank reference, original text and attachments carry over). An expense goes from its own account to the chosen one; an income (self group) from the chosen account into its own. A pair: the expense becomes a transfer between the two accounts and the income is deleted (soft, restorable), so the move counts once and both balances stay the same. All in one `atomically`; skipped entries are counted with the reasons (an entry used twice in one batch, not an expense or income, already on that account, every refusal of Change type, "the matching income does not match any more"). One Undo (kept in memory like the other screen Undo bars): the kind changes are undone last-first and the deleted incomes restored.
- **Screen (`ui/TransferCandidatesScreen.kt`).** Grouped, scrollable list (one pane, centred like Subscriptions on a wide window). Each row: tick box (the whole row is one toggle for a screen reader), title, date, "Main bank → Cash" (for an income "Savings → Main bank"), the reason, the amount (masking `Text`). Per group: "Move the money to" / "Other account" picker (all active accounts except loans), "Select all / Clear all", "Make N transfers", "Not a transfer" for the ticked rows; per row "Not a transfer". Ticked on arrival only for Cash withdrawals and only when there is exactly one Cash account; with several or none nothing is ticked and Make stays disabled until an account is chosen; with none "Create a Cash account" asks first (dialog "Create a Cash account?", "Create Cash" / "Not now") and then uses the ordinary `saveAccount` path. Notice after an action: "Made 2 transfers." / "Made 1 transfer, 1 skipped: <reason>." / "Marked 1 as not a transfer. They will not be suggested again." / "Put back 2 entries as they were."; empty state "Nothing found."; "Looking through your entries…" while it reads.
- **Tags.** `open_transfer_candidates` (More, Money), `transfer_candidates_back`, `_list`, `_loading`, `_empty`, `_notice`, `_group_<cash|own|self>`, `_title_<group>`, `_row_<entry id>` (the tick box row), `_check_<id>`, `_title_<id>`, `_route_<id>`, `_amount_<id>`, `_not_<id>`, `_dest_<group>`, `_to_<group>_<account name>`, `_all_<group>`, `_make_<group>`, `_not_selected_<group>`, `_show_dismissed`, `_create_cash`, `_create_cash_confirm`, `_create_cash_cancel`.
- **Tests.** Host: `TransferCandidatesTest` (16: every ATM wording variant, false friends (ATMOS cafe, Batman store, Treatment centre, ATM Bakery, Cash and Carry, Cashify, a debit/ATM card used in a shop, "ATM" in boilerplate, reversals, the Cash account, loans, income, zero), card advance, refused and dismissed, newest first, pairs (wording, 30 minutes against a named account and a day, arrival time, same account, card or loan, refused half, one-for-one), self wording and look-alikes (Self Help Group, Myself Foods, Selfie Studio, Money Transfer to a person), and a whole adversarial set), `TransferCandidateStoreTest` (6: a real ledger, "Not a transfer" persistence, change and Undo, currency, 20,000 entries, worst-case amounts). Device `TransferCandidatesUiTest` (13): opens from More with the group, the preselection, the false friends absent; make transfers with balances and spending checked and one Undo; untick; dismiss, reopen the screen and the app, show again; dismiss selection; empty state; two Cash accounts and choosing; no Cash account and the confirmation; a pair becomes one transfer and Undo brings both back; same-amount pairs without wording or on different days are not offered; a self transfer; a skipped entry and the notice; a 1000 dp wide window is one pane. `AccessibilityUiTest.transferCandidates` (the screen and its open menu pass the framework's checks).
- **Precision on the invented adversarial set:** 0 false positives of about 840 look-alike entries (37 hand-written look-alikes across the cash, self and pair tests, and 800 in the 20,000-entry ledger, from 8 false-friend names).
- **Also fixed:** `tools/ios_build.sh` picked its simulator by name and failed with "ambiguous destination" because two simulators are called iPhone 17; it now resolves the id of the first booted (else first available) iPhone 17 on iOS 26.5 and passes `id=`.
- **Run on the final tree:** host JVM domain 576, parsers 129, storage 423 (1,128), 0 failures (UiConventionsTest ran); common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary, forced rerun: domain 569, parsers 124, 0 failures; `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles; iPhone unit tests 39, 0 failures. Device (API 26 emulator): TransferCandidatesUiTest 13/13, DuplicatesUiTest 12/12, ChangeTypeUiTest 19/19, BulkEditTest 8/8, OwnTransferUiTest 9/9, PairingUiTest 7/7, HistoryUiTest 3/3, SubscriptionsUiTest 5/5, MoreMenuUiTest 4/4, WidePaneUiTest 6/6, InboxUiTest 14/14, AccessibilityUiTest 9/10, SearchBulkCategoryUiTest 5/6. The two failures (`AccessibilityUiTest.theChecksReallyRunAndReportAKnownBadControl`, `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest.doneOnTheKeyboardPutsItAwayAndLeavesTheResultsShown`) fail the same way on an untouched checkout of b50ec32 on this emulator (both depend on the input method and the framework's scan, not on this work).
- **Not verified:** any real bank's ATM or own-account wording beyond the invented samples (the lists are the capture parser's plus the statement codes NWD and AWL, which come from the owner's description and were not checked against a real statement); a real phone; the screen on a real history of tens of thousands of entries (only the invented 20,000-entry timing on the JVM); how long it takes on the phone's encrypted SQLCipher database (the JVM numbers are not a prediction for it).

### Slice 93 review fixes (REVIEW of Transfer candidates)
- **Other half already in the ledger (P2-1).** A lone row (cash withdrawal or self transfer) is no longer offered, and is skipped at the button, when its other half is a live entry: an entry holding its bank reference plus `/reversal` (a failed withdrawal's money coming back), or a live entry of the opposite kind for the same amount on another account within 36 hours (the credit on the destination of a self transfer that was captured too late to pair). `TransferCandidateStore.otherHalfLive` (one new query, `transferOtherHalfNear`, an index range); `TransferCandidates.find(otherHalfLive = ...)`; `EntryActions.makeTransfers` re-checks at conversion time for Transfer candidates only (Search/Home "Make transfer" is unchanged). Skip reason: "its matching money-in is already in your ledger" (money-out for an income).
- **Fee, wallet and limit texts (P2-2).** `TransferWording.readsAsAtmWithdrawal` (the one list) now refuses text with charges, chgs, fees or GST, a wallet's own "withdrawn from your Paytm Wallet to bank account", and strips "cash withdrawal limit" boilerplate before reading; a bare "withdrawn" counts only on bank and card accounts. The live capture's `saysAtmWithdrawal` is unchanged.
- **Orphans (P2-3).** A pair whose income has an attachment is not offered and is skipped at the button ("the matching income has attachments, which would be left behind"). The income's tags are added to the transfer (each once, whatever the capitals, up to 10), and its note is carried when the transfer has none (`KindChanges.change(fallbackNote)`). Its original text stays on the deleted row (kept for Undo, not shown anywhere else). Undo restores both entries with their own tags and notes (device test).
- **Backup (P2-7).** `docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md` now says pair conversions are outside the "nothing is doubled" guarantee (restoring an older backup after the newer brings the absorbed income back) and to restore only the newest backup or onto an empty ledger; `KindChangeBackupTest` asserts the behaviour. No format change.
- **Self wording (P3-4).** "to self" / "from self" must end there ("Paid to Self Drive Cars", "Self Help Group" no longer match); "my account" and "my name" are no longer self wording ("own account", "own name", "own VPA", "my VPA" still are).
- **Undo bar (P3-5).** "Not a transfer", "Show the N you marked", creating Cash and a Make that changes nothing keep the bar of the batch before. A second successful Make while the bar is up is added to the first: one Undo puts back everything made since the page opened, and the bar says "Undo puts back all N made so far."
- **Thin pairs (P3-6).** A pair of two ledger entries needs transfer wording on BOTH halves, or both accounts named in the texts, and is never made when either half reads as an ATM withdrawal ("ATM cash" 5,000 and "Transfer from RAVI KUMAR" 5,000 twenty minutes later on another account is not a pair).
- **Tests.** Host: domain 581, parsers 129, storage 429 (1,139), 0 failures (`--rerun-tasks`); native release (forced): domain 574, parsers 124; iOS compile OK; iOS unit 39. Device: TransferCandidatesUiTest 18/18 (5 new), DuplicatesUiTest 12/12, ChangeTypeUiTest 19/19, BulkEditTest 8/8, PairingUiTest 7/7, OwnTransferUiTest 9/9.


### Release 0.10.0 (Android code 33, iPhone build 30): integration
Slices 92 and 93 (change an entry's type, Transfer candidates, duplicate rule for converted entries) plus their review fixes. The release APK passed `tools/release_smoke.sh` 7/7 (after a cold boot of the emulator: the first run hit a "System UI isn't responding" dialog, unrelated to the app). The Play bundle (`bundlePlaySms`) has RECEIVE_SMS and no INTERNET; the iPhone test IPA is 0.10.0 (30) built with the cached profiles (expire 2026-11-05T10:12Z). Builds are in `~/Documents/personal/expenses_builds/` (the Desktop was not writable from the shell this time). The phone was not connected, so the IPA was built but not installed or run.

**Bulk bar: Delete was pushed out of sight (found by the full device run).** The first full device run on 4400fd4 (607 tests) had 4 failures: 3 on the known-flaky list (not touched by this release) and `BillsUiTest.aDeletedPaymentUnpaysItsBill...`, which was bisected with single-test runs on the API26 emulator: it passes 4/4 on 0.9.0 and 2/2 on b72c184, and fails 2/2 on 4691a7a and 3/3 on 4400fd4 (the full run, a class run and a single-test run). Cause: 4691a7a added the "Make transfer" button to the bulk bar's sideways-scrolling second row *before* "Delete", so on a phone (the emulator is 393dp, an iPhone 17 is 402pt) Delete sat past the edge (its bounds were 0,0,0,0, so a tap did nothing) while the test tapped it without scrolling. A real product regression (Delete is the action people expect to see), not only a test problem. Fix: `Bulk.kt` puts Delete before "Make transfer" (order: Set category, Account, Tag, Delete, Make transfer), so the rarest action is the one that may scroll; testTags unchanged, and Home and Search share the one `BulkBar`. On a 360dp screen (the emulator's density set to 480 for one run) Delete and Cancel are whole on screen on Home; the Search half of that test could not be run at 360dp (the keyboard leaves no room for results there), but it is the same composable. New device test `BulkEditTest.deleteIsWithinReachWithoutScrollingTheBarOnHomeAndOnSearchWhenExpensesAreSelected` asserts Delete is displayed and wholly on screen with no scrolling when an expense is selected (it fails with "bulk_delete is not displayed" on the old order and passes on the new one). `BillsUiTest.tap` now scrolls a node into view before the click, like the other test classes. Other new controls in 4691a7a/4400fd4 (read from the diff, not measured at 360dp): the Transfer candidates action buttons sit in a `FlowRow` (they wrap), its "Move the money to" row is a label and one button in a plain `Row` (text wraps, nothing is pushed off), and the form's kind chips are in a `FlowRow`.
Re-run after the fix (API26 emulator, 393dp): BillsUiTest 18/18, BulkEditTest 9/9, ChangeTypeUiTest 19/19, SearchBulkCategoryUiTest 6/6, SearchUiTest 8/8, TransferCandidatesUiTest 18/18, HomeListTest 1/1, AccessibilityUiTest 10/10 (89 tests, 0 failures). Host: domain 581, storage 429 (including UiConventionsTest), parsers 129, 0 failures; iOS simulator compile OK. The release APK and `bundlePlaySms` were rebuilt from the fixed code (still code 33 / 0.10.0, as the earlier 4400fd4 bundle was never uploaded); `tools/release_smoke.sh --no-build` 7/7; the bundle has RECEIVE_SMS and no INTERNET; the iPhone test IPA was rebuilt (0.10.0, build 30) and still has not been installed on a phone.

## Slice 94: three Play tester reports (after version 0.10.0 was built; Play still has 0.9.0)

A tester on the Play build reported three problems. Each was reproduced, then fixed at its root. All texts, card digits, amounts, shops and phone numbers in tests and docs are invented, in the shape of the tester's.

### 1. An ICICI Bank card spend was not captured at all
