- **Which stage dropped it: the sender check.** The text came from `BP-ICICIT-S`. `SenderRules.headerRoot` reads that as the root `ICICIT` correctly (any two-letter prefix, any one-letter suffix, any case), but `BankRules` listed only `ICICIB` (ICICI's account alerts), so `CaptureClassifier` answered `NOT_ALLOWLISTED` before the words were read, and capture health counted it under "unlisted sender". The parser was not at fault: the corpus sample `icici/cc_spend.txt` has the same wording and parses. With the `ICICIT` line taken out again, 6 of the 9 new tests fail with "expected a Transaction" (checked).
- **Fix.** `BankRules.ALL` gets `BankRule("ICICIT", "ICICI Bank")` (one issuer, two headers, as HDFC has two). `PayeeGuess` learns ICICI's shop wording `... on 01-Nov-26 on SHOP. Avl Limit: ...` (the shop follows the date with "on", no "at"): without it the row would have shown the bank and no shop. The rule needs a date, then "on", then a name with no digits, ending at a full stop that ends the sentence (a dot inside, `AMAZON.IN`, stays) or at a limit word; "on account of ..." is not a shop.
- **Not done on purpose.** No prefix family ("anything starting ICICI"): a header like ICICIPRU (insurance) or a look-alike would then be read as the bank, and the allowlist is the privacy promise. A header the list misses is added by the person (see 3). `ICICIO` and `ICICIBK` are not on the list because nothing in the repo or the tester's report shows them; add a line (and a sample in `BankRulesTest`) when one is seen.
- **Tests.** Host: `IciciCardAlertTest` (9: the tester's shape with every field (debit, spend not limit, "INR", card digits not the digits after SMS BLOCK, date from the text, shop); nine forms of the header incl. lower case, no prefix, no suffix, padded; seven wordings (INR / Rs, "spent using" / "spent on", "on SHOP" / "at SHOP", Avl Limit / Avl Lmt / Available Limit / Available Credit Limit, dd-Mon-yy and dd-Mon-yyyy); the limit is never the amount, even placed first; shop shapes; refund and bill payment on the card keep their direction; OTP, "if not you" with no amount, activation notice, offer, statement notice, declined stay uncaptured; look-alike headers and a phone number are still refused and a person-added header still works; the same wording from HDFC, SBI Card, Axis and ICICIB headers reads as before), `BankRulesTest` (sample for ICICIT). Device: `CaptureTest.anIciciCardSpendFromTheTransactionHeaderIsCapturedWithTheSpendNotTheLimit` (captured, ICICI Bank, 48,235 paise, card 4417, date, counted under ICICIT).
- **Not verified:** real ICICI traffic (the wording is the one in the repo's corpus and the tester's report, not a live capture) and whether ICICI sends card alerts from other headers.

### 2. The widget's eye did nothing (amount stayed `₹●●●●`)
- **Root cause.** Both home-screen widgets read their figures once, in `provideGlance`. Glance 1.2.0 keeps a widget's composition alive for about 45 seconds after it draws (`SessionWorker` time-out options). An `update()` / `updateAll()` that finds it alive does not run `provideGlance` again: it only re-reads the Glance state and recomposes. The eye tap wrote `widget_show_total` and asked for an update, but a widget drawn in the last ~45 s was redrawn from the old, still masked data. The same staleness hit the spent-this-month figure after adding an entry. Not the cause (checked): the PendingIntents, the receiver declaration and the `hide_amounts` setting; the eye is drawn only when Hide amounts and the App lock are both off.
- **Reproduced** on the API 26 emulator with real widgets placed through the AppWidget service (the system "Create widget" dialog accepted from the test) and the eye clicked on the host view: before the fix the Today widget stayed `[Spent today, ₹••••, + Add]` and Quick add stayed `[Spent this month, ₹••••, ... Chai, ₹20.00]`.
- **Fix** (`QuickAddWidget.kt`, `TodayWidget.kt`): `refreshWidgets` now bumps a `REFRESHED` counter in every copy's Glance state before `updateAll`, and `reloadedOnRefresh` (used by both widgets) re-reads the database whenever the counter changes (the counter is read before the first load so a refresh in between is not lost). `TodayWidget` got a `PreferencesGlanceStateDefinition` (it had none, so nothing could trigger its redraw).
- **Design decided and documented:** a shown total stays shown until the eye is tapped again (the second tap hides it again, tested). No timer and no re-mask on screen lock: the person chose to show it on their own home screen, and a timer would hide it while they read. The App lock and the app's Hide amounts still mask it and take the eye away. With masking off the amount line shows (tested).
- **Tests.** `WidgetEyeTapTest` (4 on the emulator: Quick add masked, tap shows ₹500.00, second tap masks; Today widget masked, tap shows; a total already shown with the eye offering "Hide the amount"; Hide amounts keeps it masked with no eye). With the product change stashed the two tap tests fail and the other two pass. `QuickAddWidgetTest` 23 run, 2 skipped (unchanged): Android 8 has no `cmd appwidget grantbind` and no shell permission adoption, so `bindAppWidgetIdIfAllowed` returns false there. `WidgetEyeTapTest` gets around that by accepting the system dialog; it skips itself (assumption) when the dialog does not appear within 45 s, which happened in 3 of 4 early runs before the wait was raised.
- **Not verified:** a real launcher (One UI or other) and a real finger; the click is `performClick()` on the frame Glance wraps around the eye.

### 3. Trusted senders: no sign that a name was added
- **Cause.** The "Your bank's sender name" box (More, Capture) on both Android and iPhone was a multi-line text field and a "Save names" button that wrote the whole text to the setting `capture_extra_roots` and did nothing else: no list, no notice, no checks, the text stayed in the box.
- **Fix.** New `TrustedSenders` (core/parsers: `parse`, `normalise`, `add` with results Added / Duplicate / BuiltIn / Empty / Invalid, `remove`, `serialize`) and one shared composable `TrustedSendersEditor` used by both capture screens (all commonMain, nothing Android-only). The stored format is unchanged: one setting, new-line separated on writing; reading still accepts commas, semicolons and spaces from older versions, upper-cases and drops repeats. A pasted header (`ax-mybank-s`) is reduced to its root `MYBANK`. Nothing is rescanned when a name is removed; it just stops being read.
- **Strings.** Label `Sender name`; button `Add` (Done on the keyboard also adds); `Added NAME`; `Already added`; `ICICI Bank is already read` style for a built-in root; `Type a sender name first`; `Use 3 to 12 letters or digits, like MYBANK`; list heading `Added by you`; the chips' x has the description `Remove NAME` and a 48 dp target. A refused name stays in the box to be fixed; a successful add clears it.
- **TestTags.** Kept `capture_headers` (box), `capture_headers_save` (button); new `sender_chip_<NAME>`, `sender_remove_<NAME>`, `sender_added_notice`, `sender_input_error` (NAME is the upper-case root).
- **Tests.** Host `TrustedSendersTest` (5). Device `TrustedSendersUiTest` (6: add, add a pasted header, duplicate, empty, invalid, built-in, remove, persists after leaving the screen and after the activity is relaunched, old stored values show as chips, a text from the added sender is captured before removal and not after, the remove target is 48 dp).
- **Not verified:** iPhone behaviour was compile-checked and run through the iOS unit tests only (no iOS UI test of this box); `AccessibilityUiTest` never opens this screen, so the TalkBack audit does not cover the chips.

Slice 94 (the three Play tester reports) shipped as 0.10.1; 0.10.0 (code 33) is not reused because it may already have been uploaded. Changes: ICICI Bank card spend texts from the ICICIT header are read (and name the shop); the Today and Quick add widgets' eye now shows the amount at once; the trusted-senders box shows chips with remove, "Added NAME" and plain messages. Host: domain 581, storage 429, parsers 143, 0 failures; native release guard (forced) and iOS compile OK; iOS unit 39/39. **Full Android device suite** (timing classes PerformanceBudgetTest, RefreshBudgetTest, RecreateStressTest excluded; cold-booted API 26 emulator): 619 tests, 6 failed, 2 skipped (the `QuickAddWidgetTest` pair). Failures: `AccountPeriodUiTest.searchThisAccount...` (known flaky; failed again in 2 of 2 re-runs), `BackupFilesTest` restore double-tap (known; failed 1 of 2 re-runs) and share-sheet OOM (known; passed in both re-runs), `LoansUiTest.borrowing...` (known; passed in both re-runs), and two NOT on the known list that fail every time: `NotificationsTest.aCreditCardBillWithSomethingLeftIsRemindedInTheBillsChannel...` ("Expected <1>, actual <2>") and `SearchDatesUiTest.pickingFromAndToLimits...` ("the 10th alone"). Both fail identically on 0.9.0 (00ca824) run the same day and passed in the 0.10.0 runs of 30 October, so they are date-dependent (the run day was 1 November, the first of a month), not caused by this release; not fixed here. The timing gate was not re-run (it fails the same on 0.9.0). `tools/release_smoke.sh` 7/7; the bundle has RECEIVE_SMS and no INTERNET; the APK is 0.10.1 / 34; the test IPA is 0.10.1 (31), profile expiry 2026-11-05T10:12Z, not installed on the phone.

## Slice 95: the home-screen widgets, redesigned (after 0.10.1)

The owner found the widgets plain. One calm, adaptive design for the whole family; nothing changes in what is stored, and every placed widget keeps working (same five receiver classes, provider ids and `GlanceAppWidget` classes; only their look and their picker names and previews changed).

- **Design.** A 28 dp rounded card (the system's own radius on Android 12+) in tonal colours: surface, a container for chips and the comparison, one accent for "+ Add" and the bars, muted text for captions. Colours are resources: `values-v31` maps them to the phone's Material You colours (`system_accent1`, `system_neutral1/2`, light and dark), the others use the app's violet; the shape drawables use the same names, so shapes and text always agree. The pairs text is drawn on are checked at 4.5:1 in light and dark (`WidgetDesignTest`). One figure in 24 sp bold, a small muted caption, a tiny comparison pill; Add is a pill ("+ Add") from 300 dp wide and a round "+" below; touch areas are 48 dp (44 dp on the 2 x 1 and 2 x 2 where the height or width forces it, 40 dp at the minimum 2 cells wide).
- **Sizes, one widget.** `QuickAddWidget` now uses `SizeMode.Exact` (it was four breakpoints) so bars are true to the width the launcher really gave and the layout follows every resize; `WidgetSummary.plan(width, height, what exists)` (pure, in `core/domain`) decides what fits: TINY (about 2 x 1: figure and Add), SQUARE (2 x 2: caption and eye, figure, comparison when 130 dp high, Add), WIDE (4 x 1: figure, eye, a chip from 300 dp, two from 380 dp, Add), CARD (wider than 2 cells and 100 dp or more high: header, then undo line, chips, budget bar, top categories, latest entries, each only when it fits whole, the lists keeping to 3 and 4 rows). **Today widget kept** (it answers a different question, today and per day, and people have placed it) and restyled with the same parts; it also uses the exact size and drops its per-day line when short.
- **What it reads (cheap, and only when it may be shown).** The comparison is this month so far against last month up to the same day number (a second `spendByCategory`); the budget bar is the overall limit in its own period (`BudgetStore.status`); categories come from the month's `spendByCategory` already read for the total, subcategories counted under their parent; the latest entries are `recentEntries(12)`, expenses and income only. **Privacy:** all of it is held to the rule of the total: it is read and drawn only when the person has shown the total with the eye and neither Hide amounts nor the App lock is on (`WidgetSummary.insights` returns nothing otherwise, tested), so a hidden widget costs what it always did and shows no shop names. Masked, the big card says "Tap the eye to see this month in detail" (or "Amounts are hidden" when the app forces it). Empty month: "Nothing spent yet this month". The eye fix of Slice 94 is unchanged (and `WidgetEyeTapTest` still passes: eye, hide again, Today, forced hide). The 2 x 1 has no eye (no room for a 40 dp target and the figure); make it 2 x 2 to reveal.
- **Colours before Android 12.** A widget's text colours are decided when it is drawn, so a dark-mode change would leave them as they were; `ExpensesApp.onConfigurationChanged` redraws the widgets while the process lives, and the hourly refresh and the next app start do it otherwise (Android 12 and later follow the phone by themselves). Not tested on a phone.
- **Picker.** Names "Spending: ..." with a size, a description each, and `previewImage` for all five (PNGs rendered from the real widget on the emulator with invented data, in `res/drawable-nodpi`). No `previewLayout` (that would be a second, hand-kept copy of the design); Android 12 and later therefore show the image, not a live wallpaper-coloured preview.
- **Tests.** Host: `WidgetSummaryTest` (11: comparison in whole percent and capped, the budget bar and its words past the limit, top categories rolled up to parents with shares, latest entries with signs and Today/Yesterday, nothing beyond the total unless it may be shown, the plan at every tier and size). Device: `WidgetDesignTest` (14: what each size draws from a model, hidden and forced-hidden notes, the empty month, what is read from the database with last month counted to the same day only, nothing read while hidden, colour contrast light and dark), `WidgetEyeTapTest` (6: the real widget placed through the system dialog at 110 x 110, 320 x 146 and 320 x 294, the eye unlocking the detail; every installed widget has a preview image, a description and the "Spending" name), `QuickAddWidgetTest` (layout tests moved to the new rules). Screenshots of every size, light and dark, shown and hidden, were taken from the emulator (not committed).
- **Not verified.** A real launcher and finger (sizes a launcher passes may differ from the exact sizes used here; the harness gave the widget its full size); Android 12 and later dynamic colours (no such image is installed here, only API 26); the dark-mode redraw on a real Android 11 phone; TalkBack on the widgets.

### Release 0.10.2 (Android code 35, iPhone build 32): integration
Ships Slice 94 (ICICI card alerts from ICICIT, widget eye, trusted-sender chips) and Slice 95 (widgets redesigned) on top of 0.10.0's features; 0.10.1 (code 34) was built but not released. New test: a shown widget total stays shown across redraws, the hourly check, a quick add and the screen going off (the only writer of `widget_show_total` is the eye's action; no timer exists). Host: domain 592, storage 429, parsers 143, 0 failures; native release guard (forced) and iOS compile OK; iOS unit 39/39. **Full Android device suite** (timing classes excluded, cold-booted API 26 emulator): 636 tests, 2 failed, 2 skipped (the `QuickAddWidgetTest` pair). Both failures are `BackupFilesTest` (known: restore double-tap "is not enabled", share-sheet OutOfMemoryError on the emulator's 44 MB heap); the class passed 19/19 in both alone-reruns. `tools/release_smoke.sh` 7/7; the bundle has RECEIVE_SMS and no INTERNET; APK 0.10.2 / 35; the test IPA is 0.10.2 (32), profile expiry 2026-11-05T10:12Z, installed over the existing app on the owner's iPhone and launched (version 0.10.2, build 32 seen).

## Slice 96: the widget eye and chips did nothing in the release build (0.10.0 to 0.10.2; fixed in 0.10.3)

The owner installed the 0.10.2 release APK on a Samsung Galaxy S22+: the widget stayed `₹••••` after tapping the eye, and the quick-entry chips added nothing. A Play tester had the eye problem on 0.10.0. Everything before this had been tested only on an Android 8 emulator with DEBUG builds.

- **Root cause (reproduced, with the log).** R8 (full mode, the release build) deleted the no-argument constructors of `ToggleTotalAction`, `QuickAddAction` and `UndoAction`: nothing in the app calls them, and Glance's own consumer rule (`-keep public class * extends ActionCallback`) keeps the classes but not their constructors. Glance creates the class behind every tap BY NAME (`Class.forName(name).getDeclaredConstructor().newInstance()`), so every eye, chip and Undo tap ended in `E/GlanceAppWidget: BroadcastReceiver execution failed` / `java.lang.NoSuchMethodException: app.expenses.widget.ToggleTotalAction.<init> []` and nothing on the screen. Seen on the owner's own 0.10.2 APK (owner-signed, `Expenses-0.10.2-release.apk`) on an Android 14 (API 34, Pixel Launcher) emulator, and on a 0.10.2-code release build on API 26. `+ Add` worked because it is a plain `PendingIntent.getActivity`. The debug build, `WidgetEyeTapTest` and every instrumented test passed because none of them runs R8 (the tests call the actions directly, or click through a debug build that keeps every constructor). The earlier "eye fix" (f96f682, Glance state counter) was a real second bug (a live composition not re-reading the database) but not the one the owner hit.
- **Second R8 fault found by the review.** R8 merged `TodayWidget` into `QuickAddWidget` (one class `cs3`, a hidden class-id field): Glance tells widgets apart by class name (`GlanceAppWidgetManager.getGlanceIds(javaClass)`, `updateAll`), so an update of one asked it to redraw the slots of both. Also, obfuscated class names change between builds, and Glance stores the provider's class name, so keeping the names also makes updates over an installed 0.10.2 safe.
- **Fix.** Two keep rules at the end of `androidApp/proguard-rules.pro`: `-keep class * implements androidx.glance.appwidget.action.ActionCallback { <init>(); }` and `-keep class * extends androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget { <init>(); }`. Checked in the mapping, with dexdump, and on the device: the three callbacks have `<init>()V`, `QuickAddWidget` and `TodayWidget` are separate classes with their own names.
- **Systematic audit of the same class of bug** (every kept class in `usage.txt` whose no-argument constructor was removed, 448 of them, filtered by what is created by name): manifest components (application, activities, receivers, services, provider) keep theirs (aapt2 rules), the three WorkManager workers keep `(Context, WorkerParameters)`, and the rest are library-internal helpers, Kotlin file facades and data classes' synthetic constructors, none created by name. Backups use kotlinx.serialization with compile-time serializers (not reflection). Nothing else needed a rule.
- **Upgrade over an installed 0.10.2.** On the API 34 emulator the 0.10.2 release build was installed, both widgets placed through the launcher, then the fixed build installed over it (`adb install -r`): the placed widgets kept working at once (Today: eye showed `₹0.00`, Quick add: amount, categories, latest entries). No placed widget was lost or blank.
- **Honest failures, and a log.** Every widget tap logs to `ExpensesWidget` (which tap, the process age, the time it took, how it ended; never an amount or a name) and every failure logs its exception; a reading failure no longer ends in Glance's "Problem loading widget" but in "Open the app" (a tap opens the app); a failing tap shows a message. When Hide amounts or the App lock keeps the figure masked the widget says why in every size (`App lock is on`, `Amounts hidden`; the 4 x 2 and bigger add a sentence) and a tap on it opens the app; with Hide amounts on the chip's "Added Chai ₹20.00" (the widget's undo line and the message) no longer shows the amount. **Decision:** a chip still records its entry with the App lock on (as documented since Slice 61: the widget only adds, it reveals nothing, and the Undo line and the message confirm it); the lock gates what is shown, not adding.
- **Guards.** `tools/check_release_keeps.py` (run by `tools/release_smoke.sh`, and by hand): fails if any `ActionCallback`, either widget, or any manifest component lost its no-argument constructor or was merged. `tools/release_smoke.py` has seven new widget steps on the minified release APK (Android 12 and later, the API34 AVD): save a quick entry, place the widget through the launcher's own picker, eye shows and hides the total, a chip adds (and Search shows both entries), the same from a force-stopped process, Hide amounts, and no `GlanceAppWidget` error in the log. The smoke script now works with whichever emulator runs (the newest Android when two do), skips the widget steps with a reason on API 26, and understands Android 14's file picker.
- **Tests.** `WidgetDesignTest` (the reason in every size, "Open the app" at every size, App lock wins over Hide amounts), `QuickAddWidgetTest` (callbacks created by name, no amount in the undo line with Hide amounts on, the reason read from the settings), `WidgetEyeTapTest` (a chip on a placed widget adds through the real callback path and Undo takes it back; Hide amounts chip; the caption).
- **Not verified:** a real Samsung launcher and One UI battery restrictions ("Deep sleeping apps" stops a widget's receiver; the owner's checklist is in `docs/REAL_DEVICE_CHECKLIST.md`), a real iPhone share-extension / widget tap round trip for the new foreground refresh (compiled and unit-tested only), TalkBack on the widgets, Android 12 to 13 launchers other than the Pixel Launcher of Android 14.
- **The scripted widget steps are green.** `tools/release_smoke.py` on the API34 AVD, release APK built from the final code: **14 of 14 steps, three runs in a row** (460 s, 553 s, 517 s); on the API26 AVD 7 steps pass and the 7 widget steps are skipped with the reason printed. What it took: placing the widget by a real touch-event drag (`input motionevent`: press, wait, move in steps, release) instead of `input draganddrop`, retried up to four times; reading the home screen only after tapping away the launcher's resize frame; scrolling the More page and the add form in short slow swipes in more than one column; skipping the tour with repeated taps. Earlier runs of the same script failed in those places (two of five placements worked with `draganddrop`; the tour tap and the form scroll flaked) and each fix is a line in the script. The emulator itself needs a cold boot after many runs (an ANR dialog and a 60 s install are the sign).
- **The guard fails when it should.** `tools/check_release_keeps.py` run on the real 0.10.2 release build (built from `44fe52f` with its mapping): FAIL, naming `QuickAddAction`, `ToggleTotalAction`, `UndoAction` (no no-argument constructor) and `QuickAddWidget`, `TodayWidget` (merged away). On the 0.10.3 APK and on the universal APK built from the Play bundle (`bundlePlaySms`, with its own mapping): PASS, including the four WorkManager workers. The Play bundle is therefore proven to carry the same constructors.
- **By hand on API34 (Android 14, Pixel Launcher, 1080 x 2340 at 440 dpi):** the owner's own 0.10.2 APK logged `NoSuchMethodException: app.expenses.widget.ToggleTotalAction.<init> []` and stayed `₹••••`. **Upgrade over placed widgets:** the fixed build over a placed 0.10.2 widget pair (both kept working at once), and over a placed **0.9.0** widget (built from `00ca824`, "Expenses: standard (4 x 2)"): it became the new card at once; the eye showed the total and a chip added an entry (`₹20.00` to `₹40.00`). **Light and dark** screenshots of the final widget read: the one-line `✓ Chai ₹20 added  Undo` pill, total updated with it, chips intact; dark tonal brown pill readable. **Resize** from 4 x 2 to 4 x 1 reflowed to the one-row layout (figure, eye, one chip, Add) with no clipping. **App lock on** (phone PIN set): the widget says `Spent this month · App lock is on`, `₹••••`, no eye; a chip still adds (log `chip: finished in 9 ms`); a tap on the figure opens the app. Debug parity: `WidgetEyeTapTest` (9 tests) places real widgets on a debug build and passes on API34; the same scripted smoke on the debug APK stalled in an ANR in step 8 (a debug Compose build on a loaded emulator), so parity is by the instrumented tests, not by the script.
- **Found only on Android 14 and fixed:** (1) the delta arrows (`widget_positive`, `widget_negative`) read 4.46 to 1 against Material You's tonal container: darkened to `#0F6B41` and `#A21F19`; (2) `AccessibilityUiTest.theChecksReallyRunAndReportAKnownBadControl` expected an `AssertionError`, Android 14's library throws its own `AccessibilityViewCheckException`: the test accepts either; (3) `QuickAddWidgetTest`'s two tests that skip on Android 8 run and pass on Android 14.

### 0.10.3, part two: the app was stale after a widget tap, and the Undo line lasted too long (the owner, on his Samsung S22+)
- **A. After a chip tap the entry was saved but Home, the list and the totals stayed old until Reports and back.** *Root cause (reproduced):* `LedgerViewModel` keeps `UiState` and rebuilds it (`refresh` / `refreshQuickly`) only from its own actions; nothing rebuilt it when the app came back to the front (`MainActivity.onStart` only ran `postAutomatic`, which refreshes only if a repeating item was posted), and the widget's callback writes through the same `AppContainer` in the same process, so the stopped activity's view model never heard of it. The database is not at fault (a new process reads it right; the VM, and the Compose state on top of it, outlive the stopped activity). The same gap would hit a captured text, a notification button and, on the iPhone, the texts and Quick add taps the share and widget extensions leave: `IosNotifications.checkAsync` drained them into the ledger and told nobody. *Fix:* `DataSignature` (shared, two small reads: this connection's `total_changes()`, which moves on any write through the process's one connection, and `PRAGMA data_version`, which moves when another connection commits), kept by the view model at the end of every rebuild; `refreshIfChangedOutside()` runs on `MainActivity.onStart` (and after the iPhone's drains) and, only when the fingerprint moved, puts rows and the period total right at once (`refreshQuickly`) and rebuilds the rest right behind it; the search-index tidy-up (`AppContainer.maintain`, whose merge command SQLite counts as a written row) is subtracted so leaving and returning without any change rebuilds nothing (first version of the test caught this: the count rose by one on every return). *Evidence:* `ForegroundRefreshTest` (3 tests: widget quick add while stopped shows in the list, the total and on screen when resumed; widget Undo while stopped takes the row out; a return with nothing changed does not rebuild, a return after a quick add does): with the one line `viewModel.refreshIfChangedOutside()` commented out all three fail (`ComposeTimeoutException` after 10 s), with it all three pass on API34.
- **B. The "Added" line with Undo stayed for two minutes.** Now **10 seconds** (`UNDO_WINDOW_MILLIS`), one line `✓ Chai ₹20 added  Undo` (the amount has dots when Hide amounts is on; the Undo word has its own 31 dp touch area). *How it ends:* the widget state holds the entry id and the time (`LAST_AT`); the line is drawn only while now is before that time plus 10 s, so every redraw drops it by itself; `scheduleUndoExpiry` makes one redraw happen right after: a coroutine while the app's process lives, and a one-time WorkManager job (`WidgetUndoExpiryWorker`, delay 11 s, unique name, replaced by a second tap; no new permission) when the process is gone. A tap on Undo after the time is refused by `EntryActions.undo` (it checks the entry's age) and says "Too late to undo from here"; an entry already deleted in the app is not undone twice. The total and the line arrive in one state change (the tap bumps the refresh counter in the same write), so the total does not trail the confirmation. `plan()` is unchanged: the line still takes its room first and the chips come back with the next redraw when it expires (at the 4 x 2 size on a 393 dp phone everything fits together, light and dark, screenshots read). *Evidence:* `WidgetEyeTapTest.theConfirmationLeavesTheWidgetByItself...` (real widget on API34 and a debug build: the line goes within 60 s with nothing touched, the entry stays recorded), `QuickAddWidgetTest.theUndoOfferHasATenSecondWindow...` (the model hides it past 10 s; the job is enqueued), release smoke step 12 (process killed with `am kill`, nothing touched: the line leaves, and the log says `undo line: the time is up`, i.e. the WorkManager path ran in the release build); measured by hand: the line was gone 11.7 s after the tap.

### Release 0.10.3 (Android code 36, iPhone build 33): integration, on commit 04cecd3
Everything below is from the final code (only documents changed after it). **The owner's earlier test APK came from `a77c49a` and is a different file.** Host: domain 592, storage 429, parsers 143 = 1,164 tests, 0 failures (`--rerun-tasks`); native release guard (forced): domain 585, parsers 138, 0 failures; iOS simulator compile OK; `tools/ios_build.sh unit` 39/39. **Android device, API 34 (Android 14), five classes:** `ForegroundRefreshTest` 3, `WidgetEyeTapTest`, `QuickAddWidgetTest` (including its two API26-skipped tests), `WidgetDesignTest`, `AccessibilityUiTest`: **68 tests, 0 failures** (the first run had 3 failures, all fixed above). **Full device suite on a cold-booted API 26 emulator** (timing classes `PerformanceBudgetTest`, `RefreshBudgetTest`, `RecreateStressTest` excluded; a clean worktree of the final commit): **650 tests, 3 failures, 11 skipped.** Failures: `BackupFilesTest` x2 (the known ones: restore double-tap "is not enabled", share-sheet OutOfMemoryError on the 44 MB heap) and `TrustedSendersUiTest.addingShowsAChip...` (`ComposeNotIdleException`, a load timeout, not seen before); each class run alone twice afterwards: `TrustedSendersUiTest` passed 2 of 2, `BackupFilesTest` passed 2 of 2. Skipped: `QuickAddWidgetTest` 2 and `WidgetEyeTapTest` 9, which skip themselves on Android 8 (a test cannot place a widget there); they all ran green on API 34. `tools/release_smoke.sh`: API 26 7 passed + 7 widget steps skipped with the reason; API 34 14/14 three times in a row. Artifacts in `~/Documents/personal/expenses_builds/`: `Expenses-0.10.3-release.apk` (0.10.3 / 36, owner key, md5 3c5eada0...), `Expenses-0.10.3-play-sms-code36.aab` (RECEIVE_SMS yes, INTERNET no; universal APK from it passes the keeps check), `Expenses-iOS-0.10.3-test.ipa` (0.10.3 / 33, installed over the owner's iPhone app and launched, `devicectl` reads back 0.10.3 (33)). The stale 0.10.2 files were deleted.

## Slice 97: permission and access status did not refresh when the app came back from settings (Play tester)
A tester turned on Notification access (and, the same way, the text permission) in Android's settings, came back and the screen still said "not given"; it updated only after leaving the screen and opening it again.
- **Root cause.** Status was read once in composition: `host.notificationAccessGranted()` was evaluated inline in `AppRoot` (no state, so it re-ran only when something else recomposed), `rememberSmsPermission` kept the `RECEIVE_SMS` answer in a `mutableStateOf` set only by the request launcher's result (a grant in system settings never reached it), `PrivacyScreen(host.privacyRows())` and `loadCaptureHealth(permission)` were read once at open. Only the Notifications screen already used `LifecycleResumeEffect`. A second, smaller cause: `NotificationManagerCompat.getEnabledListenerPackages` keeps its last answer and never refreshes it when the secure setting has become null, so "access given" could stay true.
- **Mechanism.** `ResumeState` / `rememberResumeState(read)` (`shared/.../ui/ResumeState.kt`): reads once, then again on every lifecycle ON_RESUME (back from Settings, the permission dialog, another app; on the iPhone, the app becoming active again) and on `refresh()` (called right after a permission request's result). A changed value only is written, so nothing recomposes or reloads otherwise. Reads are a permission check or a settings read, never a query. `NotificationCaptureService.accessGranted` now reads the setting directly.
- **Sites (before -> after).** Capture screen text permission (`AppRoot` via `MainActivity.rememberSmsPermission`, also feeds `addSenderHeader`): result only -> resume + result. Capture screen notification access (`capture_notif_switch`, `email_capture_switch`, the two red lines, both toggles): read inline -> resume. Privacy rows (`PermissionNotes.rows`, all permissions and Notification access): at open -> resume. Capture health (depends on the text permission): at open -> reloaded when that permission changes while open. Notifications screen (`notif_blocked`, POST_NOTIFICATIONS): already resume + result -> same, now through the helper. Not shown anywhere else: exact alarms, battery optimisation, storage/photo/camera (pickers and the camera app need no permission) do not exist in the app. iPhone: `notificationsAllowed()` (Notifications screen) uses the same helper; the iPhone has no text or notification access, and Privacy shows a Face ID row (read on resume now too).
- **Tests.** Device (API 34): `PermissionResumeUiTest` 3 (access given then taken back on the Capture screen with the activity stopped and resumed, the Privacy row, nothing-changed leaves the screen as it was), `ZzSmsResumeUiTest` 1 (text permission granted while stopped), `AaaNotifPermissionResumeUiTest` 1 (POST_NOTIFICATIONS granted while stopped). With the resume read removed, 3 of the 4 changed-state tests fail; restored, all pass. Taking a runtime permission away cannot be driven in the test process (revoke kills it), so that direction is by hand only. No host test: the helper is a Compose lifecycle effect.
- **By hand (API 34):** More, Capture, switched on payment notifications, the phone opened Notification access, granted with `cmd notification allow_listener` and Back: switch on, red line gone, no navigation. Home, `disallow_listener`, reopened: switch off, red line back (screenshots read).
- **Not verified:** a real Samsung/One UI notification-access flow, the iPhone (compiled for the simulator and unit-tested only; ON_RESUME on iOS comes from Compose's lifecycle owner and was not seen after Settings), a listener connect/disconnect while the screen stays in front (not wired; the system's page is a different screen so a return always passes through resume).
- **Follow-up: did the direct read of the listener setting break capture? No (measured).** On API 34 `NotificationCaptureTest` (4 of 5) and `EmailCaptureUiTest` (7 of 9) failed, and 0.10.3 had them green on API 26, so the change was suspected. Same six classes (`NotificationCaptureTest` 5, `EmailCaptureUiTest` 9, `CaptureTest` 8, `ForegroundRefreshTest` 3, `NotificationsTest` 9, `AlertsUiTest` 9; debug APKs installed with `-g`, each class run alone) on one emulator at a time:

| build | API 26 | API 34 |
|---|---|---|
| `8d9116a^` (before the change, in a git worktree) | all 6 pass (43 tests) | Notification 1/5, Email 2/9, other four pass |
| `96da3ef` (the change) | all 6 pass | same 4 + 7 failures |
| `96da3ef` + `NotificationAccess` parser and rebind | all 6 pass | same 4 + 7 failures |
| same + tests seed access with `cmd notification allow_listener` | all 6 pass | all 6 pass (43 tests) |

Cause: on Android 14 a value written only to the secure setting (`settings put secure enabled_notification_listeners`) does not make the system bind the listener (`dumpsys notification` lists it as not enabled; `cmd notification allow_listener` does bind it). The tests, not the app, relied on the old behaviour; it fails identically before the change. The two classes now also run the command (harmless on API 26).
- **Access detection hardened anyway.** The setting is parsed by `NotificationAccess.granted` (core/domain, pure; `NotificationAccessTest` 6): `pkg/full.Class`, `pkg/.Short`, several colon-separated entries in any order, a bare package, spaces, null/empty/malformed (no class, no package), and a package that only shares a prefix or suffix all give the right answer. `NotificationCaptureService.onListenerDisconnected` now asks the system to bind it again (`requestRebind`) while access is still given; the system binds an enabled listener by itself the first time, so this only recovers a dropped binding. Not seen on a real phone.
- **Mistake caught on the way:** the first commit of this slice (`8d9116a`) had one block of `AppRoot.kt` in the wrong place (a partial staging error), so that commit did not compile; `96da3ef` repaired it. The device runs above were from the working tree, so they were unaffected.

## Slice 98: the category of a captured bank message (a Play tester's report: "it still does not identify the category")

The owner relayed that the app does not identify the category correctly from bank messages (text, notification, email) and assumed it should learn from the categories people choose and suggest from their history. Evidence first, then the fixes. **All figures below are from invented messages (nothing real, no one's data) written and labelled by me, and from the repo's public sample corpus; none is a measurement of real bank traffic.**

### What was already there (read from the code, not assumed)
Order of precedence in To review (`CategorySuggest.suggest`, run by `LedgerViewModel.inboxRows`): the person's rules, what they chose for this payee (`merchant_map`, written by `confirmCapture` and by the editor for entries from texts), their history for the same payee (`SimilarEntries.topCategory` over an FTS lookup), the 372-merchant starter list, the words of a bank charge, "payment to a person" -> Other, else none. So **learning and history suggestions already existed**; the owner's idea is not missing, it was not reaching. Notification and email captures go through the same `inboxRows` as texts. Credits are never given a category (they become uncategorised income by design). A debit with no category is added as "Uncategorised", never as a wrong one.

### Measured (harness `CategoryAccuracyReportTest`, corpus `category-corpus/invented.tsv`: 275 invented messages over 17 sender wordings, 74 of them a hold-out written after the code was frozen; `CorpusCategoryReportTest` for the public sample corpus)
| | before (commit c458da4) | after |
|---|---|---|
| invented debits read at all | 258 of 272 (14 not read: SBI "debited by 250.0", one decimal) | 272 of 272 |
| a payee found | 205 of 258 (79%) | 265 of 272 (97%) |
| of 211-221 debits that have a right category: right / wrong / none | 110 (52%) / 0 / 101 (48%) | 192 (87%) / 0 / 29 (13%) |
| hold-out only (60 with a right category) | 26 of 56 (46%) | 51 of 60 (85%) |
| public sample corpus (67 debits): a payee found | 24 (36%) | 39 (58%) |
| learned under one spelling, seen as another (104 pairs of invented spellings of 8 payees) | old key found 26 (25%) | 104 (100%) |
| later sightings of a payee the app did not know the first time (7) | old key 2 | 7 |
**Wrong categories were 0 before and 0 after on this corpus** (including 30 adversarial names such as "UBER SINGH", "APOLLO TYRES", "JIO MART", marketplaces and gateway names), so on this evidence the failure the tester describes is mostly a **missing** category or payee, not a wrong one. Whether real traffic contains wrong ones (for instance from a guess accepted in bulk and then remembered) is **not measured**; see "what changed" for the guard I added anyway.

Failure patterns, in order of how many invented messages they cost:
1. **No payee at all (the biggest).** ICICI's and IDFC's UPI debit ("...; Pune Metro credited."): 14 of 14 had no payee. Card names through a gateway ("RAZ*SWIGGY", "PYU*", "PAYU*", "BILLDESK*", "SWIGGY*ORDER"): 11 of 11. A terminal or branch number in the name ("KIRANA MART 2231", "SHELL PETROL PUMP 4521"), a company name longer than four words, SBI's one-decimal amount (the text was not read at all), Axis's "UPI/P2M/ref/NAME Not you?" (the name swallowed the next words), a payment app's own name taken as the person ("Google Pay: Paid Rs 250 to Swiggy" gave "Google Pay").
2. **A payee found that the starter list does not know** (local shops, small restaurants, pharmacies): 14 of 17 got nothing, and nothing the person ever chose could help the first time. The first message of every new shop is always uncategorised unless a name's words say what it is (now a guess to check).
3. **Learning keyed on the exact text.** `merchant_map` was keyed on the payee's letters and digits as read, so a choice for "swiggy@icici" was not found for "SWIGGY BANGALORE KA IN" or "RAZ*SWIGGY": 25% of the invented spelling pairs. The editor path learned from the entry's title (which the person may have renamed), not the key To review looks up.
4. **Learning from the app's own guess.** Pressing Add (or "Add all", or a swipe) on a starter-list guess recorded it as "what you chose last time", so a wrong guess became a stronger rule with every acceptance.
5. **The waiting cards did not follow.** After choosing a category on one card, the other pending cards of that payee were recomputed but the card kept the category it was first shown with (`remember(r.id)`), so a rule added or a choice made was invisible until the screen was reopened.
6. **Merging a category silently killed its rules.** `CategoryStore.merge` moved splits, income, templates and repeating items, but not `capture_rule` or `merchant_map`; they kept pointing at the deleted category and were skipped (the suggestion must be in the visible categories).
7. Not changed: credits get no category; a payment to a **named** person is not recognised as one (only a mobile-number address is); learning ignores the amount (an Amazon Rs 199 and a Rs 2,499 are one payee); two merchants of one company are two payees on purpose.

### What changed
- **`PayeeKey`** (`core/domain`): one key for a payee however it is written (see OUR_FEATURES). Lookup tries it, then the old key; learning writes it and carries an old row over with its count. `CaptureRuleStore.learn(payee, category, label)` keeps a label for the list. No schema change; `merchant_map` rows from older versions and backups keep working.
- **`PayeeGuess`**: gateway prefixes, terminal and branch numbers, names of up to six words, "; NAME credited", Axis's path, a name at the end of the text, a masked account or the bank's own sentence ("from XX0000 to SampleRide", "HDFC Bank Card x") is never the name, words after the name ("with your BOBCARD", "for INR", ".RRN") are cut off. `GenericAlertParser`: "debited by 250.0" is an amount.
- **`PayeeWords`**: the words of an unknown name give a **guess** (tea, medicals, hospital, kirana, insurance, petrol ...; whole words only, two categories disagreeing gives nothing). **`SuggestionConfidence` / `CaptureCategory`**: SURE or CHECK per suggestion with the thresholds in OUR_FEATURES; the reason names how many choices or entries back it; other categories this payee has been in are offered as chips. A CHECK card is never added by "Add all" or a swipe (the dialog says how many have no category: they are added as Uncategorised).
- **Learning only from a choice**: `confirmCapture` learns when the category differs from what was shown, or agrees with an earlier choice (which counts); a guess accepted as it stood teaches nothing. `EntryActions.save(teach)`: a changed category on a captured entry (editor, bulk change, "file similar") teaches under the key of the original text's payee; undoing a bulk change does not teach.
- **The card follows** the app's suggestion until the person picks on it.
- **What the app has learned** (More, Capture, Category rules, below the rules): list, Change, Forget, "File N earlier expenses with no category" with Undo (one `uncategorisedExpenses` pass, only when the list is open). Merge now rewrites rules and learned choices; delete forgets learned choices for the category.
- Backup restore keeps `uses` (it was reset to 1). `docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md` (key note), `docs/UNDO_AUDIT.md`, `docs/research/OUR_FEATURES.md`, `docs/REAL_DEVICE_CHECKLIST.md` updated.

### Cost (20,000-entry ledger, host, `LearnedChoicesScaleTest`)
3,000 choices learned in 55 ms; 5,000 lookups with city noise 13 ms (a map read per payee, not a scan); the list of what was learned 8 ms; the pass over 20,000 uncategorised expenses 20 ms (only when that list is open, never on Home or the save path). Saving 20,000 entries took 13.8 s in that test and is not changed by this work.

### Tests
Host: new `PayeeKeyTest` (9: one key across spellings, different payees apart, old keys, marketplaces; prints 25% -> 100% reach), `PayeeWordsTest` (4), `SuggestionConfidenceTest` (6), `CaptureCategoryTest` (12), `PayeeGuessTest` (+8), `CaptureRuleStoreTest` (+3), `CategoryStoreTest` (+2: merge and delete), `LearnedChoicesScaleTest` (1), `CategoryAccuracyReportTest` (3: report, learning simulation, floors: 0 wrong, at least 80% of debits with a right category right), `CorpusCategoryReportTest` (1). Device (API 34): new `LearnedChoicesUiTest` (5: a choice for one spelling is found under another and the waiting card follows; a card the person picked on stays as picked; a guess and a payment to a person are marked and left out of "Add all", the dialog counts the uncategorised; the learned list shows and forgets; earlier uncategorised expenses are filed and Undo puts them back and teaches nothing). Existing tests changed for the new behaviour: `CaptureRulesUiTest` (an unknown payee name is no longer "dosa", which is now a food word) and `SimilarUiTest` ("2 of your earlier entries").

### Not verified, said plainly
Real bank traffic and a real phone (every message here is invented; labels are mine). The thresholds (2 agreeing entries, 2 choices for a person) and the word list are judgement, not tuned on use. The hold-out was written by me after the code, so it is less biased than the tuned set but not independent. A person who is paid under names the app reads as a shop word (a "Tea Stall" that is really a landlord) gets a wrong-looking guess to check, never an automatic one. iPhone: compiled and unit-tested only (`LearnedPayees.kt` and the inbox changes are shared code; no iPhone UI test of them).

### Runs for this slice
Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`): domain 617, parsers 167, storage 435 (incl. `UiConventionsTest`), 1,219 in all, 0 failures. Common domain and parsers tests as an optimised Kotlin/Native binary (forced rerun): domain 610, parsers 158, 0 failures. `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles; `tools/ios_build.sh unit`: 39 tests, 0 failures. Device (emulator API 34, 15 classes: `LearnedChoicesUiTest`, `CaptureRulesUiTest`, `InboxUiTest`, `SimilarUiTest`, `CaptureTest`, `IncomeCategoriesUiTest`, `SkipUndoUiTest`, `ChangeTypeUiTest`, `BulkEditTest`, `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest`, `CategoriesTest`, `InboxSwipeUiTest`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`, `HealthPrivacyUiTest`, `ReportAndAliasUiTest`): 104 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped. **Not run to a result:** `NotificationCaptureTest` and `EmailCaptureUiTest` (they bind the notification listener with `settings put secure enabled_notification_listeners`, which does not bind it on API 34, and the API 26 emulator could not be created: the Mac had 5.8 GB free and the AVD needs 7.4 GB). This change does not touch how a notification is read or ingested, only what To review suggests for it, and `PayeeGuessTest` has the wording "Google Pay: Paid Rs 250 to Swiggy"; but a run of those two classes on API 26 is still owed. A whole-suite device run, the timing classes and the release smoke were not run; no version was bumped and no release artifact built.


### Release 0.10.4 (Android code 37, iPhone build 34): integration, on commit 68bc6c3
Ships Slice 97 (permission and notification-access status refresh on return, `ResumeState`, `NotificationAccess`, listener rebind) and Slice 98 (payee key, name-word guesses, confidence tiers, SBI one-decimal amounts, "What the app has learned") on top of 0.10.3 (code 36, uploaded to Play: not reused). `git status` was clean and HEAD compiled (the briefly broken 8d9116a was fixed by 96da3ef).

**Review of the two workers' diff (critical read before building).** (1) *Fixed:* "File N earlier expenses with no category under X" in the learned list went through `bulkSetCategory` → `save`, whose new rule teaches on any changed category of a captured entry, so filing N entries raised the learned "chosen N times in a row" count by N and could turn a first guess into a "sure" one without the person choosing again. `bulkSetCategory`/`bulkEdit` now take `teach` (default true: a person's bulk change from search still teaches) and `fileEarlierLike` passes false. (2) Checked and fine: the one-decimal amount only widens the "debited by 12.5" form (the `Rs`/`INR` form already allowed one decimal), OTPs and balances are still screened first; a name running to the very end of a text must start with a capital ("subject to clearing" is not a payee); `merchant_map` has no new columns (only queries; learned keys carried over from the old key on the next choice, lookups try both); the merge/delete category fixes remap or delete learned rows and rules; backup restore keeps use counts only for keys not already present; "check" suggestions are excluded from "Add all" and the swipe (`easy` filter) and from nothing else; `learn` only on the person's own choice or a changed category; `onListenerDisconnected` asks for a rebind only while access is still granted; `rememberResumeState` writes only a changed value and reads cheap settings (the iPhone's `privacyRows` is a Face ID availability check). No new work on the Home or save path: `EntryActions.save` reads one origin row only when a captured entry's category changed; the learned list's scans run only when that list is opened. (3) `CaptureRulesUiTest` ("dosa@okaxis" became "zorbo@okaxis") and `SimilarUiTest` ("Bills (2 of your earlier entries)") are legitimate: "dosa" is now a food word, so the old payee would get a name-word guess and the test's "no category" premise no longer held; the reasons now carry counts. Neither assertion was weakened (they still check the same suggestion and the same correction). **Not changed, noted:** a learned choice used once for a plain shop is "sure" (as before this release); a first-ever "dosa@..." style address is now a guess to check.

**Runs, on the final code.** Host: domain 617, parsers 167, storage 435, 0 failures (`--rerun-tasks`). Native release guard (forced): domain 610, parsers 158, 0 failures. iOS simulator compile OK; `tools/ios_build.sh unit` 39/39. **Full device suite, cold-booted API 26** (timing classes excluded, clean worktree of the final commit): 660 tests, 4 failures, 5 skipped. Failures: `BackupFilesTest` x2 (the known ones; each passed alone 2 of 2) and `MotionUiTest` x2 (`theAddFormRisesIn...` and `payingABillShowsAPaidCheck...`): they failed 2 of 2 alone and passed 2 of 2 after the cause was found: the re-created emulator had its three animation scales at 0 (they need 1.0), an emulator setting and not the code. Skipped: `QuickAddWidgetTest` 2 (Android 8), `SmsDisclosureUiTest` 1 and `ZzSmsResumeUiTest` 1 (the text permission was already granted by an earlier class in the same install), `AaaNotifPermissionResumeUiTest` 1 (needs Android 13+). **API 34, the 12 classes** (`WidgetEyeTapTest`, `QuickAddWidgetTest`, `WidgetDesignTest`, `ForegroundRefreshTest`, `PermissionResumeUiTest`, `AaaNotifPermissionResumeUiTest`, `ZzSmsResumeUiTest`, `NotificationCaptureTest`, `EmailCaptureUiTest`, `CaptureTest`, `AccessibilityUiTest`, `LearnedChoicesUiTest`): 100 tests, 99 passed, 0 skipped; the one failure is `QuickAddWidgetTest.aWidgetPlacedFromEachPickerEntryIsFoundWhenTheWidgetIsUpdated` (found 1 of 4 placed copies), which passes 27 of 27 when the class runs alone and fails in the combined run; **the same combined runs fail the same way on the 0.10.3 commit (c458da4)** (that test failed in the same five-class combination there; the capture-class combination also failed on the 0.10.3 commit, but that commit lacks the Android 14 listener seeding, so only the widget failure is a like-for-like comparison), so it is an order interaction of the test harness on Android 14, not a regression of this release; it was not fixed here. The capture classes passed (5, 9 and 8 tests) in the 12-class run and failed when `QuickAddWidgetTest` ran first in a smaller set on HEAD: the same kind of order dependence, unexplained. **Timing gate** (cold-booted API 26, `PerformanceBudgetTest` + `RefreshBudgetTest`, HEAD against c458da4 alternately, three runs of each, machine load 6 to 27): the same 9 tests fail in every run of both builds, plus `tagSuggestions...` (12.5 ms limit, 14.7 to 26.5 ms on HEAD and 18.2 to 18.7 ms on the 0.10.3 commit: a borderline that flips between runs on both). Save-path numbers: slowest of 60 saves 34.3 to 51.5 ms (HEAD) against 36.7 to 52.2 ms (0.10.3); whole save path p95 45 to 154 ms against 58 to 543 ms; rest-of-screen settle p95 215 to 285 ms against 226 to 284 ms: no consistent jump above 30%. Release smoke: API 26 7 passed + 7 widget steps skipped (reason printed); API 34 14/14, twice in a row. `tools/check_release_keeps.py` PASS on the APK and on the universal APK built from the Play bundle.
