## Slice 99: three-digit account digits, "Which account is this?", and fixable dropped bank texts (after 0.10.4; a Play tester's report)

A tester said some ICICI Bank account texts carry only the last THREE digits of the account, so the entry did not know which account it came from. All texts, digits, names and amounts in tests and here are invented, in the shapes of the public sample corpus (`tools/sms-corpus`, `core/parsers/src/jvmTest/resources/corpus/icici`: ICICI account alerts there read `Acct XX000`, three digits; cards `XX0000`, four; IDFC accounts are three too) and of the tester's report. No version was bumped and no release artifact built.

### 1. Three digits (the most important)
- **Root cause.** Everything between the text and the account assumed FOUR digits: the account form refused anything but four (`AccountLooks.parseLast4`), `accountsWithLast4` was an exact SQL match, and the pairing, bill, statement and duplicate code compared the strings. The parser already read `XX123` as `123` (its mask took 3 to 6 digits), but no account could carry three digits, so no text could ever match, and shapes such as `ending 123`, `x123`, `ac no. ending 4321` or IDFC's `a/c ending XXXXXXXX000` gave nothing.
- **Parser.** `AccountMasks` (core/parsers) reads the digits with the length the text shows: an account word with its digits (`A/c XX1234`, `Acct XX123`, `A/c *123`, `Account XXX123`, `ac no. ending 4321`), a card word with four, `ending 123` / `ending in 3025`, `Card XX123` (masked three), and last a bare mask (`x123`, `XX123`) of one to five mask characters (a long mask is the OTHER account of a transfer and is never the text's own). A longer run is read as its last four. `TransferClues` (the other end of an own-account transfer) is length-aware too: ICICI's `Acct XX000 debited ... & Acct XX001 credited` now gives own `000`, other `001`. `AccountDigits` (core/domain) is the one place that knows what three or four digits are: `parse`, `fromRun`, `compatible` (equal, or one ends the other), `same`, `mentionedIn` (a statement line names an account by a mask or an account word, never by a bare three-digit number).
- **Matching.** `AccountMatch` (core/domain), used by To review, own-account pairing (`TransferScan`), bills, notifications, transfer candidates and the statement import's card-payment finder. An account FITS when its digits equal the text's or one ends the other, and its NAME does not plainly belong to another known bank (`BankNames`, built from the banks and card issuers of `BankRules`; payment apps and billers are no bank; a name that names no bank, "Salary", still fits). Order: (1) exactly the same digits and nothing else fits (this is every four-digit match, as before); (2) what the person chose before for this bank and these digits (the existing `capture_acct:<issuer>:<digits>` setting); (3) exactly one account fits (three digits find an account saved with four that ends the same, and the reverse); (4) otherwise nothing is guessed: `Several` names the accounts that fit and the card asks, `None` when none does. A text of one bank never lands on another bank's account by digits alone when the account's name says its bank. Pairing uses the same rule for each half and, for the other end of the transfer, digits alone (its bank is not said): a pair is never made on an ambiguous account.
- **Settings field.** The account form's digits field accepts three or four digits (label "Last 3 or 4 digits (optional)", help "... Some banks show only 3 digits; enter what your bank texts show."; two or five digits are refused with "Type 3 or 4 digits, like 821 or 4821, as your bank's texts show them, or leave it empty."). Stored in `account_look.last4` unchanged: no schema change, no migration; an older backup restores as before, and a backup with three digits restored by the previous app drops those digits only (documented in `docs/BACKUP_FORMAT.md`).
- **Decisions.** A new account is not offered by digits alone on a different bank (names count only as evidence AGAINST); an earlier explicit choice outranks digits that only end the same, but exactly equal four digits still outrank it (as before: no existing behaviour moved). Where two accounts of one bank fit, including "123" and "4123", the card asks: that is the spec's "more than one matches". The corpus answer key already held 17 three-digit expectations (ICICI and IDFC accounts); `CorpusReportTest` now checks that each is read as exactly three digits.
- **Measured (host, invented).** `AccountMatchTest`: 2,000 invented ledgers of 2 to 4 accounts across 5 banks with deliberately clashing digits, 6,004 texts about the person's own accounts: right account 5,882, asked because several fit 122, **wrong account 0**, **an account of another bank 0**, missed 0; 9,995 texts whose digits no account of their bank carries: matched or asked 0. Own-account pairing: 1,500 invented transfers (400 with the same last three digits at another bank): paired on the right two accounts 1,204, left for the person because the ICICI account was ambiguous 296, **wrong or false pairs 0**, clear ones left unpaired 0. Public corpus: three-digit account texts read as exactly three digits 17 of 17; account digits right on 112 of 125 texts that print any.
- **Tests.** Host: `AccountMatchTest` (digits, statement mentions, matching rules, bank words, the two precision runs), `AccountDigitsReadingTest` (every wording above with the length kept; the tester's shape; numbers around a text that are not digits; the other end of an IMPS own transfer), `AccountLookStoreTest` (+2: three digits saved, found by three or four, ambiguity; backup round trip with three digits and an older file with four), `CorpusReportTest` floor. Device: `AccountAskUiTest` and three-digit cases in `AccountTilesUiTest` (field, help line, refusal, saved value; a text goes to the account saved with those digits, not another bank's). The existing setup-hint string now says "Set the last 3 or 4 digits" (`OwnTransferUiTest` updated).
- **Not verified.** Real ICICI traffic (the shapes are the corpus's and the tester's report); other banks that print three digits; an account whose name names no bank is matched on digits alone (one unnamed account ending in the same three digits as a real ICICI account would be taken if it is the only one that fits); the iPhone beyond compile and unit tests.

### 2. "Which account is this?" asked once, inline in To review
- **Why.** Add all and swipe need a known account (`rememberedAccount`), so a new install (two default accounts, Cash and Bank) got neither until each bank card had been added by hand once, and a text whose digits fit two accounts was silently put on the first account.
- **Behaviour.** A waiting card whose account is not known (digits fit none, fit several, or the text prints none, and the person has two or more accounts) shows "Which account is this?" above "Goes to": why ("I have not seen ICICI Bank ending 123 before." / "ICICI Joint and ICICI Savings all end in 123, so I cannot tell which this is." / "<bank> texts do not say which account they are about."), the account chips (only the ones that fit when several do), and "New account ending 123" (a small form: name "ICICI Bank 123", type Bank account, or Credit card when the text says credit card; "Create and use it" makes an account carrying the digits). One tap on an account puts this text there and remembers it for the bank and digits (the same `capture_acct` setting an added item writes, no schema change); the other waiting cards of the same bank and digits follow at once and "Using X for ICICI Bank ending 123 · N more waiting texts follow" is offered with Undo (restores the earlier choice and removes an account the choice created). When several accounts fit there is no guess: Add waits for the answer. Otherwise Add keeps working as before (first account as the suggestion). Add all and swipe leave these cards out as before; the top now says "N waiting texts need an account first, so they are left out of Add all and swipe. Answer ... once for each bank card; the others of the same card follow." with a button "Choose the account (K)" that scrolls to the first such card, and the Add-all dialog says how many have no account.
- **Files.** `AccountResolver.kt` (resolver glue and the ask text), `ui/AccountQuestion.kt`, `LedgerViewModel.chooseCaptureAccount/createCaptureAccount/undoAccountChoice`, `InboxRowUi.accountAsk/accountChoices/newAccountName/newAccountType`, the card and top of `ui/CaptureScreens.kt`. New testTags: `inbox_account_ask_<id>`, `inbox_account_title_<id>`, `inbox_account_why_<id>`, `inbox_pick_<id>_<account>`, `inbox_new_account_<id>`, `inbox_new_name_<id>`, `inbox_new_type_<id>_BANK|CREDIT_CARD`, `inbox_new_create_<id>`, `inbox_new_cancel_<id>`, `inbox_new_problem_<id>`, `inbox_needs_account`, `inbox_fix_accounts`, `account_last4_help`; all earlier tags are kept.
- **Decision.** "New account" takes two taps (open the form, Create) rather than one: the type of a card or loan cannot be changed once it has entries, so the type is shown, not guessed.

### 3. Dropped bank texts are fixable in one tap: "Senders we skipped" (header only; reworked after the first version)
- **Decision (the owner, overnight): the app must NOT look at the words of a text from an unlisted sender.** The first version of this part looked at such a body once, in memory, to see whether it looked like a payment (a payment-shape check, an `UNLISTED_PAYMENT` outcome, a masked outline kept per sender). The Play SMS declaration was accepted on the promise that only texts from listed or trusted senders are read, so that version was **removed entirely** (the shape check, the outcome, the outline and its storage and tests) and every privacy wording it had changed was **restored byte for byte from commit e067d5b**: `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`, `docs/site/privacy.html` (including its effective date), `docs/PLAY_SMS_DECLARATION.md`, `docs/IOS.md`, the in-app disclosure and "what it does not do" sentences, the Android permission note, the iPhone screen's sentence and the Shortcuts description. The code and those texts agree again: a text from an unlisted sender is dropped before its words are read (`CaptureClassifier.classify` answers `NOT_ALLOWLISTED` from the sender alone), and nothing about its words exists anywhere.
- **What the list uses.** Only what the app already recorded about a skipped text: the sender's header root and when (the `capture_event` row has always held `sender_root` and the time; capture health lists those roots). The one thing added is the header's category suffix, read from the sender NAME (`SenderRules.headerSuffix`: `S` in `AX-HDFCBK-S`). `SkippedSenders` (core:domain) keeps, per root, in the settings table under `skipped_sender:<ROOT>`, `count|lastAt|suffix letters` (`3|1730000000000|ST`; `-` for a header with no suffix): the name, a count, a time and letters, **no body, no amount, no digit from a text** (a header root may itself contain a digit, as the sender chose it). At most 20 roots, the quietest dropped first; a sender quiet for 30 days starts again from one; not on the backup whitelist (`SkippedSenderStoreTest` proves a backup file holds no such key); cleared when the name is trusted (button, chip editor or the health screen). No schema change. Counted on both ingest paths (Android `Capture.ingestWith`, iPhone `MessageIngest.ingest`) only for an alphanumeric header from a text (never a phone number, an email, a notification, or a text with no sender), right where the existing `NOT_ALLOWLISTED` event is recorded.
- **What is shown (More > Capture, under the sender chips; on the iPhone in its Capture screen).** "Senders we skipped": each header root with "N texts skipped" and "Last one: <date and time>", and a button **"Treat NAME as my bank"** (48 dp; adds the trusted sender through `TrustedSenders.add`, "Added NAME" in `skipped_added_notice`, the chip appears, the row goes, the counter is cleared, the next text from the name is read). Explanation: "Banks send alerts from names like ICICIT. If one of these is your bank, tap Treat as my bank; only new texts from it will be read. We never read the texts of other senders." Which names are listed is decided by the suffix: **T or S** (transactional, service or bank alert) listed; **P** (promotional) and **G** (government) hidden; a header with **no suffix or another letter** only once it has sent **3 texts**; a phone number or an email never; a name that has sent both P and S texts is listed (the S text may be a bank's). Most texts first, then the most recent. The skipped texts are not kept, so only new texts are read after trusting (the explanation says so).
- **Tests.** Host: `SkippedSendersTest` (domain 7: the stored value is three fields only, counting and suffix memory, the 30-day restart, the suffix rule, built-in, trusted and old senders left out, order, the cap of 20), `SkippedSenderStoreTest` (4: counting, cap, clearing, no backup carries a counter), `SenderSuffixTest` (2). Device (API 34): `SkippedSendersUiTest` (4: a -S and a -T name shown with counts, -P, -G, a phone number and a two-text no-suffix name hidden, the explanation, the 48 dp button, trust -> chip, "Added MYCOOP", row gone, next text captured, counter gone, still so after a relaunch; three texts bring a no-suffix name; a name typed by hand removes the row; nothing shown for P, G or phone senders only) and two new `CaptureTest` cases (a text from an unlisted name leaves only the name, count, time and suffix: searched for the body's name, digits, amount, reference and words in the settings, events and inbox; a phone number, an email and a built-in or trusted header are never counted).
- **Decisions.** No heuristic on words, ever; a header with a suffix the app cannot classify waits for 3 texts rather than being guessed; trusting is undone with the chip's remove (no new Undo row).
- **Redaction gap fixed on the way (the "report a message" feature).** `MessageRedactor` masked the digits after TWO or more mask characters (`XX1234`) or after an account word (`A/c 1234`), but not after ONE (`A/C X1234` SBI-style, `x123`, `*123`), so those digits went into the shown and mailed report. A single mask character that does not belong to a longer word is now read as a mask (`MessageRedactorTest.aSingleLetterMaskAndThreeDigitMasksAreRedactedLikeTheLongOnes`: eight shapes, with and without "keep the amounts", and ordinary words stay readable).
- **Not verified.** Real ICICI and other banks' headers (the suffix rule is India's registration convention, not checked against live traffic: a bank may send an alert from a header with a different suffix or none, which then waits for 3 texts); the iPhone path on a phone and the Swift test edit (not run); the checklist item for a real unlisted sender (`docs/REAL_DEVICE_CHECKLIST.md`).

### Runs for Slice 99 (final commit, run from a clean checkout of that commit, `git archive`, so other people's unfinished edits were not in it)
- **Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`):** domain 636, parsers 176, storage 441 (incl. `UiConventionsTest`), 0 failures. **Native release guard (forced rerun, optimised Kotlin/Native on the simulator):** domain 629, parsers 167, 0 failures (it found one common-code mistake, `String.toSortedSet` is JVM-only, fixed before the final commit). **iOS simulator compile** (`:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`) OK. **`tools/ios_build.sh unit`** (under the ios lock): 39 of 39.
- **Device, API 34 emulator, debug build** (one run at a time under `/tmp/emu.lock`): `OwnTransferUiTest` 9, `InboxUiTest` 14, `OriginUiTest` 1 (the three older tests whose waits now scroll to the card), `AccessibilityUiTest` 10, `AccountAskUiTest` 10, `SkippedSendersUiTest` 4, `CaptureTest` 10, `TrustedSendersUiTest` 6, `HealthPrivacyUiTest` 2, `AccountTilesUiTest` 10: 76 tests, 2 failures, both mine and both a wrong expectation in the new `CaptureTest` cases (they compared the time the phone stamped on the text, but the counter keeps the time the app saw it); fixed, and `CaptureTest`, `SkippedSendersUiTest` and `TrustedSendersUiTest` were run again on the final commit: 20 of 20.
- **Not run:** the whole device suite, the timing classes, API 26, a real phone. The earlier part-1/2 run of `DuplicatesUiTest`, `PairingUiTest` and the rest of the capture classes was on the commit before this rework; this rework changed no code those use.
- **A layout cost, said plainly:** a card that asks "Which account is this?" is taller. Only the first waiting card of a bank card (issuer and digits) carries the full question; the others show one line ("Answer on the first ... card above: this one follows", `inbox_account_follow_<id>`).

## Slice 100: income categories for credits, Undo for rules and learned choices, and the "no category" Home card (after 0.10.4)

**All texts and names are invented; every figure below is from them, not from real bank traffic.**

### A. Income categories for money received in To review
- **Before.** Every credit became income with no category (the Slice 98 note "credits get no category"; `InboxUiTest.aCreditBecomesIncomeWithoutACategory...` pinned it), so income reports were mostly empty. New installs already have 9 income categories (5 original + Dividends, Rent received, Reimbursement, Freelance and business: `StarterCategories`); older installs add the missing ones from Suggested categories (a unit test checks every category the words can point at is a starter income category).
- **Behaviour.** `CategorySuggest.suggestIncome` / `CaptureCategory.adviseIncome`: own income rule (sure) > what the person chose for this payer (key `in:`+`PayeeKey`, stored in the same `merchant_map`, so an income choice never touches an expense category of the same name and vice versa; sure after 1 choice for a company, after 2 for a person-like payer or UPI address) > their earlier income from the payer (2 agreeing, none against = sure) > clear words (`IncomeWords`: interest/Int.Pd/SB INT, salary/payroll/SAL, dividend, cashback/refund, reimbursement, rent, gift/shagun; always a check, never added by Add all or a swipe; two categories' words in one text = nothing). Money from a person with no purpose in the words gets no guess and four income chips on the card. Refunds/reversals of an earlier expense are untouched (still a REFUND tied to that expense, no income, no double count); a payment into a card is still a transfer with no category. Editor of an income with no category shows "Suggested: X (why)". Learning from the editor now covers income from a text. A capture rule now has "Money out" / "Money in" so rules only fire in their own direction.
- **Test decision.** `InboxUiTest.aCreditBecomesIncomeWithoutACategory...` asserted the old default; renamed and rewritten: a credit from a person with no purpose in its words still gets no guess, no "check", and adds as uncategorised income, plus income chips are present. Other assertions (account remembered, amount, kind) untouched.
- **Also fixed:** `GenericAlertParser` did not read "INR.1,000.00" (a dot after INR), so IDFC's interest text was not a transaction at all (`amountToken`: `inr\.?`; test added).
- **Precision (invented corpus `invented_credits.tsv`, 66 credits: UPI from persons, NEFT/IMPS/RTGS from persons and companies, salary, interest, dividend, refund, cashback, rent, gift, 8 name/word traps):** 66/66 read as credits; 32 have a right category and all 32 get it (as a check); 34 have none and get none; **0 wrong, 0 sure with nothing learned** (so no wrong "sure"). Simulation: person payer chosen once = check, twice = sure; company once = sure; income choice invisible to expense and the reverse.
- **Files.** `IncomeWords.kt`, `CategorySuggest.kt`, `PayeeKey.kt`, `SuggestionConfidence.kt`, `CaptureCategory.kt`, `PayeeKind.kt` (`paysLikeAPerson`), `CaptureRuleStore.learn(income)`, `LedgerViewModel.inboxRows/confirmCapture`, `CaptureScreens.kt`, `LearnedPayees.kt` ("Money in", income file-earlier via `uncategorisedIncomes`), `EntryActions`, `EntryForm`.

### B. Undo for removing a rule and forgetting/changing a learned choice
`removeCaptureRule`, `forgetLearned`, `changeLearned` offer the standard Undo bar on the Category rules screen ("Removed the rule "swiggy"", "Forgot X", "Changed X to Y"); Undo restores exactly (rule place in the order; learned category, count in a row, label) via `snapshotRule/restoreRule/snapshotLearned/restoreLearned`; a restore that cannot be made (category deleted, same-words rule made since) says why and changes nothing. Filing earlier expenses keeps its Undo (same bar). `UNDO_AUDIT.md` updated.

### C. "N expenses have no category" card and review
Home card (3 or more; "500+" at the cap; Review / Not now = 7 days, setting `categorise_nudge_snoozed_at`; counts only, no amounts). Count = one capped indexed query (`uncategorisedExpenseCount`, partial index `spend_line_uncategorised`, also created by `SchemaUpgrade`) computed with Home's slow figures after the last write, never on the save path or Home open; snoozed = no query. 20,000-entry ledger, 4,000 uncategorised (host): capped count 0.25 ms, uncapped 2.4-27 ms (varies with load), list of 300 0.7 ms; `LearnedChoicesScaleTest` unchanged. Review screen (`CategoriseScreen`): one at a time, "N left", suggestion tap (guess marked), 8 chips + More, Skip, done state "All done. Filed N expenses. Left M as they were.", payees with most waiting first (`ReviewSession.order`), choice saved and taught, Undo bar on the last choice. Shared, foreign-currency, refunded and deleted expenses are not counted/listed. Tags: `categorise_nudge`, `categorise_nudge_text/review/later`, `categorise_screen`, `categorise_item_<id>`, `categorise_left`, `categorise_suggest_<cat>`, `categorise_chip_<cat>`, `categorise_more`, `categorise_skip`, `categorise_done(_text)`, `categorise_finish`, `categorise_back`.

### Tests and runs (final, on the merged commit; see "Verification round" below for the device numbers)
New host tests: `IncomeWordsTest` 9, `CaptureCategoryTest` +3, `GenericAlertParserTest` +1, `CreditCategoryReportTest` 4, `CaptureRuleStoreTest` +2, `UncategorisedReviewTest` 4, `UncategorisedCountTest` 2. New device tests: `IncomeCaptureUiTest` 6, `RulesUndoUiTest` 3, `CategoriseUiTest` 5, `RefreshBudgetUncategorisedTest` 1.

### Verification round (after the first push; the emulator had stalled, so nothing below is from that run)
- **Why the first device batches stalled.** The one test of `CategoriseUiTest` that seeded entries called a helper that opens its own write from INSIDE a write (`db { ... bare(...) }`): the write lock is not re-entrant, so the test thread waited for itself for ever, the Gradle run never finished and the emulator looked "stalled" (CPU idle, load 0.3). It also explains the "4 of 99 tests in 55 minutes": the batch sat on that test. Fixed in the test (seed outside the write). The product was not at fault. The two earlier timeouts of the review's Undo step were the same stall plus a loaded emulator; on a cold-booted emulator the Undo step passes (it is back in the test: tap Undo after the last choice, the expense is current again, has no category, what was learned goes back, and it can be filed again).
- **Emulator.** Cold-booted (`-no-snapshot`, animation scales 1.0, `googlequicksearchbox` disabled), one lock at a time, alternating with the other worker.
- **Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`, final merged commit after `git pull --rebase`; the other worker's Slice 99 was already in):** domain 649, parsers 184, storage 445 = 1,278, 0 failures. **Native release guard (forced):** domain 642, parsers 171, 0 failures. `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles. **`tools/ios_build.sh unit` 39/39** (the first run had 1 failure, `QuickAddTests`: the iPhone widget lists only 6 quick entries and the simulator keeps the test app's data, so after 6 earlier runs the entry the test saved was not in the list. A test-hook fix removes the leftovers first; not related to this slice).
- **Device (API 34, cold-booted), final code:** `CategoriseUiTest` 5/5 in each of 3 consecutive runs (Undo of the last choice included). To-review and capture batch (`InboxUiTest`, `InboxSwipeUiTest`, `LearnedChoicesUiTest`, `CaptureRulesUiTest`, `AccountAskUiTest`, `CategoriseUiTest`) 42/42; (`IncomeCaptureUiTest`, `RulesUndoUiTest`, `DuplicatesUiTest`, `OwnTransferUiTest`, `CaptureTest`, `SkippedSendersUiTest`) 44/44; an earlier regression batch on the same code (`CaptureRulesUiTest`, `LearnedChoicesUiTest`, `InboxUiTest`, `InboxSwipeUiTest`, `CategoriesTest`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`) 38/38, (`ReportsUiTest`, `ReportAndAliasUiTest`, `AccessibilityUiTest`, `HomeListTest`, `IncomeCategoriesUiTest`, `SimilarUiTest`) 38/38, (`IncomeCaptureUiTest`, `RulesUndoUiTest`, `SkipUndoUiTest`, `ChangeTypeUiTest`, `BulkEditTest`, `ForegroundRefreshTest`) 44/44. The last batch (`CategoriesTest`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`, `HomeListTest`, `ReportsUiTest`, `AccessibilityUiTest`, `RefreshBudgetUncategorisedTest`) 31/32: **`CategoriesTest.aHiddenCategoryIsNotOfferedWhenAddingButAnEditedEntryKeepsIt` is flaky and not caused by this slice**: alone on this commit it failed 5 of 6 runs, and on commit 68bc6c3 (before this work, built in a separate worktree, run alternately on the same emulator) it failed 4 of 6, always at the same line (the editor's `category_chip_Fun` does not appear in 10 s); the same class passed 8/8 in two earlier batches. Not investigated further.
- **Home speed, A/B against 68bc6c3 (same emulator, alternating runs, 100,000 entries and 508 categories, `RefreshBudgetTest` = all categorised, 3 runs each; `RefreshBudgetUncategorisedTest` = 20,000 expenses with no category and the card on screen with its count capped at 500, 6 runs each):** save to new row and total, p50 / median p95: plain 3.57 ms / 5.7 ms (this work) vs 3.50 ms / 7.4 ms (68bc6c3); uncategorised 3.47 ms / 6.6 ms vs 3.37 ms / 6.6 ms. Rebuild after the row (p50 / median p95): plain 18.1 / 24.2 ms vs 17.8 / 25.2 ms; uncategorised 16.6 / 23.5 ms vs 16.0 / 24.3 ms. The differences are inside the run-to-run spread (p95 of one run ranged 5.3 to 8.5 ms on the old commit alone). One of the 6 runs of the new commit had a p95 of 12.5 ms, at the 12.5 ms limit of the gate (the old commit's worst was 8.5 ms), so the new test inherits the gate's small noise risk; its p50 was 3.2 ms in that run. A later run of the same test on the final code: p50 3.5 ms, p95 7.1 ms, rebuild p95 22.4 ms. The save path has no new work (the count is read only by the slow-figures pass, 0.25 ms capped on the host), which is what these numbers show.

### Release 0.10.5 (Android code 38, iPhone build 35): integration, after Slice 99 and Slice 100
Ships the two slices above on top of 0.10.4 (code 37, which the owner may have uploaded: code 38 is the next unused one; Play's last known build is 0.10.3 / 36). Versions: `androidApp/build.gradle.kts` 38 / "0.10.5"; `iosApp/project.yml` MARKETING_VERSION 0.10.5, CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION 35 (xcodeproj regenerated). `git status` was clean at the start (HEAD 8649782, the same as origin/main).

**Critical review of the combined diff, and what was fixed.** All figures and texts are invented.
- **(a) `AccountMatch` could put a text on an account of another bank. FIXED.** Step (1) of `AccountMatch.resolve` ("the same digits and nothing else fits") took a three-digit or "ends the same" match on an account whose NAME names no bank ("Salary", "Joint") without asking, so a Federal Bank text ending 123 went to a plain account that happened to carry 123. Now a match is taken without asking only when the digits are four equal digits (as before), or the sender is no known bank (a payment app compares nothing: `BankNames.isBank`), or the account's name says the sender's bank. Anything else is a `Result.Several`, so the card asks "Which account is this?", and an earlier answer for that bank and those digits settles it. For one fitting but unconfirmed account the question reads "<name> carries <digits>, but nothing says it is <bank>'s, and other banks' accounts can end the same. Is it this one?" (`AccountAsk.of`, `allowEndsWith`). This replaces step (1) and (3) of the Matching bullet of Slice 99 above, and closes its "Not verified" item about an account with no bank in its name. Test: `AccountMatchTest.anAccountWhoseNameNamesNoBankIsAskedAboutOnThreeDigitsAndMatchedOnFourEqualDigitsAsBefore`; the 2,000-ledger run (wrong account 0, other bank 0) and the pairing run (false pairs 0) still hold.
- **(b) Three-digit transfer pairing. FIXED (a hole, not a measured error).** `TransferScan` paired two accounts on digits that merely END the same (123 and 4123). It now uses `allowEndsWith = false`: a pair forms on equal digits only; the named-account reader of `LedgerViewModel` ignores `Basis.ENDS_WITH` too. The 1,500-transfer run (400 with clashing three digits at another bank): false or wrong pairs 0.
- **(c) Undo of "New account ending 123" could orphan entries. FIXED.** `undoAccountChoice` deleted the account the answer had created even after entries had been added to it (the first waiting card is added at once, so it normally has one). Now an account that has entries stays (with the remembered bank and digits) and the bar says "That account already has entries, so it stays. You can archive it from More, Accounts."; one with none is removed and the remembered choice restored.
- **(d) Skipped senders. CHECKED, nothing to fix.** The counters hold only the sender header's root, a count, the last time and the suffix letters (setting `skipped_sender:<ROOT>`), never a text, number or digits; they are not on the `BackupStore.SETTINGS` whitelist so no backup carries them; they count only non-email SMS from a sender that is not on the list. `git diff e067d5b..HEAD` of `docs/PLAY_SMS_DECLARATION.md`, `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`, `docs/site/privacy.html` and every `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` is empty (the 0.10.4 text still says what the app does).
- **(e) Income categories. CHECKED.** Only a rule, a learned choice or the person's own history can give a "sure" income category; the words list (`IncomeWords`) is always "check"; income is learned under the key prefix `in:`, apart from expense keys (an expense category is never learned from a credit and the reverse); a refund or reversal goes through the existing refund rules to the original expense and is not an income, so nothing is counted twice.
- **(f) The "no category" count. CHECKED.** One indexed query, capped at 500, run with Home's slow figures after a write, never on the save path or when Home opens; a snooze is checked before the query; hidden under 3. `RefreshBudgetUncategorisedTest` (20,000 uncategorised entries, card on screen) is in the timing runs below.
- **(g) Backup and migration. CHECKED with tests added.** The only schema change is a partial index (`spend_line_uncategorised`), created `IF NOT EXISTS` by `SchemaUpgrade`: no column, no data migration. New `BackupStoreTest`: `aBackupOfTheNineZeroEraAndOneOfPointTenFourBothRestoreAndKeepWhatTheyCarry` (a 0.9.0-shaped file, no learned or captured tables, and a 0.10.4 one with learned payees restore and keep their contents) and `aBackupOfThisReleaseWithThreeDigitAccountsAndIncomeChoicesRestoresAndAnAccountWithJunkDigitsStillComesBackWithoutThem`. 21/21 in that class.

**Flaky and unknown tests: root causes (no "known flaky" list is carried forward).**
| Test | Root cause | Fix | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CategoriesTest.aHiddenCategoryIsNotOffered...` | After Cancel on the Add form the test tapped the Movie row while the form was still sliding away over Home (Android 14 showed Home without the editor in the failure screenshot, the tap having landed on the leaving form). A test race. | The test waits for the form to be gone (`new_category_chip`) and the screen to be idle | failed 2 of 2 on API 34 before; 3 of 3 and every later run after (8/8) |
| `BackupFilesTest` restore double-tap | The test raced the phone's speed: the restore could finish before the "second tap" assertion. | The test holds the database write lock, so the restore is certainly in flight, then asserts `restore_confirm` is disabled | class 19/19 twice alone on API 26 and in two full API 34 passes |
| `BackupFilesTest` share-sheet OOM | PRODUCT: `AttachmentFiles.copyStream` hashed a copied file by reading it back whole (`readBytes`), so a 14 MB attachment needed a second 14 MB array on the ~44 MB heap of the Android 8 emulator (the test built two such files as well) | `copyStream` hashes with the streaming `Sha256.Digest` while it copies; the test writes its 14 MB files in blocks | same runs as above; the full API 26 suite |
| `LoansUiTest.borrowing...` timeout | Tapping "+ New" for a person in the freshly opened Add form while the keyboard rises: it shrinks the form and moves the chip, so a tap in that moment hits nothing and the name dialog never opens. Reproduced once in 8 runs with the emulator's CPUs kept busy (screenshot: the form with the numeric keyboard up, no dialog; tags on screen without `new_person_name`) and by the first run after a cold boot twice | the test's `newPerson()` taps again, scrolled to the chip where it now is, if the dialog has not appeared in 4 s | 12 of 12 runs under the same load pass; 3 of 3 plain |
| `QuickAddWidgetTest.aWidgetPlacedFromEachPickerEntry...` (Android 14, combined runs) | The test asked Glance for the placed widget ids right after the launcher placed them, before the widget's receivers had recorded them | polls up to 30 s until `getGlanceIds` holds all placed ids | in two combined API 34 passes |
| `NotificationCaptureTest` / `EmailCaptureUiTest` after `QuickAddWidgetTest` (Android 14) | Both write `enabled_notification_listeners` and post at once; the system binds the listener a moment later, and after the widget test (launcher, Glance) later still, so a posted notification was missed. The same failure was present on the 0.10.3 commit. The cause is established by the cure, not by a captured failure of the old order | `NotificationCaptureService.connected` (set in `onListenerConnected`/`onListenerDisconnected`) is a plain flag: the tests wait for it before posting and for the disconnect in tear-down | two consecutive API 34 passes of 31 classes, in which these run after the widget classes: 237 tests, 0 failures each |
| `MotionUiTest` | An emulator setting: the re-created AVD came up with the three animation scales at 0 | `tools/emulator_setup.sh` (scales 1, Google app disabled, screen awake); documented in `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md` | passes with scales 1 |
| `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`, `AccountPeriodUiTest` | Not reproduced: 3/3 and 2/2 alone, no failure in the full API 26 suite or in two API 34 passes. They failed earlier only in long runs on an emulator that had been up for hours | none needed; see the next row | |
| Six classes at the end of the alphabet in the long API 26 run (`SearchBulkCategoryUiTest`, `SearchDatesUiTest`, `SearchUiTest`, `TagsUiTest`, `TransferCandidatesUiTest`, `WidePaneUiTest`: 20 failures, all "Condition still not satisfied after 10000 ms") | The Android 8 emulator itself: after about an hour its own "System UI isn't responding" dialog was on screen (screenshot taken in a failing run) and everything behind it timed out. Nothing to do with the app | the checklist now says to cold-boot before a long run and halfway through it; a failing class is judged alone on a cold-booted emulator | the six classes, run together on a cold boot: 46/46 twice |
| `release_smoke` step "add an expense", once, API 34 under a host load of 32 | The welcome card was found, then the tour covered it a moment later | the first-launch step now counts the card only after 4 s alone on screen | 14/14 in 2 runs after the change (load 32 and 13), 14/14 twice before it |

**Runs, on the final code** (the product code of the artifacts is that of commit 82dbc66; the commits after it touch only docs, `tools/release_smoke.py` and test files).

## Slice 101: UX pass on Search, the bulk bar, To review, Capture, Home nudges, confirmations and empty states (after 0.10.5; no version bump)

Evidence first: each item started from a screenshot on the API 34 emulator (invented data), and each result was checked by reading screenshots at 393 dp and 360 dp, light and dark, and one set at font scale 2.0 (reset afterwards). All figures and names are invented. A scratch test (`ScratchShotsTest`, not committed) seeded the data and took the screenshots; the paths below are in `/tmp/ux/` (not kept in the repo).

### 1. Search opened from an account
- **Before** (`before_search_open.png`): the keyboard covered the lower half; the account chips (Any account / Bank / Cash) ran past the right edge with nothing to say so; the tab bar floated above the keyboard.
- **Now** (`a393l_search_open.png`, `l360_search_open.png`, `d360_search_open.png`): opened from an account it does not take the keyboard (the box is one tap away), nor when coming back from an entry opened out of the results; the filter chips **wrap** (all account chips show); the day chips row, which still scrolls, **fades at the edge** that has more; everything under the box is one list, and while typing with the keyboard up the page moves up so the results sit under the box (`a393l_search_typed.png`), a drag puts the keyboard away (existing nested-scroll hook, kept) and brings the filters back; choosing a chip, day or saved search puts the keyboard away; `rememberKeyboardOpen` now also reads the keyboard's inset (the old frame test never fired on API 34, which is why the tab bar floated) and reads it at once, not only on the next layout.
- **Tests changed:** `SearchUiTest.theDateChipsKeepOnlyWhatIsInRange` scrolls to the chip (typing moves the page up). Tags unchanged.

### 2. Selection bar (`Bulk.kt`)
Set category, Make transfer (Account where Make transfer is not offered), Delete and **More** (Account…, Tag…); the bar wraps instead of scrolling (`l360_bulk_home.png` first run: More wrapped; after tightening button padding all four fit one line at 360 dp, `d360_bulk_home.png`; at font 2.0 it wraps to three lines with Delete in view, `f2_360_bulk_large.png`). The add button on Home is hidden while entries are chosen. **Tests changed:** `TagsUiTest` taps `bulk_more` first; `BulkEditTest` asserts the bar's four buttons are wholly on screen (it asserted Delete unscrolled before; still does).

### 3. To review cards
"3 of 12" at the top of every card (a transfer card says "5 and 6 of 14"), tag `inbox_progress_<id>`; the account question is the first thing on a card that has one and keeps only the title and the choices; **why it is asked** and **what is remembered** are under one `inbox_more_options_<id>` toggle, as are the other categories of a payee when the card is not a guess (a guess still shows them at once, with the "This is a guess: please check it" line, which is never folded). Add and Skip stay directly under the decision block on every card. The longer "Read automatically: please check it before adding" line is still under Show message, as before. **Tests changed:** `AccountAskUiTest` opens More options before reading the explanation, and scrolls to the second card (lazy list).

### 4. Capture screen
Sections **Set up** (the three sources, each a card with its switch, one line, notes, and the long explanation under "Details"; the bank sender names in a group that folds, open at first), **Rules** and **Health**, each with a heading and one line (`section_*`, `capture_group_senders`, `capture_details_*`); the iPhone Shortcuts page got the same headings; the rules screen got "Your rules" and "New rule" headings, an empty line, and "What the app has learned" folds when it has more than 8 entries. Every old tag kept. **Tests changed:** `EmailCaptureUiTest` opens Details before reading the note.

### 5. One Home nudge at a time
Order, rules and the More page: `docs/HOME_NUDGES.md`. `HomeNudges` (core/domain) is pure; `HomeNudgesTest` 9 tests (order, one card + the rest, due bill over inbox, none present, put-off for a day and the return, more arriving brings it back at once, clock gone backwards, encoding, one More notice). Home draws the first present nudge not put off, an "Also waiting: … See More" line, and a close button on the bills, To review and money-to-get-back cards. More shows the same nudges as cards (two fit) and one notice (signing before backup).

### 6. Confirmation audit (a read-only audit of the screens, then fixes)
Found: the sentence of `state.message` was only shown on Home, so every action on another screen that sets one said nothing until Home (or never); Your data's result sat above the fold of a long page; Undo of Post/Skip on a repeating item was Home-only; archive, delete account, remove billing dates (which also silently removed the credit limit), category merge (one tap, no summary), Subscriptions "Not one"/"Remind me" (silent exits), "Notifications are blocked" (lost), "This is my bank" in Health, a refused text permission, the auto-export folder (silently turns weekly on), Loans remove.
Fixed: a one-line bar for every screen except Home (`MessageHost.kt`) carrying `say(...)` lines (archive, delete, billing dates, merge, folder, remove person, sender added, subscription hidden/added, test notification sent or blocked, text permission refused), plus the Undo of Post/Skip on Bills and Repeating; Your data scrolls to its result (live region); category merge asks "Merge into X?" once more. **Not done:** Undo for archive/delete/billing dates/Not one (said, not undoable); a confirm for deleting an unused account; `saveAccount` clearing billing dates when a card changes type is still unsaid. **Tests changed:** `CategoriesTest` confirms the merge.

### 7. Empty states and wording
Bills, To review (both branches), Search with no results ("Clear the search"), Home inside an account (Add an expense / Show all time), Subscriptions, Transfer candidates, Reports, learned choices, rules: plain words, one first action where a screen was missing one (Bills and To review lead to Capture). Errors: the generic catches no longer show `e.message` or a class name (attach, import, backup, data actions, transfers, save, key lost, OCR, auto copy); store and ledger messages that showed an id or minor units are plain. **Not done:** `BackupStore` restore messages with ids, the iPhone-only debug strings, Search's two internal `require` messages.

### Tests and runs
- **New:** `HomeNudgesTest` 9 (host), `UxPolishUiTest` 10 (device; the tenth proves the "Rule added" bar covers nothing and does not catch the next tap).
- **Follow-up round on the final commit** (clean `git worktree` of the commit, so the other worker's unfinished widget files were not in it): host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`) domain 658, parsers 184, storage 447, 0 failures; **native release guard (forced, optimised Kotlin/Native)** domain 651, parsers 171, 0 failures; `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles; **`tools/ios_build.sh unit` 39 of 39** (under `/tmp/ios.lock`); **device, API 26 emulator, cold-booted twice (after the first 4 batches, and again before the last 6), `tools/emulator_setup.sh`, batches of 6 classes: 36 classes, 284 tests, 0 failures** in the last pass: `UxPolishUiTest`, `BulkEditTest`, `TagsUiTest`, `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest`, `SearchUiTest`, `SearchStartUiTest` (43); `SearchDatesUiTest`, `SavedSearchUiTest`, `AccountPeriodUiTest`, `InboxUiTest`, `InboxSwipeUiTest`, `AccountAskUiTest` (38); `SkippedSendersUiTest`, `IncomeCaptureUiTest`, `DuplicatesUiTest`, `OwnTransferUiTest`, `CategoriseUiTest`, `CategoriesTest` (44); `CategoryKindsUiTest`, `AccountsUiTest`, `AlertsUiTest`, `SubscriptionsUiTest`, `BillsUiTest`, `FirstWeekUiTest` (52); `MoreMenuUiTest`, `HomeListTest`, `ListHeaderTest`, `ChangeTypeUiTest`, `LoansUiTest`, `GoalsUiTest` (31); `BackupFilesTest`, `DataTest`, `AccessibilityUiTest`, `NotificationsTest`, `TransferCandidatesUiTest`, `HomeLayoutUiTest` (76). The Capture group (`CaptureTest`, `EmailCaptureUiTest`, `CaptureRulesUiTest`, `LearnedChoicesUiTest`, `RulesUndoUiTest`, `TrustedSendersUiTest`, 37 tests) passed in its own batch on the commit before the last two (search) changes, which touch no capture code; `CaptureRulesUiTest` also passed 4/4 on API 34 with the final bar.
- **Failures found on API 26 and their causes** (each re-run alone twice before judging):

Fix: the notice scrolls itself into view (`LaunchedEffect(state.notice)`); 6/6 after.
  2. `SavedSearchUiTest` 1 of 5, all runs, **tests, caused by the same design**: the filters and the new saved chip are above the results once the page has moved up. Found with a log of each step: the chips were pressed fine, the saved chip was not in the lazy list. The test now scrolls to a filter that is not there (waiting a moment for a menu item first, because scrolling the list away from its chip closes the menu: my first helper did that and failed differently) and presses again if the page moved in that instant; 5/5 twice.
  3. `CaptureRulesUiTest.theFirstRuleWinsAndMovingARuleUpChangesTheSuggestion` with the "Rule added" line (API 34, 2 of 2): **the bar, a product bug**. The Material snackbar I had drawn over the screen swallowed every touch on its area; the tap on the next rule's category chip landed on it, so the second rule kept the first rule's category (read from the log: both rules `Transport`, the card's "Goes to" stayed Transport; screenshot confirmed the card). The bar is now a row under the screen (the screen gets the room left; it covers nothing and takes no touch; Undo is its only button), and the line is back. Screenshots read: `c360f2_bill_bar.png` (old bar covering "Mark paid" and "Remove" at font 2.0) against `e360f2_bill_bar.png` (new bar under the screen, buttons clear), `g360_rule_bar.png`, `g360f2_rule_bar.png`. Also fixed in that screenshot: "Add rule" was squeezed to a sliver at font 2.0 (a Row); it wraps now.
  4. `CategoriesTest` failed twice in the first API 26 pass (two different tests, one in the batch, one on the first rerun) and passed 3 of 3 alone and in the later cold-booted batch. **Cause not established**: no screenshot of a failure was captured, the failing tests differ, and the class changed only by the merge step; the emulator had been up about two hours and had just run failing, long-waiting classes. Not left as "known flaky": it is listed under not verified.
- **Confirmations of failed actions / exception text (grep):** every `say(...)` is after the call that can refuse (a refusal is a `require` in the store, whose own words are shown by `plainMessage`, and no `say` runs); the failure texts of `say` are said as failures ("Android did not allow it", "Notifications are blocked", "Could not set up"). No `e.message` reaches a screen unfiltered from the generic catches (`plainMessage` replaces an empty or library message with "That did not work. Nothing was changed. Try again."); no `simpleName`, `localizedDescription` or class name is left in a user-bound string in common, Android or iPhone code (the iPhone OCR, keychain, file, xlsx and share messages were the last ones; debug-only strings in `IosBackupDebug`/`IosAutoExport` are test hooks).

## Slice 102: widgets: 48 dp touch targets, a way to reveal on the 2 x 1, and the in-app accent (after 0.10.5; no version bump)

Widget code, widget resources, widget tests and docs only. All figures and names in tests and screenshots are invented.

### 1. Every control is at least 48 dp, at every size
- **Before.** The eye and "+ Add" were 44 dp on a 2 x 2 (40 dp at its narrowest and on the Today widget), a quick-entry chip about 35 dp high, the Undo word about 31 dp, and the 2 x 1 and 2 x 2 had a 44 or 40 dp row squeezed under 8 dp of card padding.
- **Now.** The eye, "+ Add" (round or pill), a chip and the Undo word are 48 dp in both directions; the visible shapes stay small (eye icon 20 dp, round Add 40 dp, the card looks as before) because the target carries the margin. How it fits: the card's own inset shrank (top and bottom 4 dp, 2 dp on a 2 x 2, no side padding where two 48 dp targets must sit side by side on 110 dp: the controls row has the full width, texts keep their own 12 to 14 dp margin); a one-letter chip gets side padding so it is not narrower than 48 dp (`chipSidePad`); `WidgetSummary.Room` has `TARGET = 48`, `UNDO = 54`, `CHIPS = 54`, `PAD = 4` (the header was already 48), so a 4 x 2 at 110 dp still keeps its three chips and one Undo line fits there; all five widget providers declare `minHeight`/`minResizeHeight` 56 dp instead of 40 (a 48 dp row plus the inset; launchers count cells as (size + 30) / 70, so one cell is still one cell). The widget narrower than 150 dp on a 2 x 2 puts the eye beside Add (two 48 dp targets are 96 of 110 dp).
- **Everything else opens the app.** The captions, the figure, the bars, the empty space: the whole card is one tap target (the root), and the controls sit on top of it. Before, only some texts were separate small targets. The reason is explicit: a 48 dp box around a caption cannot fit in a 56 dp widget, and the card as a whole is the target a person aims at anyway.
- **Measured on a device** (`WidgetTargetsTest`): the widget is composed to RemoteViews exactly as a launcher gets it, inflated and laid out at an exact size, and every view that answers a tap is measured. 6 models (masked, asking "Show?", shown with Undo and every list, shown without Undo, forced masked by the App lock, data unreadable) x 10 widths (110 to 420 dp) x 9 heights (56 to 294 dp): every control is at least 48 dp in both directions (a pixel of rounding allowed), inside the widget, and apart from every other control; the card itself is always a target. It found the controls it should (a floor of 1,000 measured controls) and named them per size.

### 2. The 2 x 1 widget can reveal the amount
- **Why not an eye.** A 48 dp eye, a 48 dp Add and a figure of about 40 dp need 176 dp; the real launcher minimum of a 2 x 1 is 110 dp, and a widget of 56 dp high has no second row. So the FIGURE is the control: its target is the whole text column (caption and figure) and its left margin, about 54 x 48 dp at 110 dp wide.
- **Accidental reveal.** The figure is the part of a widget people tap to open the app, so one tap must not show spending on a home screen in public. A tap on the masked figure only ASKS: the caption turns to "Confirm", the figure to "Show?" and the content description to "Tap again to show the amount" for five seconds (`ArmRevealAction`; the question is kept in that copy's own widget state, nothing in the ledger changes; the widget redraws by itself when the time runs out). A second tap inside that time shows the amount (the same `ToggleTotalAction` as the eye, so every widget follows); a late second tap asks again. A tap on a SHOWN figure hides it at once (hiding needs no confirmation), with the description "Hide the amount"; the masked one says "Show the amount", as the eye does everywhere else. When the app forces the figure masked (Hide amounts, App lock) there is nothing to reveal, no description, and the card opens the app.
- **Cost, said plainly.** On the 2 x 1 a tap on the caption or the figure no longer opens the app while the amount may be revealed: the card's edges and the Add button (which opens the Add screen) do. A 2 x 2 or wider keeps the eye and a figure that opens the app. Screen-reader users make the two taps as two double-taps; the description of the second says what it is.
- **Tests.** `WidgetDesignTest` (the figure carries "Show the amount", "Tap again to show the amount" and "Hide the amount"; none when forced masked; the question is open for its window only, never on a shown total), `QuickAddWidgetTest` (first tap asks and changes nothing in the ledger, second shows and forgets the question), `WidgetEyeTapTest` on a really placed 110 x 60 widget: tap, "Show?", tap, the amount, tap, masked again.

### 3. The accent chosen in the app
- **Checked first.** The app has no dynamic-colour setting (its theme is `colorSchemeFor(look, dark)` from the Material baseline plus the chosen accent), so Material You would only have made the widget disagree with the app: it is removed (the `values-v31` and `values-night-v31` colour files are deleted; the Android 12 corner radius stays). The default is the app's default accent, violet. The app's light/dark choice (More, Appearance) is not followed: the widget follows the phone's mode, as every widget does, which can differ from the app when the person forces the app dark or light.
- **How the choice reaches the widget.** `QuickAddWidget.load` and `TodayWidget.loadToday` read the `appearance` setting (the one `LedgerViewModel.setAppearance` writes) inside the read they already make and carry the accent in `WidgetData`/`TodayData` and `WidgetModel`. `WidgetAccents` maps it to a drawable (`widget_pill_<accent>`, used by the Add button and the bars) and the colour of the text on it. The drawables use `widget_accent_<accent>` colours in `values` and `values-night`, which are `Appearances.primaryFor(accent, dark)`, the function the app's theme uses (dark mode lightens the accent until it reads on the dark surface); the text colour is `Appearances.onColor` of the same. An unknown or missing accent is violet, as in the app.
- **Redraw.** `refreshWidgets` runs when the accent changes while the app is open (`MainActivity` watches the accent of the UI state, so a widget on screen beside the app, or the next glance after leaving, is already right) and, as before, whenever the app leaves the screen and every hour.
- **Colours, all six accents, light and dark** (`WidgetDesignTest.everyAccentColourIsTheOneTheAppsThemeMakes...`): the resource equals `Appearances.primaryFor`, text on the accent is at least 4.5:1 (lowest: orange in light mode, 4.85:1), the button against the card at least 3:1, the neutral text pairs at 4.5:1 as before (and now they are the colours drawn on every Android version, so the check is not about Material You any more).
- **Pixels** (`WidgetTargetsTest`): the widget composed for 6 accents x light and dark x 110 x 60, 110 x 110, 320 x 146 and 320 x 294 and drawn to a bitmap: the exact accent colour is on the Add button and the bars, and no OTHER accent's colour is anywhere. `WidgetEyeTapTest.anAccentChosen...`: on a really placed widget the accent is changed in the running app (teal, orange, violet) and the placed widget draws each within 25 seconds without the app leaving the screen.
- **Looked at** (36 renders written by the test, read as images): violet, blue, orange in light and dark at 2 x 1, 4 x 2 and 4 x 4; the button, the bars, the text on them and the chips all agree; the dark accents are the app's lightened ones (violet dark is #8977B7, not the old widget's #D0BCFF).
- **Not changed:** the picker's preview images (`widget_preview_*.png`) stay violet: they are static pictures; Android 15's generated previews are not used.

### Runs and what was found on the way
- **Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`, on the tree rebased onto Slice 101):** domain 659 (`WidgetSummaryTest` 12, one new), parsers 184, storage 447, 0 failures. **Native release guard (forced):** domain 652, parsers 171, 0 failures. `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles (shared code is untouched; `core/domain` changed).
- **Device, final code, the five widget classes** (`WidgetDesignTest`, `WidgetEyeTapTest`, `QuickAddWidgetTest`, `ForegroundRefreshTest`, `WidgetTargetsTest`; cold-booted emulators, animation scales 1): **API 34: 70 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped; API 26: 70 tests, 0 failures, 3 skipped** (`QuickAddWidgetTest` 2 need Android 12; `WidgetEyeTapTest.theWidgetIsDrawnAtTheSmall...` skipped once because the device refused to bind a widget from a test, which the test treats as "cannot run here"; it ran in the other API 26 runs).
- **Release build, `tools/release_smoke.sh`:** API 34 14/14, three times in a row; API 26 7 passed, 7 skipped (Android 8 has no launcher picker). `tools/check_release_keeps.py` PASS (9,380 classes). The first API 34 attempt failed 3 of 3 at "eye and chip from a process that was not running": the expiry job's log line, which that step looks for, had been reworded; it is back to "undo line: the time is up" (the arming question uses the same job and line).
- **A test race the 2 x 1 revealed (not a product fault):** a really placed widget is first drawn at the provider's minimum size (now a 2 x 1, whose figure carries "Show the amount"), then at the size the test asks for. `WidgetEyeTapTest.aShownTotalStaysShown...` waited only for "Show the amount", which the 2 x 1 already satisfied, tapped it, and so asked "Show?" instead of showing; it failed 3 of 3 alone on API 26 until it also waited for the 4 x 4's caption ("Spent this month"), and then passed 3 of 3 (12/12 each). The widget's own log and a per-draw trace (removed) showed the 2 x 1 draw, then the 250 x 250 one, then the "asking" callback at the instant of the redraw.
- **Not verified:** a real launcher and finger (the sizes were laid out by the test, not by a launcher, and a phone with fewer than 56 dp for one cell would clip a target); TalkBack on the 2 x 1's two-tap reveal; the iPhone widget (it is a separate SwiftUI widget and was not touched: it still follows its own colours); the picker previews, which stay violet; that a launcher honours `minResizeHeight` 56 dp (the cell arithmetic says one cell is unchanged).

### Release 0.10.6 (Android code 39, iPhone build 36): integration
Ships Slice 101 (the UX pass) and Slice 102 (widgets) on top of 0.10.5 (code 38, built; it may or may not have been uploaded, so 39 is the next code; Play's last known build is 0.10.3 / 36). Versions: `androidApp/build.gradle.kts` 39 / "0.10.6"; `iosApp/project.yml` MARKETING_VERSION 0.10.6, CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION 36. `git status` was clean at the start (head 6daced0).

**Review of the combined diff (82dbc66..6daced0).**
- **(a) The message bar and "no confirmation when it failed".** Read every `say(...)` the Slice added (archive, delete account, merge, billing dates, folder, remove person, rule added, sender added, subscriptions, test notification): each comes after the call that can refuse, and a refusal (`require`, `IllegalArgumentException`) ends the block before `say` and shows `plainMessage` instead; the stores that delete (`deleteAccount`, `PeopleStore.remove`) throw, they do not return silently, so no "Deleted X" for a delete that did not happen. Undo bars: `MessageBarHost` takes the Undo of Bills and Repeating and keeps its own Undo timer; `BackupFilesTest` and the undo classes pass. **Found:** `AttachmentsTest.anUnreadableSourceFailsCleanlyAndLeavesNoFile` still expected the exception's own text ("disk gone") on the screen, which Slice 101 deliberately removed: it failed in the full API 26 run; the test now expects the plain message and that the exception text is NOT shown.
- **(b) Home nudges.** `HomeNudges.visible` takes the nudges present in the enum's order, minus those put off; `isPutOff` is false once more is waiting than when it was put off, and a clock gone backwards counts as inside the day. Persistence is one setting per nudge, `nudge_snoozed:<key>` = `atMillis|count`, read with the other small settings on every rebuild (no query). They are not on `BackupStore.SETTINGS` and not a prefix the restore accepts, so a backup neither carries nor restores them. Not a bug, a property: the card is re-evaluated when Home recomposes, so one put off for a day returns at the next recomposition after the day, not at the exact minute.
- **(c) The bulk bar.** Set category, Make transfer (or Account where transfer is not offered) and Delete are on the bar; Account (when transfer is on the bar) and Tag are under More. Every action is reachable; `bulk_category`, `bulk_transfer`, `bulk_delete`, `bulk_more`, `bulk_account`, `bulk_tag` and the menu items are the same tags (Account and Tag now sit inside More's menu, which the tests open first). Checked by `BulkEditTest`, `TagsUiTest`, `SearchBulkCategoryUiTest`.
- **(d) The widget accent and the 2 x 1.** `Appearances.decode` of one setting is read inside the read the widget already makes (no extra query); `MainActivity` redraws the widgets when the accent of the UI state changes (the first value is the baseline, so opening the app draws nothing extra), and, as before, when the app leaves the screen and every hour; an unknown accent is violet. The 2 x 1: one tap on the masked figure only asks "Show?" (5 s, kept in that copy's own state), the second tap inside that time shows it; with Hide amounts or the App lock on there is no reveal control, and even a stale tap cannot show a total: the figure is masked from `canToggle`, which both settings clear. One property of the eye that the 2 x 1 shares and is not new: `widget_show_total` stays "1" while the lock is on, so when the lock goes off the widget shows the total again without a tap; it is the person's own earlier choice and the 4 x 2's eye has always done it.
- **(e) CategoriesTest, investigated for real.** Not reproducible on a cold API 26 emulator (6 of 6), so the emulator's CPUs were kept busy (4 busy loops). Then it failed about 1 run in 6, always a different test of the class. With screenshots taken AT the failing wait (a JUnit rule hears of a failure only after `@After`, when the activity is gone and the picture is the launcher: the first two screenshots were useless for that reason) the cause is one: **a tap placed while the screen is still moving is lost**. The Add form opens with the amount focused and the keyboard rises a moment later, which shrinks the form and moves "+ New" (or Save, or the Movie row on Home after the form closes); a tap before it settles hits nothing, and the next step waits 10 s for a result that never comes. Seen in the screenshots: the Add form with the keyboard up and the category chips but no dialog; the form with the note typed and Save not pressed; Home with the Movie row and no editor. It is the test's timing, not the product (a person taps again). Fixed in the tests: `openAddForm`, `openNewCategoryDialog`, `saveTheForm` and the Movie row repeat their tap until the result is there. Busy emulator: 0 failures in 60 runs of the worst test and in 40 of the other two after the first fixes (39 of 40 before the Save fix, whose one failure was the Save tap). The same cause then showed in `CaptureRulesUiTest.aRuleIsTestedAgainst...` (6 of 40 busy runs on API 34: "Test" tapped as the keyboard rose) and `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...` (once, in a long batch): both repeat their taps now (60 of 60 busy runs for the first). `FailureShot` (a test helper) takes the screenshot at a failing wait.
- **(f) What Slice 101 left without Undo.** Done: **delete account asks first** ("Delete X?" Delete / Keep it; `account_delete_confirm`, `account_delete_cancel`), because nothing can bring a deleted account back; **archive an account has an Undo bar on the Accounts page** ("Archived X. Its entries stay."; `UndoUi.unarchiveAccountId`; the Home bar offers the same if the person goes back within the time; Restore from the Archived list is still there). Both are in `AccountsUiTest` (the Undo test first waited for itself: it read an account id inside a database write, whose lock is not re-entrant, and hung the API 26 batch for two hours; fixed in the test and noted for the next person). **Not done, listed:** Undo for "remove billing dates" (three numbers, said in the bar and shown in the dialog that removes them) and for "Not one" on Subscriptions (the hidden list has a button that shows them again, and the bar says so); `saveAccount` clearing billing dates when a card changes type is still unsaid. `docs/UNDO_AUDIT.md` updated.

**Runs, on the final code** (product code is that of commit 4ef60af; the later commits change tests and docs only, checked with `git diff --stat 4ef60af HEAD` over `androidApp/src/main`, `shared`, `core`, `iosApp`, `tools`).
- **Host (JVM, `--rerun-tasks`):** domain 659, parsers 184, storage 447 = 1,290, 0 failures. **Native release guard (forced):** domain 652, parsers 171, 0 failures. `:shared:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` compiles. **`tools/ios_build.sh unit` 39 of 39.**
- **Full API 26 suite** (109 classes; the four timing classes excluded; cold-booted with `tools/emulator_setup.sh` before each of three parts, because of the one-hour System UI freeze): **715 tests, 2 failures, 3 skipped** (`AaaNotifPermissionResumeUiTest` 1, `QuickAddWidgetTest` 2: Android 12 and later). Failures: `AttachmentsTest.anUnreadableSource...` (the test, as above) and `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest.theAllCategoriesList...` (a lost tap: passed 2 of 2 alone, and the class now repeats its taps). Final confirmation on the final commit, cold-booted: `CategoriesTest`, `CaptureRulesUiTest`, `SuggestedCategoriesUiTest`, `AttachmentsTest`, `AccountsUiTest`, `UxPolishUiTest`: **42 tests, 0 failures on API 26, and 42 tests, 0 failures on API 34.**
- **API 34** (50 classes: widgets, permission and foreground refresh, notification and e-mail capture, accounts, To review, categorise, search, bulk, tags, learned choices, rules, income, backup, loans, subscriptions, bills, More): 355 tests in one 46-minute batch, 2 failures, both lost taps (`CategoriesTest.aHiddenCategoryIsNotOffered...`: Home with the Movie row and no editor; `CaptureRulesUiTest.aRuleIsTested...`: the keyboard up, "Test" not pressed). I should have rebooted the emulator halfway; both classes passed 3 of 3 alone on a cold boot, and the stress runs above found and fixed the cause. Fixed, the 12 tests of the two classes: 3 of 3, then 60 of 60 busy runs.
- **Timing gate, A/B against 82dbc66** (`PerformanceBudgetTest`, `RefreshBudgetTest`, plus the new `RefreshBudgetUncategorisedTest` on this commit; cold-booted API 26, worktrees alternating old, this, this, old, old, this; every `PERF` line kept). **The host was never quiet:** its load average at the start of the six runs was 10.7, 36.6, 10.8, 8.7, 9.4, 18.1 (the earlier 0.10.5 check had a quiet pair; this one did not), so budget tests failed on BOTH commits in every run (old 4, 9, 7 of 15; this 10, 9, 9 of 16), as they do under load; the limits are the phone budget divided by 4 and were NOT changed. Ratios this/old in the three adjacent pairs, per test: **no test is above 1.3 in all three pairs; none is flagged.** Medians (old / this, under that load): save to new row and total p50 21.7 / 21.3 ms, rebuild p50 79.5 / 85.3 ms, save end to end p95 53 / 45 ms. This does not show an absence of small regressions under a quiet host, only that none is consistent here.
- **Release build and smoke:** `assembleRelease`, `bundlePlaySms`, `packagePlaySmsUniversalApk` built; `tools/check_release_keeps.py` PASS on the APK and on the Play bundle's universal APK with the R8 mapping (9,385 classes); APK badging 0.10.6 / 39 (`app.expenses`; the Play build is `io.github.chandu007.expenses`, 0.10.6 / 39); the bundle's manifest has `RECEIVE_SMS` (1) and no `INTERNET` (0). `tools/release_smoke.sh`: **API 34 14 of 14, three times in a row; API 26 7 passed, 7 widget steps skipped (Android 8).**
